Proposition~\ref{prop:fejer-su2-inverse} cannot be obtained by replacing the
physical multiplier $M_q$ with its compression
$P_{\mathcal E}M_qP_{\mathcal E}$ and silently treating $\mathcal E$ as an
invariant physical block.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Since $A_3$ is self-adjoint and $\mathcal E$ is finite dimensional and
invariant, $\mathcal E$ is spanned by finitely many spherical harmonics.
Consequently every element of $\mathcal E$ is real analytic.  The restriction
$M_q|_{\mathcal E}$ is self-adjoint, so it has a nonzero eigenvector
$f\in\mathcal E$:
\[
 qf=\lambda f.
\]
The zero set of the nonzero real-analytic function $f$ has empty interior.
Hence $q=\lambda$ on the dense open set where $f\neq0$, and continuity gives
$q\equiv\lambda$ on $\mathbb S^2$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Scalar conjugate averages cannot implement the Fej\'er repair]
\label{prop:scalar-conjugate-average-no-go}
Let $S=[s_0,s_1]\Subset(0,\infty)$ be nondegenerate and let
$\mathcal E\subset C^\infty(\mathbb S^2)$ be finite dimensional and invariant
under $A_3=-\Delta_{\mathbb S^2}$.  Write $P=P_{\mathcal E}$,
$d=\dim\mathcal E$, and
\[
 A_{\mathcal E}=A_3|_{\mathcal E},
 \qquad
 A_{\mathcal E}^{\circ}
 =A_{\mathcal E}-\frac{\operatorname{tr}A_{\mathcal E}}d\Id.
\]
Assume that $A_{\mathcal E}^{\circ}\neq0$.  For arbitrary finite families
$w_j\geq0$, $c_j\in\mathbb R$, and
$q_j\in C^\infty(\mathbb S^2;\mathbb R)$, put
\begin{equation}
 K_s=
 P\sum_jw_j
 e^{isc_jM_{q_j}}A_3e^{-isc_jM_{q_j}}
 P\bigg|_{\mathcal E}.
 \label{eq:scalar-adjoint-average}
\end{equation}
Define
\begin{equation}
 \eta_S=
 \inf_{a,b\in\mathbb C}
 \sup_{s\in S}|1+as+bs^2|.
 \label{eq:quadratic-constant-gap}
\end{equation}
Then $\eta_S>0$ and
\begin{equation}
 \inf_{\lambda\in C(S;\mathbb R)}
 \sup_{s\in S}
 \|K_s-(-A_{\mathcal E}+\lambda(s)\Id)\|
 \geq
 \frac{\eta_S}{2}
 \|A_{\mathcal E}^{\circ}\|>0.
 \label{eq:scalar-fejer-average-gap}
\end{equation}
In particular,
\begin{equation}
 \eta_S\geq
 \frac{(s_1-s_0)^2}{s_0^2+6s_0s_1+s_1^2}.
 \label{eq:explicit-quadratic-gap}
\end{equation}
Consequently the ideal construction in Proposition
~\ref{prop:fejer-su2-inverse} cannot be transferred by merely replacing its
$\mathrm{SU}(2)$ adjoint rotations with positive Lie--Trotter averages of
scalar-phase conjugates of the forward Laplacian, even if asymmetric
conjugates are allowed.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For real $q$ the product rule gives the exact quadratic identity
\begin{equation}
 e^{iscM_q}A_3e^{-iscM_q}
 =
 A_3+scB_q+s^2c^2M_{|\nabla q|^2},
 \qquad
 B_q=i\left(2\nabla q\cdot\nabla+\Delta_{\mathbb S^2}q\right).
 \label{eq:scalar-adjoint-parabola}
\end{equation}
Thus, for some matrices $L,R$ on $\mathcal E$ and
$W=\sum_jw_j\geq0$,
\begin{equation}
 K_s=WA_{\mathcal E}+sL+s^2R.
 \label{eq:compressed-scalar-adjoint-parabola}
\end{equation}

Let $\Phi$ be a norm-one linear functional on the matrix space with
$\Phi(A_{\mathcal E}^\circ)=\|A_{\mathcal E}^\circ\|$.  Taking traceless
parts removes every scalar phase, and applying $\Phi$ to
\eqref{eq:compressed-scalar-adjoint-parabola} gives, with
\[
 c=(W+1)\|A_{\mathcal E}^\circ\|>0,
 \qquad
 a=\frac{\Phi(L^\circ)}c,
 \qquad
 b=\frac{\Phi(R^\circ)}c,
\]
the estimate
\[
 \sup_{s\in S}
 \|(K_s+A_{\mathcal E})^\circ\|
 \geq
 c\sup_{s\in S}|1+as+bs^2|
 \geq
 c\eta_S
 \geq
 \eta_S\|A_{\mathcal E}^\circ\|.
\]
Since $\|T^\circ\|\leq2\|T\|$, this proves
\eqref{eq:scalar-fejer-average-gap}.

It remains to quantify $\eta_S$.  Put
$s_m=(s_0+s_1)/2$ and let $\ell_0,\ell_m,\ell_1$ be the Lagrange coefficients
which evaluate a quadratic at zero from its values at
$s_0,s_m,s_1$.  For every $p(s)=1+as+bs^2$,
\[
 1=p(0)=
 \ell_0p(s_0)+\ell_mp(s_m)+\ell_1p(s_1).
\]
A direct calculation gives
\[
 |\ell_0|+|\ell_m|+|\ell_1|
 =
 \frac{s_0^2+6s_0s_1+s_1^2}{(s_1-s_0)^2}.
\]
Therefore $\|p\|_{C(S)}\geq
(s_1-s_0)^2/(s_0^2+6s_0s_1+s_1^2)$, proving
\eqref{eq:explicit-quadratic-gap}.  In particular $\eta_S>0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Drift-decorated broadband transpositions close the inverse]
\label{prop:broadband-transposition-criterion}
Let $A\geq0$ be self-adjoint and let
\[
 \mathcal E=\operatorname{span}\{e_1,\ldots,e_d\},
 \qquad
 Ae_j=\lambda_je_j,
 \qquad
 \lambda_\ast=\max_j\lambda_j.
\]
Choose orthonormal auxiliary eigenvectors $f_1,\ldots,f_d\perp\mathcal E$,
\begin{equation}
 Af_j=\mu_jf_j,
 \qquad
 \Delta_j=\mu_j-\lambda_j>0,
 \qquad
 \min_j\Delta_j>d\lambda_\ast,
 \label{eq:high-auxiliary-gap}
\end{equation}
and put
$\mathcal F=\operatorname{span}\{e_1,\ldots,e_d,f_1,\ldots,f_d\}$.
Let $P_j$ be the unitary on $\mathcal F$ which swaps $e_j$ and $f_j$ and fixes
the other displayed basis vectors.

Suppose that, for every $\delta>0$ and every $j$, there is a physically
admissible positive-flight scalar-mask word $\mathcal S_{j,s}$, with total
free-flight time $T_j\geq0$, and a continuous phase $\xi_j:S\to\T$ such that
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{s\in S}
 \|(\mathcal S_{j,s}
      -\xi_j(s)e^{-isT_jA}P_j)|_{\mathcal F}\|<\delta.
 \label{eq:phase-clean-broadband-swap}
\end{equation}
Then for every $\tau>0$ and $\varepsilon>0$ a physically admissible word
$\mathcal W_s$ and a continuous $\chi:S\to\T$ satisfy
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{s\in S}
 \|(\mathcal W_s-\chi(s)e^{is\tau A})|_{\mathcal E}\|<\varepsilon.
 \label{eq:swap-synthesized-inverse}
\end{equation}
The same implication holds after a prescribed finite compact-output safety
space is included in an $A$-invariant enlargement in
\eqref{eq:phase-clean-broadband-swap}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Set
\[
 a_0=\sum_{j=1}^d\frac1{\Delta_j},
 \qquad
 b_0=\sum_{j=1}^d\frac{\lambda_j}{\Delta_j},
 \qquad
 r=\frac{1+b_0}{a_0}.
\]
The gap condition gives
\[
 r\geq\frac1{a_0}
 \geq\frac{\min_j\Delta_j}{d}
 >\lambda_\ast.
\]
Put $T_\Sigma=\sum_jT_j$.  Choose $D>0$ so large that all the numbers
\begin{equation}
 x_j=
 \frac{
  D(r-\lambda_j)+
  \tau(b_0/a_0-\lambda_j)-T_\Sigma/a_0
  }{\Delta_j}
 >T_j,
 \label{eq:positive-echo-times}
\end{equation}
and put
\begin{equation}
 d_j=x_j-T_j>0,
 \qquad
 C=Dr+\tau\frac{b_0}{a_0}-\frac{T_\Sigma}{a_0}.
 \label{eq:echo-scalar-phase}
\end{equation}
The definitions give
\[
 \sum_{j=1}^dx_j
 =Ca_0-(D+\tau)b_0
 =D-T_\Sigma
\]
and, for every $j$,
\begin{equation}
 D\lambda_j+x_j\Delta_j=C-\tau\lambda_j.
 \label{eq:echo-eigenvalue-reversal}
\end{equation}

Let
$\widetilde{\mathcal S}_{j,s}=e^{-isT_jA}P_j$.  Since $P_jAP_j$ and $A$
are diagonal in the displayed basis,
\begin{equation}
 \widetilde{\mathcal S}_{j,s}e^{-isd_jA}
 \widetilde{\mathcal S}_{j,s}
 =
 \exp\!\left[
  -is\{T_jA+(d_j+T_j)P_jAP_j\}
 \right].
 \label{eq:drift-decorated-single-echo}
\end{equation}
All generators on the right commute.  Consider the ideal positive-flight echo
\[
 \mathcal V_s=
 \prod_{j=1}^d
 \left(
  \widetilde{\mathcal S}_{j,s}e^{-isd_jA}
  \widetilde{\mathcal S}_{j,s}
 \right).
\]
On $e_k$ the accumulated eigenvalue is
\[
 \sum_{j\neq k}(d_j+2T_j)\lambda_k
 +T_k\lambda_k+(d_k+T_k)\mu_k
 =D\lambda_k+x_k\Delta_k
 =C-\tau\lambda_k.
\]
Consequently
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal V_s|_{\mathcal E}
 =e^{-isC}e^{is\tau A}|_{\mathcal E}.
 \label{eq:ideal-swap-echo}
\end{equation}

Replace both occurrences of $P_j$ in the $j$th echo by
$\mathcal S_{j,s}$.  Since every factor is unitary,
\eqref{eq:phase-clean-broadband-swap} and one insertion of the ideal
$\mathcal F$-invariant evolution on each side give
\[
 \sup_{s\in S}
 \left\|
  \mathcal S_{j,s}e^{-isd_jA}\mathcal S_{j,s}
  -
  \xi_j(s)^2
  \widetilde{\mathcal S}_{j,s}e^{-isd_jA}
  \widetilde{\mathcal S}_{j,s}
 \right\|_{\mathcal F\to\mathcal H}
 \leq2\delta.
\]
A telescoping product estimate therefore bounds the error of all $d$ echoes
on $\mathcal E$ by $2d\delta$.  Take
$\delta<\varepsilon/(2d)$ and absorb
$e^{-isC}\prod_j\xi_j(s)^2$ into $\chi(s)$.  Concatenating the physical swap
words with the flights $d_j>0$ is again a word of the form
\eqref{eq:lens-assisted-word}.  The identical telescoping argument applies
after adjoining any finite safety space required by the compact-output
estimate.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Internal $\mathrm{SU}(2)$ swap phases cancel in the echo]
\label{prop:population-swap-phase-cancellation}
Retain the notation and the gap hypothesis of Proposition
~\ref{prop:broadband-transposition-criterion}.  Its conclusion remains true
if assumption~\eqref{eq:phase-clean-broadband-swap} is replaced by the
following weaker population-inversion hypothesis.  For every $\delta>0$ and
every $j$, there are a physically admissible word $\mathcal S_{j,s}$ with
total flight time $T_j$, a continuous scalar phase $\xi_j:S\to\T$, and
unitaries $R_{j,s}$ on $\mathcal F$ such that
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{s\in S}
 \|(\mathcal S_{j,s}
      -\xi_j(s)e^{-isT_jA}R_{j,s})|_{\mathcal F}\|<\delta,
 \label{eq:population-only-broadband-swap}
\end{equation}
where $R_{j,s}$ fixes
$\operatorname{span}\{e_j,f_j\}^{\perp}\cap\mathcal F$ and, on the ordered
basis $(e_j,f_j)$, has the form
\begin{equation}
 R_{j,s}|_{\operatorname{span}\{e_j,f_j\}}
 =
 \begin{pmatrix}0&b_j(s)\\ a_j(s)&0\end{pmatrix},
 \qquad |a_j(s)|=|b_j(s)|=1,
 \qquad a_j(s)b_j(s)=-1.
 \label{eq:su2-population-swap}
\end{equation}
Thus no uniform control of the internal population-transfer phase is needed.
It is enough to produce an isolated two-level inversion whose
interaction-picture block lies in $\mathrm{SU}(2)$ and whose complementary
safety vectors are refocused.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Let
\[
 D_j=\Id_{\mathcal F}
 -2\bigl(|e_j\rangle\langle e_j|+|f_j\rangle\langle f_j|\bigr).
\]
Equation~\eqref{eq:su2-population-swap} gives
\[
 R_{j,s}^{\,2}=D_j,
 \qquad
 R_{j,s}AR_{j,s}^{-1}=P_jAP_j.
\]
The second identity is independent of $a_j(s)$ and $b_j(s)$.  With
$d_j>0$ chosen exactly as in
\eqref{eq:positive-echo-times}--\eqref{eq:echo-scalar-phase}, the ideal
population-swap echo is therefore
\begin{align}
 &(e^{-isT_jA}R_{j,s})e^{-isd_jA}
   (e^{-isT_jA}R_{j,s})
 \notag\\
 &\qquad =
 \exp\!\left[
  -is\{T_jA+(d_j+T_j)P_jAP_j\}
 \right]D_j .
 \label{eq:population-swap-echo}
\end{align}
All the $D_j$ commute with $A$ and with every $P_kAP_k$.  Moreover,
\[
 \left.\prod_{j=1}^{d}D_j\right|_{\mathcal E}
 =-\Id_{\mathcal E},
\]
because precisely one factor changes the sign of each $e_k$.  Consequently
the product of the ideal echoes equals
$-e^{-isC}e^{is\tau A}$ on $\mathcal E$.  The minus sign and the factors
$\xi_j(s)^2$ are common scalar phases.  Replacing the ideal factors by
\eqref{eq:population-only-broadband-swap} and using the same unitary
telescoping estimate as in Proposition
~\ref{prop:broadband-transposition-criterion} completes the proof.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Point-to-point symmetrization gives a selective universal rotation]
\label{prop:pp-to-ur-symmetrization}
Let
\[
 I_x=\frac12\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\1&0\end{pmatrix},
 \qquad
 I_y=\frac12\begin{pmatrix}0&-i\\i&0\end{pmatrix},
 \qquad
 I_z=\frac12\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\0&-1\end{pmatrix},
\]
and let $V_\nu$ be the endpoint of the two-quadrature Bloch pulse
\begin{equation}
 \dot V_\nu(t)
 =-i\{\nu I_z+x(t)I_x+y(t)I_y\}V_\nu(t),
 \qquad V_\nu(0)=\Id ,
 \label{eq:bloch-pp-pulse}
\end{equation}
on $0\leq t\leq T$.  Define its time-and-phase reversal by
\[
 x^{\mathrm{tr}}(t)=x(T-t),
 \qquad
 y^{\mathrm{tr}}(t)=-y(T-t),
\]
and denote the corresponding endpoint by $V_\nu^{\mathrm{tr}}$.  Then
\begin{equation}
 V_\nu^{\mathrm{tr}}
 =e^{-i\pi I_y}V_\nu^{-1}e^{i\pi I_y}.
 \label{eq:time-phase-reversal-identity}
\end{equation}
Consequently, if $\alpha$ is a continuous real function on a compact offset
set $\Omega$ and
\begin{equation}
 V_\nu I_yV_\nu^{-1}
 =
 I_y\cos\frac{\alpha(\nu)}2
 +I_z\sin\frac{\alpha(\nu)}2,
 \qquad \nu\in\Omega,
 \label{eq:point-to-point-profile}
\end{equation}
then the positive-time concatenation of the reversed pulse followed by the
original pulse satisfies
\begin{equation}
 V_\nu V_\nu^{\mathrm{tr}}
 =e^{-i\alpha(\nu)I_x},
 \qquad \nu\in\Omega.
 \label{eq:selective-universal-rotation}
\end{equation}
If the left side of~\eqref{eq:point-to-point-profile} differs from its right
side by at most $\varepsilon$ in operator norm, then the error in
\eqref{eq:selective-universal-rotation} is at most $\pi\varepsilon$.

In particular, on two disjoint compact offset sets one may prescribe
$\alpha=\pi$ on the passband and $\alpha=0$ on the stopband, joining them
continuously through the separating gap.  Any point-to-point pulse for that
profile yields, by the exact symmetrization above, a phase-clean
$\pi$ rotation on the passband and the identity on the stopband.  Thus
spectator refocusing is not a second independent design problem in the ideal
two-level model.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Reverse the time ordering in~\eqref{eq:bloch-pp-pulse}.  Conjugation by
$e^{-i\pi I_y}$ changes the signs of $I_x$ and $I_z$ and fixes $I_y$.
The inverse propagator has the reversed Hamiltonian with every sign changed,
so the two operations together give precisely the controls
$(x(T-t),-y(T-t))$.  This proves
\eqref{eq:time-phase-reversal-identity}.

Using $e^{-i\pi I_y}=\exp(-i\pi I_y)$ and
\eqref{eq:point-to-point-profile},
\begin{align*}
 V_\nu V_\nu^{\mathrm{tr}}
 &=
 V_\nu e^{-i\pi I_y}V_\nu^{-1}e^{i\pi I_y}\\
 &=
 \exp\!\left[
  -i\pi\left(
   I_y\cos\frac{\alpha(\nu)}2
   +I_z\sin\frac{\alpha(\nu)}2
  \right)
 \right]e^{i\pi I_y}
 =e^{-i\alpha(\nu)I_x}.
\end{align*}
The last equality is the elementary half-angle decomposition of an
$x$-rotation; it is also the point-to-point to universal-rotation identity
of~\cite{LuyKobzarSkinnerKhanejaGlaser2005}.  Finally, Duhamel's formula for
two skew-Hermitian exponentials gives
\[
 \|e^{-i\pi H}-e^{-i\pi K}\|\leq\pi\|H-K\|,
\]
which proves the quantitative assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Every continuous ideal point-to-point profile is reachable]
\label{prop:ideal-pp-profile-reachable}
Let $\Omega\subset\mathbb R$ be compact and let
$\alpha\in C(\Omega;\mathbb R)$.  For every $\varepsilon>0$ there are
$T>0$ and piecewise-constant real controls $x,y$ in
\eqref{eq:bloch-pp-pulse} such that
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{\nu\in\Omega}
 \left\|
  V_\nu I_yV_\nu^{-1}
  -
  \left(
   I_y\cos\frac{\alpha(\nu)}2
   +I_z\sin\frac{\alpha(\nu)}2
  \right)
 \right\|<\varepsilon .
 \label{eq:ideal-pp-profile-reachable}
\end{equation}
The controls may be replaced by smooth compactly supported controls after an
arbitrarily small increase of the error.  In particular, on separated
passband and stopband sets, the choices $\alpha=\pi$ and $\alpha=0$ give,
after Proposition~\ref{prop:pp-to-ur-symmetrization}, an arbitrarily accurate
selective universal rotation.

This is an ideal ensemble statement only.  The construction below gives a
finite $L^1$ and $L^\infty$ norm for each fixed $\varepsilon$, but no bound
uniform as $\varepsilon\downarrow0$.  It therefore does not supply the
accuracy-independent amplitude budget required by the bounded physical
potential.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Put
\[
 X=-iI_x,\qquad Y=-iI_y,\qquad Z=-iI_z,\qquad D(\nu)=\nu Z,
\]
and work in the uniform topology of
$C(\Omega;\mathfrak{su}(2))$.  First note that the uniform reachable closure
contains the reversible constant flows generated by $X$ and $Y$.  Indeed,
for $r\in\mathbb R$, a pulse of height
$M\operatorname{sgn}(r)$ and duration $|r|/M$ gives
\[
 \exp\!\left[
  \frac{|r|}{M}
  \{D(\nu)+M\operatorname{sgn}(r)X\}
 \right]
 \longrightarrow e^{rX}
\]
uniformly on $\Omega$ as $M\to\infty$, and similarly for $Y$.  The positive
drift flow is obtained with zero control.  Conjugating it by
$e^{\pi X}$ changes $Z$ to $-Z$, so the drift $D$ is reversible in the same
closure.

The standard commutator and Lie--Trotter product formulas now give every
flow generated by the real Lie algebra of $X,Y,D$.  Since
\[
 [D,X]=\nu Y,\qquad [D,Y]=-\nu X,\qquad
 [\nu^jX,\nu^kY]=\nu^{j+k}Z,
\]
induction gives
\[
 p(\nu)X,\qquad p(\nu)Y,\qquad p(\nu)Z
\]
for every real polynomial $p$.  By the Weierstrass theorem, these polynomial
fields are uniformly dense in
$C(\Omega;\mathfrak{su}(2))$; this is the polynomial Lie-extension mechanism
used in Bloch ensemble control~\cite{LiKhaneja2009}.

Apply the preceding conclusion only to the continuous field
\[
 F(\nu)=-\frac{\alpha(\nu)}2\,iI_x .
\]
Polynomial approximation of its scalar coefficient, followed by the same
finite product formulas, produces a physical pulse whose endpoint is
uniformly as close as desired to
$G_\nu=\exp[-i\alpha(\nu)I_x/2]$.  Since
\[
 G_\nu I_yG_\nu^{-1}
 =
 I_y\cos\frac{\alpha(\nu)}2
 +I_z\sin\frac{\alpha(\nu)}2,
\]
and conjugation of $I_y$ changes by at most the endpoint error, this proves
\eqref{eq:ideal-pp-profile-reachable}.  Every finite approximating word has
finite amplitude, duration, and $L^1$ cost.  Finally, smoothing finitely many
jumps in $L^1$ changes the unitary endpoint by an arbitrarily small amount,
by Duhamel's formula.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Broadband two-level inversion has a positive energy floor]
\label{prop:zs-broadband-energy-floor}
Let $x,y\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R;\mathbb R)$ and let $V_\nu$ be the
full-line endpoint of
\begin{equation}
 \dot V_\nu(t)
 =
 -i\{\nu I_z+x(t)I_x+y(t)I_y\}V_\nu(t),
 \qquad
 V_\nu(-\infty)=\Id .
 \label{eq:full-line-bloch-pulse}
\end{equation}
Write the first column of $V_\nu(+\infty)$ as
\[
 \begin{pmatrix}a(\nu)\\ b(\nu)\end{pmatrix},
 \qquad |a(\nu)|^2+|b(\nu)|^2=1.
\]
If $J\subset\mathbb R$ is an interval of positive length and
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{\nu\in J}|a(\nu)|\leq\delta<1,
 \label{eq:uniform-inversion-transmission-bound}
\end{equation}
then
\begin{equation}
 \int_{\mathbb R}\{x(t)^2+y(t)^2\}\,\dd t
 \geq
 \frac{4|J|}{\pi}\log\frac1\delta .
 \label{eq:zs-inversion-energy-lower-bound}
\end{equation}
In particular, the same conclusion holds if $V_\nu$ is
$\delta$-close in operator norm, on $J$, to any $\mathrm{SU}(2)$ population
swap.

More generally, let finitely many pulses have inversion intervals $J_j$ whose
union contains an interval $J$.  If every pulse obeys the same error
$\delta<1$ on its own interval, then their total quadratic energy satisfies
\begin{equation}
 \sum_j\int_{\mathbb R}\{x_j(t)^2+y_j(t)^2\}\,\dd t
 \geq
 \frac{4|J|}{\pi}\log\frac1\delta .
 \label{eq:zs-cover-energy-lower-bound}
\end{equation}
Thus an isolated two-level passband cover of a fixed detuning band cannot
have arbitrarily small total quadratic cost.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Put $\omega=x+iy$.  In the normalization of the continuum selective-excitation
transform, its reflection coefficient $r$ is evaluated at $\xi=\nu/2$ and
\begin{equation}
 M_z(\nu)
 =
 \frac{1-|r(\nu/2)|^2}{1+|r(\nu/2)|^2}
 =
 2|a(\nu)|^2-1.
 \label{eq:zs-reflection-transmission-identity}
\end{equation}
Consequently
\[
 1+|r(\nu/2)|^2=\frac1{|a(\nu)|^2}.
\]
The Zakharov--Shabat energy formula, including arbitrary bound-state data, is
\begin{equation}
 \int_{\mathbb R}|\omega(t)|^2\,\dd t
 =
 \frac4\pi\int_{\mathbb R}\log(1+|r(\xi)|^2)\,\dd\xi
16\sum_k\operatorname{Im}\xi_k .
 \label{eq:magland-zs-energy-formula}
\end{equation}
The discrete-spectrum term is nonnegative; see
\cite[Section~1.7, ``Energy Formulas'']{Magland2009}.  Restrict the integral to
$\xi\in J/2$.  Equation
\eqref{eq:uniform-inversion-transmission-bound} gives
\[
 \log(1+|r(\xi)|^2)\geq2\log(1/\delta),
\]
and $|J/2|=|J|/2$.  Substitution in
\eqref{eq:magland-zs-energy-formula} proves
\eqref{eq:zs-inversion-energy-lower-bound}.

If a unitary is $\delta$-close to a population swap, its corresponding
diagonal entry has modulus at most $\delta$, so the preceding argument
applies.  Finally sum
\eqref{eq:zs-inversion-energy-lower-bound} over $j$ and use
$\sum_j|J_j|\geq|J|$ to obtain
\eqref{eq:zs-cover-energy-lower-bound}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[The matrix Zakharov--Shabat energy floor]
\label{prop:matrix-zs-energy-floor}
Let $Q\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R;
\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb C^p,\mathbb C^q))$ and consider
\begin{equation}
 \frac{\dd}{\dd t}\Psi(t,k)
 =
 \begin{pmatrix}
  -ik\Id_p&Q(t)^*\\
  -Q(t)&ik\Id_q
 \end{pmatrix}\Psi(t,k).
 \label{eq:matrix-zs-energy-system}
\end{equation}
Let $A(k)$ be the $p\times p$ transmission block for incidence from the
$\mathbb C^p$ side.  If, for every $k$ in an interval $J$, some unit input in
$\mathbb C^p$ has residual norm at most $\delta<1$ in the same block, then
\begin{equation}
 \int_{\mathbb R}\tr\{Q(t)^*Q(t)\}\,\dd t
 \geq
 \frac{2|J|}{\pi}\log\frac1\delta .
 \label{eq:matrix-zs-energy-lower-bound}
\end{equation}
The input vector may depend on $k$.  In particular the conclusion applies
when one fixed input vector is transferred to the opposite block with error
at most $\delta$ throughout $J$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The scattering matrix of~\eqref{eq:matrix-zs-energy-system} is unitary on the
real axis.  If $C(k)$ is the corresponding reflection/transfer block, then
\begin{equation}
 A(k)^*A(k)+C(k)^*C(k)=\Id_p.
 \label{eq:matrix-zs-unitarity}
\end{equation}
All singular values of $A(k)$ are therefore at most one.  The transfer
hypothesis gives $s_{\min}(A(k))\leq\delta$, and hence
\begin{equation}
 |\det A(k)|\leq\delta,\qquad k\in J.
 \label{eq:matrix-transmission-determinant-small}
\end{equation}

For completeness, we record the first matrix trace identity.  The scalar
function
\[
 a(z)=\det A(z)
\]
is analytic and of bounded type in the upper half-plane, has contractive
boundary values, and tends to one at infinity.  Compact support excludes a
singular inner factor.  The Volterra expansion of the Jost solution gives
\begin{equation}
 \log a(z)
 =
 -\frac{i}{2z}
 \int_{\mathbb R}\tr\{Q(t)^*Q(t)\}\,\dd t
 +O(z^{-2}),
 \qquad z\longrightarrow\infty,\quad\operatorname{Im}z>0.
 \label{eq:matrix-transmission-high-energy}
\end{equation}
Factor $a$ into its upper-half-plane Blaschke product and outer factor.  If
$z_n$ are its zeros, counted with multiplicity, comparison of the
$z^{-1}$ coefficients gives
\begin{equation}
 \int_{\mathbb R}\tr\{Q(t)^*Q(t)\}\,\dd t
 =
 4\sum_n\operatorname{Im}z_n
 -
 \frac2\pi\int_{\mathbb R}\log|\det A(k)|\,\dd k .
 \label{eq:matrix-zs-first-trace-identity}
\end{equation}
This is the determinant version of
\eqref{eq:magland-zs-energy-formula}.  One may first prove it when the zero
set is finite and then use the canonical-factorization monotone limit; the
Blaschke contribution is nonnegative.

Discard that contribution, restrict the real-axis integral to $J$, and use
\eqref{eq:matrix-transmission-determinant-small}.  This gives
\[
 \int_{\mathbb R}\tr(Q^*Q)\,\dd t
 \geq
 -\frac2\pi\int_J\log|\det A(k)|\,\dd k
 \geq
 \frac{2|J|}{\pi}\log\frac1\delta,
\]
which is~\eqref{eq:matrix-zs-energy-lower-bound}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[The selected adjacent-block multiband route has a cost floor]
\label{cor:dipole-multiband-energy-no-go}
Fix a finite family of ideal adjacent-shell dipole lines and a nondegenerate
energy interval $J$.  Suppose finitely many carrier words are assigned
passbands $J_j\subset J$ whose union covers $J$.  Assume that the resonant
reduction of word $j$ is a rectangular matrix Zakharov--Shabat system with
detuning $k=\kappa_j(s)$, where $\kappa_j$ is affine with nonzero slope drawn
from the fixed line family, and that this word achieves a $\delta$-accurate
population transfer throughout $J_j$.  Then the total quadratic control cost
has a strictly positive lower bound depending only on $J$, the fixed line
family, the Clebsch normalization, and $\delta<1$.  In particular it is
independent of the number and widths of the carrier passbands.

Consequently the adjacent-block tiling requested in Problem
~\ref{prob:dipole-multiband-tail} cannot at the same time have arbitrarily
small $X_3$ cost.  Chebyshev tail cancellation and Stark-residue equalization
repair operator-norm patching, but they do not repair this nonlinear
bandwidth--energy obstruction.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
On line $a$ the ideal resonant potential is
\[
 Q_a(t)=\alpha_a\sum_{q=-1}^{1}u_q(t)C_{a,q}.
\]
The map $u\mapsto Q_a$ is linear on a fixed finite-dimensional control space.
Because the line family is fixed and finite, there is one constant $C$ such
that every constituent word satisfies
\[
 \tr(Q_a(t)^*Q_a(t))
 \leq C\sum_{q=-1}^{1}|u_q(t)|^2.
\]
On the fixed physical profile space,
$\sum_q|u_q|^2$ is bounded by a constant times the squared $X_3$ norm of the
scalar mask.  Apply Proposition~\ref{prop:matrix-zs-energy-floor} to word $j$
after the affine change of variable $k=\kappa_j(s)$.  If
\[
 d_*=\min_j|\kappa_j'|>0,
\]
where the minimum may be taken over the fixed line family, then
\[
 \int_{\mathbb R}\tr(Q_j^*Q_j)\,\dd t
 \geq
 \frac{2d_*|J_j|}{\pi}\log\frac1\delta.
\]
Sum over $j$ and use $\sum_j|J_j|\geq|J|$.  The preceding norm comparison
transfers this positive lower bound to the total physical $X_3$ cost.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Exact consequence for the weak-layer idea]
\label{rem:weak-layer-energy-no-go}
The estimate is nonlinear and applies to the endpoint of the whole pulse, not
to one term of its Dyson expansion.  Splitting a $\pi$ rotation into an
arbitrarily large number of weak subpulses therefore cannot lower its energy
below~\eqref{eq:zs-inversion-energy-lower-bound} while retaining a fixed
detuning passband.  Absolute carrier start phases remain harmless for a
completed population inversion by Proposition
~\ref{prop:population-swap-phase-cancellation}; they do not evade the energy
identity.  Any surviving arbitrarily small-cost broadband construction must
use genuinely multilevel excursions and cannot be a concatenation of isolated
two-level selective inversions.
\end{remark}

\begin{corollary}[The swap obstruction is global, not local]
\label{cor:fixed-energy-swap}
Let $A_3=-\Delta_{\mathbb S^2}$, let $\mathcal F$ be a finite span of spherical
harmonics, and let $P$ be any unitary on $\mathcal F$.  For every fixed
$s_\star>0$ and $\delta>0$, there are a positive-flight scalar-mask word
$\mathcal S_s$ and a number $0<h<s_\star$ such that
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{|s-s_\star|<h}
 \|(\mathcal S_s-P)|_{\mathcal F}\|<\delta.
 \label{eq:fixed-energy-physical-swap}
\end{equation}
Thus a phase-clean physical swap always exists on a nonempty parameter
window.  The unproved content of
\eqref{eq:phase-clean-broadband-swap} is a word on the whole prescribed
continuum $s\in S$, or a quantitative lower bound on successive local window
widths strong enough to make their accumulated hazard diverge.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Extend $P$ to a unitary on $L^2(\mathbb S^2)$.  The simultaneous approximate
controllability theorem for the rotating molecule
\cite{BoscainCaponigroSigalotti2012} applies to the drift $A_3$ and the three
coordinate multiplication controls.  It gives, at one fixed scaling, a
piecewise-constant scalar-potential evolution approximating $P$ on an
orthonormal basis of $\mathcal F$ within $\delta/2$.  For the scaling
$s_\star$, rescale the control time by $s_\star^{-1}$.  Finally, on each
constant-control interval,
the Lie--Trotter product formula replaces
$e^{-is_\star t(A_3+M_q)}$ by alternating factors
$e^{-is_\star(t/n)A_3}e^{-is_\star M_{tq/n}}$.  All flight times are positive,
and finiteness of $\mathcal F$ makes the accumulated approximation uniform on
its unit sphere.

For the resulting fixed finite word, $s\mapsto\mathcal S_s\psi$ is continuous
for every $\psi\in L^2(\mathbb S^2)$: mask factors are norm-continuous because
their generators are bounded, and free factors are strongly continuous.
Strong continuity is uniform on the compact unit sphere of the
finite-dimensional space $\mathcal F$.  Hence
$s\mapsto\mathcal S_s|_{\mathcal F}$ is operator-norm continuous.  Shrinking
to some $|s-s_\star|<h$ keeps the additional error below $\delta/2$ and proves
\eqref{eq:fixed-energy-physical-swap}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Adiabatic scalar monodromy has the wrong phase orientation]
\label{prop:adiabatic-phase-orientation-no-go}
Let $S=[s_0,s_1]\Subset(0,\infty)$ with $s_0<s_1$, let
$e_0=|\mathbb S^2|^{-1/2}$, and let $e$ be an $A_3$-eigenvector with
$A_3e=\lambda e$, $\lambda>0$.  Consider any finite concatenation of
positive-time adiabatic passages for
\begin{equation}
 i\dot\psi=s\bigl(A_3+M_{q(t)}\bigr)\psi,
 \qquad q(t)\in C^\infty(\mathbb S^2;\mathbb R),
 \label{eq:adiabatic-scaled-scalar-flow}
\end{equation}
along loops based at $q=0$, interspersed with positive free flights.  Assume
the usual separated-cluster hypotheses, allowing conical crossings only among
excited levels, and suppose that the limiting adiabatic monodromy returns both
$e_0$ and $e$ to their rays.  If $U_{s,\epsilon}$ denotes the resulting slow
propagator, then for every $\tau>0$,
\begin{equation}
 \liminf_{\epsilon\downarrow0}
 \inf_{\chi\in C(S;\mathbb T)}
 \sup_{s\in S}
 \left\|
  U_{s,\epsilon}-\chi(s)e^{is\tau A_3}
 \right\|_{\operatorname{span}\{e_0,e\}\to L^2}
 \geq
 \sin\!\left(
  \frac14\min\{\tau\lambda(s_1-s_0),2\pi\}
 \right)>0.
 \label{eq:adiabatic-wrong-orientation-gap}
\end{equation}
Consequently uniform adiabatic population-transfer or tight-binding
constructions cannot supply the broadband inverse, even if their leakage is
arbitrarily small.  A successful scalar word must be genuinely nonadiabatic.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For every real scalar potential on the connected sphere, the lowest
eigenvalue of $A_3+M_q$ is simple.  Along any compact control path it therefore
stays separated from the excited cluster.  The common factor $s$ changes the
eigenvalues but not the eigenvectors.  Hence the uniform adiabatic theorem,
including its conical-crossing version
\cite{AugierBoscainSigalotti2018}, gives, after division by the phase of the
ground branch,
\begin{equation}
 \frac{\langle e,U_{s,\epsilon}e\rangle}
      {\langle e_0,U_{s,\epsilon}e_0\rangle}
 =
 \eta_\epsilon e^{-isa_\epsilon}+o(1)
 \quad\hbox{uniformly for }s\in S,
 \label{eq:adiabatic-relative-forward-action}
\end{equation}
where $\eta_\epsilon\in\mathbb T$ is geometric and independent of $s$.  The
relative dynamical action $a_\epsilon$ is a sum of positive flight
contributions and integrals
\[
 T_r\int_0^1
 \bigl(\lambda_{\nu_r(t)}(q_r(t))-\lambda_0(q_r(t))\bigr)\,dt .
\]
Every branch $\nu_r(t)$ occurring here is excited, so
$a_\epsilon\geq0$.  Excited--excited conical crossings do not change this
order inequality.

Modulo a scalar phase, the target in
\eqref{eq:adiabatic-wrong-orientation-gap} has relative phase
$e^{is\tau\lambda}$.  Thus the relevant scalar discrepancy is
\[
 \eta_\epsilon e^{-is(a_\epsilon+\tau\lambda)}-1.
\]
For $b>0$, an interval of phases
$\{\eta e^{-ibs}:s\in[s_0,s_1]\}$ has angular length
$b(s_1-s_0)$.  Optimizing its initial phase gives
\[
 \inf_{\eta\in\mathbb T}\sup_{s\in S}|\eta e^{-ibs}-1|
 =
 2\sin\!\left(\frac{b(s_1-s_0)}4\right)
 \quad\hbox{if }b(s_1-s_0)\leq2\pi,
\]
and the value is $2$ once the arc covers the circle.  Since
$b=a_\epsilon+\tau\lambda\geq\tau\lambda$, the right side is bounded below
by twice the sine in \eqref{eq:adiabatic-wrong-orientation-gap}.  If the
operator error were $\delta$, comparison on $e_0$ and $e$ would make the
relative-phase error at most $2\delta$.  Letting $\epsilon\downarrow0$ proves
the claim.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[A single scalar profile cannot isolate the high-mode line]
\label{prop:single-carrier-high-mode-no-go}
Let $S=[s_0,s_1]\Subset(0,\infty)$, put $r=s_1/s_0>1$, and write
\[
 A_3Y_{\ell m}=\lambda_\ell Y_{\ell m},
 \qquad
 \lambda_\ell=\ell(\ell+1).
\]
Fix $\ell\geq1$, $L>\ell$, and magnetic indices $m,M$, and set
\[
 \Delta=\lambda_L-\lambda_\ell=(L-\ell)(L+\ell+1).
\]
Suppose that
\begin{equation}
 L\geq \ell\,\frac{r+1}{r-1}.
 \label{eq:single-carrier-high-mode-threshold}
\end{equation}
If a spatial profile $q\in L^2(\mathbb S^2)$ cancels every ground-state
transition whose detuning band overlaps the target band, in the precise sense
that
\begin{equation}
 \langle Y_{JN},M_qY_{00}\rangle=0
 \quad\hbox{whenever}\quad
 [s_0\lambda_J,s_1\lambda_J]\cap[s_0\Delta,s_1\Delta]\neq\varnothing,
 \label{eq:cancel-overlapping-ground-lines}
\end{equation}
for every admissible $J,N$, then
\begin{equation}
 \langle Y_{LM},M_qY_{\ell m}\rangle=0.
 \label{eq:target-line-also-cancelled}
\end{equation}
Thus, in the asymptotic high-mode regime, one fixed spatial profile cannot
obtain a spectrally isolated $\ell\to L$ transition by zeroing all resonant
ground leakage.  In particular this line-isolation strategy cannot turn a
single-profile chirped population inversion into the phase-clean high-mode
swap required by Proposition~\ref{prop:broadband-transposition-criterion}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Two positive detuning intervals overlap exactly when
\[
 [s_0\lambda_J,s_1\lambda_J]\cap[s_0\Delta,s_1\Delta]\neq\varnothing
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \frac{\lambda_J}{\Delta}\in[r^{-1},r].
\]
The extreme ratios in the Clebsch--Gordan range are
\begin{equation}
 \frac{\lambda_{L-\ell}}{\Delta}
 =1-\frac{2\ell}{L+\ell+1},
 \qquad
 \frac{\lambda_{L+\ell}}{\Delta}
 =1+\frac{2\ell}{L-\ell}.
 \label{eq:clebsch-gordan-extreme-gap-ratios}
\end{equation}
Condition~\eqref{eq:single-carrier-high-mode-threshold} makes the second
ratio at most $r$ and, a fortiori, makes the first at least $r^{-1}$.
Monotonicity of $J\mapsto\lambda_J$ then shows that every degree
\[
 J\in\{L-\ell,\ldots,L+\ell\}
\]
has a ground-transition band overlapping the target band.

Because $Y_{00}$ is constant,
\[
 \langle Y_{JN},M_qY_{00}\rangle
 =|\mathbb S^2|^{-1/2}\langle Y_{JN},q\rangle.
\]
Hence~\eqref{eq:cancel-overlapping-ground-lines} annihilates every spherical
harmonic component of $q$ in degrees $L-\ell,\ldots,L+\ell$.  On the other
hand the Clebsch--Gordan product rule says that
$Y_{LM}\overline{Y_{\ell m}}$ has harmonic support only in those degrees.
Therefore it is orthogonal to $q$, which is precisely
\eqref{eq:target-line-also-cancelled}.  The argument already holds for
complex $q$; imposing the physical reality condition only restricts the
available profiles further.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Adjacent-shell resonance has full finite control algebra]
\label{prop:adjacent-shell-full-lie-algebra}
For $\ell\geq0$, let
\[
 \mathcal H_n=\ker(A_3-\lambda_n),\qquad
 \lambda_n=n(n+1),
\]
put $\mathcal G_\ell=\mathcal H_\ell\oplus\mathcal H_{\ell+1}$, and let
$P_\ell$ be the orthogonal projection onto $\mathcal G_\ell$.  Set
\[
 J=2\ell+1,\qquad
 \Delta_\ell=\lambda_{\ell+1}-\lambda_\ell=2(\ell+1),
\]
and let $\mathcal Q_J$ be the real vector space of real spherical harmonics
of pure degree $J$.  Then the following two statements hold.

\begin{enumerate}
\item If $b>a\geq0$, $\lambda_b-\lambda_a=\Delta_\ell$, and
\[
 P_{\mathcal H_b}M_qP_{\mathcal H_a}\neq0
 \quad\hbox{for some }q\in\mathcal Q_J,
\]
then $(a,b)=(\ell,\ell+1)$.

\item With
\[
 Z_\ell=
 P_\ell A_3P_\ell
 -\frac{\operatorname{tr}(P_\ell A_3P_\ell)}
        {\dim\mathcal G_\ell}P_\ell,
 \qquad
 B_q=P_\ell M_qP_\ell,
\]
the real dynamical Lie algebra is
\begin{equation}
 \operatorname{Lie}_{\mathbb R}
 \{\,iZ_\ell,iB_q:q\in\mathcal Q_J\,\}
 =
 \mathfrak{su}(\mathcal G_\ell)
 \cong\mathfrak{su}(4\ell+4).
 \label{eq:adjacent-shell-full-su}
\end{equation}
\end{enumerate}
Thus the phase-clean gate is not obstructed by the exact resonant
finite-dimensional algebra.  What remains is to lift an ensemble word for
this block to the full sphere uniformly in $s$, with leakage control when
distinct transition bands overlap.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Write $d=b-a\geq1$.  Equality of the gaps gives
\[
 d(a+b+1)=2(\ell+1).
\]
If $d=1$, then $a=\ell$ and $b=\ell+1$.  If $d\geq2$, then
$a+b\leq\ell<J$.  The spherical-harmonic triangle rule requires
$J\leq a+b$ for a degree-$J$ multiplier to couple
$\mathcal H_a$ to $\mathcal H_b$, a contradiction.  This proves the first
claim.

For the second claim, complexify.  Under rotations,
$\mathcal H_n$ is the irreducible spin-$n$ module $V_n$, and the degree-$J$
profiles form $V_J$.  Since $J=2\ell+1$, the triangle and parity rules make
$B_q$ purely off diagonal on
$V_\ell\oplus V_{\ell+1}$.  Its lower-left block is the nonzero equivariant
irreducible tensor map
\[
 T:V_J\longrightarrow
 \operatorname{Hom}(V_\ell,V_{\ell+1}).
\]
The two eigenvalues of $Z_\ell$ on the summands are distinct.  Commuting
$B_q$ with $Z_\ell$ therefore separates, in the complexified Lie algebra,
each tensor component $T_N:V_\ell\to V_{\ell+1}$ from its adjoint
$T_N^\ast$.

Coupled commutators $[T_N,T_M^\ast]$ are block diagonal.  The standard
tensor recoupling formula shows that their rank-$K$ components on the lower
and upper shells have reduced coefficients proportional, respectively, to
\begin{equation}
 \begin{Bmatrix}
  J&J&K\\ \ell&\ell&\ell+1
 \end{Bmatrix},
 \qquad
 \begin{Bmatrix}
  J&J&K\\ \ell+1&\ell+1&\ell
 \end{Bmatrix}.
 \label{eq:adjacent-shell-racah-coefficients}
\end{equation}
The first is nonzero for $0\leq K\leq2\ell$, and the second is nonzero for
$0\leq K\leq2\ell+2$.  Indeed, with $J=2\ell+1$, the Racah sum for either
$6j$ symbol has the single index
$z=4\ell+2+K$; every factorial in that term is nonnegative in the displayed
range.

For $K=2\ell+1$ and $K=2\ell+2$ there is no lower-shell tensor component.
Consequently the complexified Lie algebra contains the two highest
irreducible tensor spaces, supported on $V_{\ell+1}$ alone.  We use the
following elementary matrix-unit fact.  On a spin-$L$ module, a Lie algebra
containing the complete tensor spaces of ranks $2L$ and $2L-1$ is
$\mathfrak{sl}(V_L)$.  Indeed, the weight-$2L$ tensor is a nonzero multiple
of $E_{L,-L}$.  At weight $2L-1$, the rank-$2L$ and rank-$(2L-1)$ tensors
are two independent combinations of
$E_{L,-L+1}$ and $E_{L-1,-L}$, so both matrix units belong to the algebra.
Their adjoints and brackets give the corresponding diagonal differences.
Descending the tensor weight, these already obtained diagonal differences
separate the nonzero Clebsch--Gordan coefficients in the rank-$2L$ and
rank-$(2L-1)$ tensors; induction gives every $E_{m',m}$ with $m'\neq m$.
Their brackets give the full traceless diagonal algebra.  Taking
$L=\ell+1$ therefore yields
$\mathfrak{sl}(V_{\ell+1})$ supported on the upper block.

Finally $T_0$ is injective.  In the magnetic basis it maps the vector with
index $m$ to a nonzero multiple of the upper-shell vector with the same
index; nonvanishing follows from the saturated triangle
$J=\ell+(\ell+1)$.  Acting on $T_0$ from the left by
$\mathfrak{sl}(V_{\ell+1})$, and adjoining $T_0$ itself, produces every map
$V_\ell\to V_{\ell+1}$.  Taking adjoints produces every reverse map, and
their commutators produce all block-diagonal matrices of total trace zero.
Thus the complexified algebra is
$\mathfrak{sl}(4\ell+4,\mathbb C)$.  The original generators are
skew-Hermitian and traceless, so their compact real form is exactly
\eqref{eq:adjacent-shell-full-su}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Forward drift suffices on the finite adjacent-shell ensemble]
\label{prop:finite-adjacent-shell-ensemble-gate}
Retain the notation of Proposition
~\ref{prop:adjacent-shell-full-lie-algebra}, let
$S\Subset(0,\infty)$ be a compact interval, and choose a real basis
$q_1,\ldots,q_{4\ell+3}$ of $\mathcal Q_{2\ell+1}$.  Consider
\begin{equation}
 \dot U_s(t)=
 -is\left(
  Z_\ell+\sum_{\nu=1}^{4\ell+3}u_\nu(t)B_{q_\nu}
 \right)U_s(t),
 \qquad U_s(0)=\Id ,
 \label{eq:finite-adjacent-shell-ensemble}
\end{equation}
where the controls are real and piecewise constant.  For every continuous
$G:S\to\mathrm{SU}(\mathcal G_\ell)$ and every $\varepsilon>0$, there are a time
$T>0$ and controls $u_\nu$ such that
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{s\in S}\|U_s(T)-G(s)\|<\varepsilon.
 \label{eq:finite-adjacent-shell-uniform-gate}
\end{equation}
Moreover the same endpoint lies in the uniform closure of finite products
of signed compressed scalar masks and positive compressed free flights.
Thus positivity of the Talbot drift is not an obstruction on the finite
resonant block.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
First identify the control-only algebra.  In a real spherical-harmonic
basis every $B_q$ has the block form
\[
 B_q=\begin{pmatrix}0&C_q^{\mathsf T}\\ C_q&0\end{pmatrix}.
\]
Conjugation by
$D=\operatorname{diag}(\Id_{\mathcal H_\ell},
i\Id_{\mathcal H_{\ell+1}})$ sends $iB_q$ to
\[
 \begin{pmatrix}0&C_q^{\mathsf T}\\-C_q&0\end{pmatrix},
\]
so the control-only algebra $\mathfrak h_\ell$ is compact and lies in
$D^{-1}\mathfrak{so}(4\ell+4)D$.  Its action on $\mathcal G_\ell$ is
irreducible.  To see this without assuming a Galerkin invariance, consider
the finite matrices only.  Products $B_qB_p$ contain the coupled tensors
$T_q^\ast T_p$ on $V_\ell$ and $T_qT_p^\ast$ on $V_{\ell+1}$.  The profile
indices may be coupled into irreducible ranks.  For ranks $2\ell+1$ and
$2\ell+2$ the lower block has no such tensor, while the upper Racah
coefficient in \eqref{eq:adjacent-shell-racah-coefficients} is nonzero.
Hence the unital associative algebra generated by the $B_q$ contains,
supported on $V_{\ell+1}$ alone, the complete two highest tensor spaces.
The matrix-unit induction in the preceding proof shows that these generate
$\operatorname{End}(V_{\ell+1})$; in particular, the upper block projection
belongs to the associative algebra.  Multiplying $B_q$ by the two block
projections separates $T_q$ from $T_q^\ast$.  Since $T_0$ is injective,
left multiplication of $T_0$ by
$\operatorname{End}(V_{\ell+1})$ gives every map
$V_\ell\to V_{\ell+1}$.  The algebra is closed under adjoints, so it also
contains every reverse map, and their products give
$\operatorname{End}(V_\ell)$.  Thus the associative algebra is
$\operatorname{End}(\mathcal G_\ell)$ and its commutant consists only of
scalars.

The connected control-only group $H_\ell$ is therefore compact and
irreducible.  Its Lie algebra is semisimple: the center of a compact
irreducible matrix Lie algebra is scalar by Schur's lemma, whereas every
element here is traceless.  The driftless ensemble generated by the $B_q$
is uniformly controllable on $H_\ell$.  To apply the semisimple ensemble
theorem of \cite{LiZhang2020} with its stated hypotheses, choose a finite
cover of $\mathfrak h_\ell$ by spin-representation $\mathfrak{su}(2)$
triples.  Every member of those triples is a Lie polynomial in the $iB_q$.
Uniform commutator products realize it as a reversible Lie extension with
coefficient $s^d$ for some $d\geq1$.  The resulting finite parameter curve
$(s^{d_1},\ldots,s^{d_m})$ lies in the positive orthant, and each fixed-$s$
system generates $H_\ell$.  The cited theorem therefore gives uniform
controllability on $C(S,H_\ell)$.  These control-only gates remain in the
uniform closure of \eqref{eq:finite-adjacent-shell-ensemble}: for fixed
$t\in\mathbb R$,
\[
 \exp\!\left[
  -is\frac{|t|}{M}
  \bigl(Z_\ell+M\operatorname{sgn}(t)B_q\bigr)
 \right]
 \longrightarrow e^{-istB_q}
\]
uniformly for $s\in S$ as $M\to\infty$.

The Haar average of $RZ_\ell R^{-1}$ over $H_\ell$ commutes with
$H_\ell$ and is therefore scalar by irreducibility.  Its trace is zero, so
the average vanishes.  The orbit spans an $H_\ell$-invariant real space
containing $Z_\ell$.  If zero were on the relative boundary of its convex
hull, a nonzero supporting functional would be nonnegative on the orbit;
its zero Haar average would force it to vanish on the entire orbit and hence
on its span, a contradiction.  Thus zero is a relative interior point.
By Carath\'eodory's theorem there are
$R_1,\ldots,R_m\in H_\ell$, weights $w_j>0$, and $c>0$ such that
\begin{equation}
 -cZ_\ell=\sum_{j=1}^m w_jR_jZ_\ell R_j^{-1}.
 \label{eq:orthogonal-orbit-negative-drift}
\end{equation}
Approximate each constant $R_j$ and its inverse by the control-only
ensemble.  Positive free flights conjugated by these gates, followed by
the Lie product formula and \eqref{eq:orthogonal-orbit-negative-drift},
uniformly synthesize $e^{+istZ_\ell}$ for every $t>0$.  Thus both signs of
the drift, as well as both signs of every control, belong to the uniform
reachable closure.

The system is now effectively driftless with reversible generators whose
finite Lie algebra is
$\mathfrak{su}(4\ell+4)$ by Proposition
~\ref{prop:adjacent-shell-full-lie-algebra}.  Repeat the preceding
spin-triple Lie-extension construction for
$\mathfrak{su}(4\ell+4)$.  Its parameter curve again consists of positive
powers of $s$, every fixed-$s$ system is controllable, and the semisimple
ensemble theorem supplies every target in
$C(S,\mathrm{SU}(\mathcal G_\ell))$.  This proves
\eqref{eq:finite-adjacent-shell-uniform-gate}.
Finally, a uniform Lie--Trotter splitting of every constant-control
interval in \eqref{eq:finite-adjacent-shell-ensemble} gives signed scalar
masks separated by positive free flights.  A signed constant mask removes
the scalar trace phase that distinguishes $P_\ell A_3P_\ell$ from $Z_\ell$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[The physical resonant cluster grows with angular momentum]
\label{prop:adjacent-shell-growing-resonant-cluster}
Let $S=[s_0,s_1]\Subset(0,\infty)$, $r=s_1/s_0>1$, fix $\ell\geq0$, and put
$J=2\ell+1$.  For every integer
\begin{equation}
 \ell\leq a\leq\lfloor r(\ell+1)\rfloor-1
 \label{eq:overlapping-adjacent-shell-range}
\end{equation}
and every nonzero $q\in\mathcal Q_J$,
\begin{equation}
 P_{\mathcal H_{a+1}}M_qP_{\mathcal H_a}\neq0,
 \qquad
 [s_0\Delta_a,s_1\Delta_a]\cap
 [s_0\Delta_\ell,s_1\Delta_\ell]\neq\varnothing,
 \quad
 \Delta_a=2(a+1).
 \label{eq:every-overlapping-adjacent-coupling}
\end{equation}
In particular, if
\begin{equation}
 \ell+1\geq\frac1{r-1},
 \label{eq:next-shell-overlap-threshold}
\end{equation}
then every nonzero target profile also couples the next pair
$\mathcal H_{\ell+1}\leftrightarrow\mathcal H_{\ell+2}$, whose detuning
band overlaps the target band.  The number of obligatorily coupled adjacent
pairs is
\[
 \lfloor r(\ell+1)\rfloor-\ell,
\]
which grows linearly in $\ell$.  Consequently an isolated two-shell
rotating-wave estimate cannot lift Proposition
~\ref{prop:finite-adjacent-shell-ensemble-gate} on any fixed nondegenerate
energy interval.  A valid lift must control the entire growing resonant
cluster, whose Hilbert-space dimension is of order $\ell^2$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For $a\geq\ell$, the degree $J=2\ell+1$ satisfies
\[
 |(a+1)-a|\leq J\leq a+(a+1),
 \qquad
 a+(a+1)+J\in2\mathbb Z.
\]
Thus $V_J$ occurs once in
$V_{a+1}\otimes V_a^\ast$.  The map
\[
 q\longmapsto P_{\mathcal H_{a+1}}M_qP_{\mathcal H_a}
\]
is a nonzero equivariant map from the irreducible module $V_J$, hence is
injective.  This proves the first assertion in
\eqref{eq:every-overlapping-adjacent-coupling}.  The two positive detuning
bands overlap exactly when
\[
 \frac{\Delta_a}{\Delta_\ell}
 =\frac{a+1}{\ell+1}\in[r^{-1},r].
\]
The lower inequality is automatic for $a\geq\ell$, and the upper inequality
is exactly \eqref{eq:overlapping-adjacent-shell-range}.  Taking
$a=\ell+1$ gives \eqref{eq:next-shell-overlap-threshold}.  The count and the
quadratic dimension estimate follow by summing
$\dim\mathcal H_a=2a+1$ over the displayed range.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Exact adjacent-shell band-isolation threshold]
\label{prop:adjacent-shell-band-isolation}
Retain the notation of Proposition
~\ref{prop:adjacent-shell-growing-resonant-cluster}, let $q\in\mathcal Q_J$ be
nonzero, and write $\Delta_\ell=2(\ell+1)$.  Among all positive spectral gaps
\[
 \delta_{a,b}=\lambda_b-\lambda_a,\qquad b>a\geq0,
\]
for which
$P_{\mathcal H_b}M_qP_{\mathcal H_a}\neq0$, the smallest is
$\Delta_\ell$, attained only by $(a,b)=(\ell,\ell+1)$, and the next smallest
is
\[
 \Delta_{\ell+1}=2(\ell+2)
 =\left(1+\frac1{\ell+1}\right)\Delta_\ell .
\]
Every nonadjacent admissible gap is at least $3\Delta_\ell$.  Consequently
\begin{equation}
 [s_0\Delta_\ell,s_1\Delta_\ell]
 \cap[s_0\delta_{a,b},s_1\delta_{a,b}]
 =\varnothing
 \quad\hbox{for every other coupled pair }(a,b)
 \label{eq:adjacent-shell-all-band-isolation}
\end{equation}
if and only if
\begin{equation}
 \frac{s_1}{s_0}<1+\frac1{\ell+1}.
 \label{eq:adjacent-shell-ratio-threshold}
\end{equation}
For a centred window $S=[c-h,c+h]\Subset(0,\infty)$ this is exactly
\begin{equation}
 h<\frac{c}{2\ell+3}.
 \label{eq:adjacent-shell-centred-halfwidth}
\end{equation}

In particular, the growing cluster in Proposition
~\ref{prop:adjacent-shell-growing-resonant-cluster} is unavoidable on a fixed
nondegenerate interval as $\ell\to\infty$, but it disappears on windows of
the sharp relative scale $h\asymp(\ell+1)^{-1}$.  If
$c_j$ remains in one compact subset of $(0,\infty)$ and
$h_j=\vartheta c_j/(2\ell_j+3)$ with fixed $0<\vartheta<1$, then
\begin{equation}
 \sum_j h_j=\infty
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \sum_j\frac1{\ell_j+1}=\infty.
 \label{eq:adjacent-shell-harmonic-hazard}
\end{equation}
Thus an isolated-block implementation would reduce the chromatic stopping
test to a quantitative bound on the growth of the auxiliary angular momenta;
mere existence of a local gate gives no such bound.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Put $d=b-a$.  The Clebsch--Gordan rules for a degree-$J$ multiplier require
\begin{equation}
 d\leq J\leq a+b,\qquad a+b+J\in2\mathbb Z.
 \label{eq:pure-degree-admissible-pair}
\end{equation}
Conversely, for every pair satisfying these rules, the map
\[
 \mathcal Q_J\longrightarrow
 \operatorname{Hom}(\mathcal H_a,\mathcal H_b),\qquad
 q\longmapsto P_{\mathcal H_b}M_qP_{\mathcal H_a},
\]
is a nonzero equivariant map out of the irreducible module $V_J$ and hence is
injective.  Thus every nonzero $q$ couples every admissible pair.

Because $J=2\ell+1$ is odd, the parity condition in
\eqref{eq:pure-degree-admissible-pair} makes $d$ odd.  If $d=1$, then
$J\leq2a+1$ gives $a\geq\ell$, and
\[
 \delta_{a,a+1}=2(a+1).
\]
The first two such gaps are therefore $\Delta_\ell$ and
$\Delta_{\ell+1}$.  If $d\geq3$, then $a+b\geq J$ and
\[
 \delta_{a,b}
 =d(a+b+1)
 \geq d(J+1)
 \geq3\Delta_\ell.
\]
This proves the ordering and also recovers the uniqueness assertion of
Proposition~\ref{prop:adjacent-shell-full-lie-algebra}.

For every other coupled pair the gap satisfies
$\delta_{a,b}>\Delta_\ell$.  Its positive detuning band meets the target band
exactly when
\[
 s_0\delta_{a,b}\leq s_1\Delta_\ell
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \frac{\delta_{a,b}}{\Delta_\ell}\leq\frac{s_1}{s_0}.
\]
The smallest ratio on the left is
$(\ell+2)/(\ell+1)$, proving
\eqref{eq:adjacent-shell-all-band-isolation}--%
\eqref{eq:adjacent-shell-ratio-threshold}.  Substituting
$s_0=c-h$ and $s_1=c+h$ and cross-multiplying gives
$(2\ell+3)h<c$, which is
\eqref{eq:adjacent-shell-centred-halfwidth}.  On a fixed compact set of
centres, $c_j$ is bounded above and below by positive constants, so comparison
with the harmonic series gives
\eqref{eq:adjacent-shell-harmonic-hazard}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Finite growing-cluster audit]
For
\[
 \mathcal C_{\ell,A}=\bigoplus_{a=\ell}^{A}\mathcal H_a,
 \qquad N_{\ell,A}=\dim\mathcal C_{\ell,A},
\]
direct finite Lie closure gives the following checks for all real
degree-$(2\ell+1)$ profiles:
\[
\begin{array}{c|c|c|c}
(\ell,A)&N_{\ell,A}&\dim\mathfrak h_{\rm control}
&\dim\mathfrak h_{\rm drift+control}\\ \hline
(0,2)&9&36&80\\
(1,3)&15&105&224\\
(2,4)&21&210&440\\
(3,5)&27&351&728
\end{array}
\]
Thus in every checked growing cluster the control-only algebra has dimension
$N_{\ell,A}(N_{\ell,A}-1)/2$, and adjoining the compressed drift gives
$N_{\ell,A}^2-1$.  This is evidence for conjugates of
$\mathfrak{so}(N_{\ell,A})$ and $\mathfrak{su}(N_{\ell,A})$, respectively;
it is not used as a theorem for arbitrary $\ell$ and $A$.

There is also a decisive warning.  The noncommutativity of the compressed
controls comes from inserting $P_{\mathcal C_{\ell,A}}$ between scalar
multipliers.  On the full sphere the multiplication operators themselves
commute.  Therefore even a proof of the displayed finite pattern would not
be the physical lift: one must retain drift-mediated noncommutativity and
prove that excursions outside the growing cluster refocus uniformly.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Exact scope of the available infinite-dimensional control theorems]
\label{rem:infinite-control-theorem-audit}
Two quantitative ingredients needed for a physical lift are already known
for one fixed system.  The multi-input theorem of
\cite{BoscainCaponigroSigalotti2013} proves phase-sensitive approximate
simultaneous controllability of any prescribed finite frame for the
three-dimensional rotating molecule, with an $L^1$ control bound independent
of the final accuracy.  The bounded-variation Galerkin theorem of
\cite{BoussaidCaponigroChambrion2014} says that, after a total-variation bound
and a finite input frame are fixed, one Galerkin cutoff approximates the full
evolution uniformly in time for every control inside that bound.  Thus, at
one fixed scaling, leakage tolerance does not force a circular choice between
the control budget and the cutoff.

Neither result contains the required continuum quantifier.  The partial
ensemble theorem of \cite{Chambrion2013} keeps the drift fixed, places the
uncertain parameter only in the control coupling, and prescribes moduli of
matrix entries on a selected two-level block rather than a phased unitary
frame.  Here the family is
\[
 \dot U_s=-is\left(A_3+\sum_\nu u_\nu(t)M_{q_\nu}\right)U_s,
 \qquad s\in S,
\]
so the drift frequencies and all control strengths scale together.  The
small-time theorem of \cite{ChambrionPozzoli2023} also does not remove this
mismatch: its reachable maps for a general state are multiplication phases
$e^{i\phi}$, and its sphere corollary transfers only the two extremal
harmonics $Y^j_{-j}$ and $Y^j_j$ inside one degenerate eigenspace.  It does
not provide a simultaneous finite-frame gate.

Accordingly, the missing theorem is now quantitative and unambiguous.  One
must either prove a phased, bounded-budget ensemble extension for the
fully scaled family above, strong enough to invoke the uniform Galerkin
estimate, or construct isolated adjacent-shell windows with indices
$\ell_j$ satisfying the nonsummability condition
\eqref{eq:adjacent-shell-harmonic-hazard}.  Pointwise control, local
continuity, and small-time phase synthesis do not imply either alternative.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Uniform Galerkin approximation for the fully scaled family]
\label{prop:uniform-scaled-galerkin}
Let $A=A_3$, let $B_1,\ldots,B_m$ be multiplication by fixed smooth real
functions on $\mathbb S^2$, and let $P_N$ be the projection onto the first
$N$ eigenspaces of $A$.  Let $S=[s_0,s_1]\Subset(0,\infty)$.  Given
$L<\infty$, $\varepsilon>0$, and finitely many smooth input vectors
$\psi_1,\ldots,\psi_r$, there is $N$ such that, for every $s\in S$, every
$T>0$, and every piecewise integrable real control satisfying
\[
 \sum_{\nu=1}^m\int_0^T|u_\nu(t)|\,\dd t\leq L,
\]
the full propagator $U_s^u$ of
\[
 i\dot U_s^u
 =s\left(A+\sum_{\nu=1}^mu_\nu(t)B_\nu\right)U_s^u
\]
and its $N$-dimensional Galerkin propagator $U_{s,N}^u$ satisfy
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{\substack{s\in S\\0\leq t\leq T\\1\leq p\leq r}}
 \left\|
  U_s^u(t)\psi_p-U_{s,N}^u(t)P_N\psi_p
 \right\|<\varepsilon.
 \label{eq:uniform-scaled-galerkin}
\end{equation}
Thus the common-scale parameter creates no additional leakage/cutoff
circularity once one common control with an a priori $L^1$ bound has been
constructed.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For fixed $s$, set $\tau=st$ and
$v_{\nu,s}(\tau)=u_\nu(\tau/s)$.  In the $\tau$ variable both the full and
Galerkin equations have the unscaled drift $A$ and controls $v_{\nu,s}$.
Moreover
\[
 \sum_{\nu=1}^m\int_0^{sT}|v_{\nu,s}(\tau)|\,\dd\tau
 =s\sum_{\nu=1}^m\int_0^T|u_\nu(t)|\,\dd t
 \leq s_1L.
\]
Apply the bounded-variation Galerkin theorem of
\cite{BoussaidCaponigroChambrion2014} with the single budget $s_1L$.
Its cutoff is uniform in time and over every control inside that budget, so
the same $N$ works for every $s\in S$.  Replacing $\tau$ by $st$ gives
\eqref{eq:uniform-scaled-galerkin}.  The stated finite-control version follows
by the same estimate with the variation norm $\sum_\nu|v_\nu|$; the proof of
the cited theorem is unchanged for finitely many bounded control operators.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Full-operator bandpass filter on an isolated shell pair]
\label{prop:isolated-shell-bandpass-filter}
Let $J=2\ell+1$, let $0\neq q\in\mathcal Q_J$, and let
\[
 C_q=P_{\mathcal H_{\ell+1}}M_qP_{\mathcal H_\ell},
 \qquad
 \Delta_\ell=2(\ell+1).
\]
Suppose $S=[s_0,s_1]$ satisfies
\eqref{eq:adjacent-shell-ratio-threshold}.  For every
$f\in C(S;\mathbb C)$ and every $\varepsilon>0$, there are $T>0$ and a real
$u\in C_c^\infty((0,T))$ such that
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{s\in S}
 \left\|
  \int_0^T
   u(t)e^{istA_3}M_qe^{-istA_3}\,\dd t
  -
  \{f(s)C_q+\overline{f(s)}C_q^\ast\}
 \right\|
 <\varepsilon.
 \label{eq:full-operator-bandpass-filter}
\end{equation}
The norm is the full operator norm on $L^2(\mathbb S^2)$; no Galerkin
projection is inserted.

Consequently, if $U_{s,\eta}$ solves
\[
 i\dot U_{s,\eta}
 =s(A_3+\eta u(t)M_q)U_{s,\eta},
 \qquad U_{s,\eta}(0)=\Id,
\]
and $V_{s,\eta}(T)=e^{isTA_3}U_{s,\eta}(T)$, then for every
$g\in C(S;\mathbb C)$ the filter may be chosen with $f(s)=g(s)/s$, and
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{s\in S}
 \left\|
  V_{s,\eta}(T)
  -\Id
  +i\eta\{g(s)C_q+\overline{g(s)}C_q^\ast\}
 \right\|
 \leq
 s_1|\eta|\varepsilon+\tfrac12X_\eta^2e^{X_\eta},
 \label{eq:full-operator-small-filtered-gate}
\end{equation}
where
\[
 X_\eta=s_1|\eta|\,\|q\|_\infty\|u\|_{L^1}.
\]
Hence every continuous complex quadrature of the target transition is a
genuine full-sphere interaction-picture Lie direction.  By first sending the
filter error to zero and then taking $\eta$ sufficiently small relative to
$\|u\|_{L^1}$, one obtains a sequence with full operator-norm leakage
$o(|\eta|)$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Use the Fourier convention
\[
 \widehat u(\omega)=\int_{\mathbb R}u(t)e^{i\omega t}\,\dd t.
\]
By Proposition~\ref{prop:adjacent-shell-band-isolation}, the target band
\[
 K_0=[s_0\Delta_\ell,s_1\Delta_\ell]
\]
is separated by a positive gap from every other positive frequency
$s(\lambda_b-\lambda_a)$ coupled by a degree-$J$ multiplier.  In fact all
such frequencies belong to $[\omega_1,\infty)$ for
\[
 \omega_1=s_0\Delta_{\ell+1}>s_1\Delta_\ell.
\]

We first construct a real time filter.  On $K_0$ prescribe
$F(\omega)=f(\omega/\Delta_\ell)$.  Approximate $F$ uniformly by a smooth
function, extend it smoothly to a compactly supported function on
$(0,\omega_1)$, and impose Hermitian symmetry
$F(-\omega)=\overline{F(\omega)}$.  Its inverse Fourier transform is real and
Schwartz.  Truncation in time approximates $F$ uniformly on the whole real
axis, because the discarded inverse transform has arbitrarily small
$L^1$ norm.  To put the truncation in positive time, first multiply the
prescribed frequency function by $e^{-it_0\omega}$, perform the symmetric
truncation, and then translate the time function by $t_0$.  For $t_0$ larger
than the truncation radius this gives a real
$u\in C_c^\infty((0,T))$ satisfying, for an arbitrarily small $\delta>0$,
\begin{align}
 \sup_{\omega\in K_0}|\widehat u(\omega)-F(\omega)|&<\delta,
 \label{eq:target-filter-error}\\
 \sup_{|\omega|\geq\omega_1}|\widehat u(\omega)|&<\delta.
 \label{eq:stopband-filter-error}
\end{align}

Decompose the degree-$J$ multiplier into its finitely many shell shifts,
\[
 M_q=\sum_{d=-J}^{J}B_q^{(d)},\qquad
 B_q^{(d)}=\sum_{a\geq\max\{0,-d\}}
 P_{\mathcal H_{a+d}}M_qP_{\mathcal H_a}.
\]
Only shifts of the correct parity occur.  For fixed $d$, the domain blocks
and range blocks are mutually orthogonal, so
$\|B_q^{(d)}\|\leq\|M_q\|=\|q\|_\infty$.  On the block
$\mathcal H_a\to\mathcal H_{a+d}$ the integral in
\eqref{eq:full-operator-bandpass-filter} multiplies $B_q^{(d)}$ by
\[
 \widehat u\!\left(s(\lambda_{a+d}-\lambda_a)\right).
\]
Equations~\eqref{eq:target-filter-error}--%
\eqref{eq:stopband-filter-error}, the reality relation at negative
frequencies, and the exact band isolation therefore leave
$f(s)C_q+\overline{f(s)}C_q^\ast$ on the target pair and give a residual norm
at most $(2J+1)\delta\|q\|_\infty$.  Choosing $\delta$ sufficiently small
proves~\eqref{eq:full-operator-bandpass-filter}.

Finally the interaction propagator satisfies
\[
 i\dot V_{s,\eta}
 =s\eta u(t)e^{istA_3}M_qe^{-istA_3}V_{s,\eta}.
\]
The first Dyson term, with $f=g/s$, is the displayed target in
\eqref{eq:full-operator-small-filtered-gate}; its filter error is at most
$s_1|\eta|\varepsilon$.  The sum of all Dyson terms of order at least two is
bounded by $e^{X_\eta}-1-X_\eta\leq\tfrac12X_\eta^2e^{X_\eta}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[The physical isolated-band tangent algebra is full]
\label{cor:isolated-shell-full-tangent-algebra}
Under the hypotheses of Proposition
~\ref{prop:isolated-shell-bandpass-filter}, put
\[
 H_{q,\theta}
 =e^{i\theta}C_q+e^{-i\theta}C_q^\ast,
 \qquad q\in\mathcal Q_J,\quad\theta\in\mathbb R.
\]
Then
\begin{equation}
 \operatorname{Lie}_{\mathbb R}
 \{\,iH_{q,\theta}:q\in\mathcal Q_J,\ \theta\in\mathbb R\,\}
 =\mathfrak{su}(\mathcal G_\ell).
 \label{eq:isolated-shell-full-tangent-su}
\end{equation}
Every generator on the left, with an arbitrary continuous scalar coefficient
in $s$, is obtained as a full-sphere interaction-picture tangent direction
with $o(|\eta|)$ operator-norm leakage.  Thus the isolated-band physical lift
has no Galerkin, first-variation, or finite-dimensional Lie-algebra
obstruction.  The remaining step is nonlinear: integrate these causal
time-dependent tangent directions into one drift-decorated swap with a
finite quantitative control budget.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The complexification of the displayed real Lie algebra contains $C_q$ and
$C_q^\ast$ separately, because the phases $\theta=0$ and $\theta=\pi/2$
give their two real quadratures.  The proof of Proposition
~\ref{prop:adjacent-shell-full-lie-algebra}, starting after the separation of
$T_q$ from $T_q^\ast$, then applies verbatim: the coupled commutators give
the two highest tensor ranks on the upper shell, the matrix-unit induction
gives $\mathfrak{sl}(\mathcal H_{\ell+1})$, and the injective tensor $C_q$
connects both shells.  The complexified algebra is
$\mathfrak{sl}(4\ell+4,\mathbb C)$, and its skew-Hermitian real form is
\eqref{eq:isolated-shell-full-tangent-su}.  The physical tangent assertion is
Proposition~\ref{prop:isolated-shell-bandpass-filter}, with the filter
accuracy chosen along a diagonal sequence.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Causal phase correction has superpolynomial filter cost]
\label{prop:causal-filter-cost}
Let $K=[a,b]\Subset(0,\infty)$ be nondegenerate and fix
$0<\varepsilon<1$.  For every integer $N\geq1$ there is
$c_{K,\varepsilon,N}>0$ with the following property.  If $\tau\geq1$ and
$u\in L^1(\mathbb R)$ is supported in $[\tau,\infty)$ and satisfies
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{\omega\in K}
 \left|\widehat u(\omega)-1\right|\leq\varepsilon,
 \qquad
 \widehat u(\omega)=\int_{\mathbb R}u(t)e^{i\omega t}\,\dd t,
 \label{eq:delayed-filter-fixed-phase}
\end{equation}
then
\begin{equation}
 \|u\|_{L^1}\geq c_{K,\varepsilon,N}\tau^N .
 \label{eq:delayed-filter-superpolynomial-cost}
\end{equation}
Thus the fixed-phase delayed filters required by a sequential
interaction-picture Euler construction cannot have a uniform $L^1$ budget.
More precisely, if such a filter is used in
\eqref{eq:full-operator-small-filtered-gate}, the perturbative requirement
$|\eta|\|u\|_{L^1}\to0$ forces
\[
 |\eta|\tau^N\longrightarrow0
 \qquad\text{for every fixed }N.
\]
Consequently Corollary~\ref{cor:isolated-shell-full-tangent-algebra} cannot
be integrated by repeating fixed-size first-order gates at successively
later times.  This is a no-go for that proof method, not for a one-shot
nonperturbative broadband gate.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Choose a nonnegative $\phi\in C_c^\infty((a,b))$ with
$\int_K\phi(\omega)\,\dd\omega=1$, and put
\[
 \Phi(t)=\int_K\phi(\omega)e^{i\omega t}\,\dd\omega .
\]
By Fubini and~\eqref{eq:delayed-filter-fixed-phase},
\[
 1-\varepsilon
 \leq
 \left|\int_K\phi(\omega)\widehat u(\omega)\,\dd\omega\right|
 =
 \left|\int_\tau^\infty u(t)\Phi(t)\,\dd t\right|
 \leq
 \|u\|_{L^1}\sup_{t\geq\tau}|\Phi(t)|.
\]
Integrating by parts $N$ times and using the compact support of $\phi$ gives
\[
 \sup_{t\geq\tau}|\Phi(t)|
 \leq
 \|\phi^{(N)}\|_{L^1}\tau^{-N}.
\]
This proves~\eqref{eq:delayed-filter-superpolynomial-cost} with
$c_{K,\varepsilon,N}=(1-\varepsilon)/\|\phi^{(N)}\|_{L^1}$.  The final
assertions follow from the definition of $X_\eta$ in
\eqref{eq:full-operator-small-filtered-gate}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Power-of-two saturated gaps are uniquely resonant]
\label{prop:power-two-saturated-gap}
Fix $\ell\geq0$ and $k\geq1$, and put
\begin{equation}
 d=2^k,\qquad L=\ell+d,\qquad J=\ell+L,\qquad
 N=\lambda_L-\lambda_\ell=d(2\ell+d+1),
 \quad \lambda_a=a(a+1).
 \label{eq:power-two-saturated-data}
\end{equation}
For every nonzero $q\in\mathcal Q_J$,
\[
 P_{\mathcal H_L}M_qP_{\mathcal H_\ell}\neq0.
\]
Moreover, among all positive coupled gaps
\[
 \lambda_b-\lambda_a,\qquad
 b>a\geq0,\qquad
 P_{\mathcal H_b}M_qP_{\mathcal H_a}\neq0,
\]
the value $N$ occurs only for $(a,b)=(\ell,L)$.

All spherical Laplacian gaps are even integers.  Consequently, if
$S=[c-h,c+h]\Subset(0,\infty)$ and
\begin{equation}
 h\leq\frac{c}{2(N+1)},
 \label{eq:saturated-carrier-isolation-window}
\end{equation}
then every carrier mismatch
\[
 s(\lambda_b-\lambda_a)\pm cN
\]
which is not one of the two resonant orientations of the target pair is
bounded away from zero uniformly in $s\in S$ and in the coupled pair
$(a,b)$.  The lower bound may be chosen to depend only on $c$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The triangle and parity rules are satisfied by the target pair because
$J=\ell+L$ is saturated and $2J$ is even.  For uniqueness, suppose that a
coupled pair $(a,b)$, $b>a$, has gap $N$.  Set
\[
 d'=b-a,\qquad n'=a+b+1.
\]
Then $N=d'n'$.  Since $J$ is even, the coupling parity rule makes $a+b$
even, and hence $n'$ is odd.  Write
\[
 n=2\ell+d+1=J+1,
\]
which is also odd.  Since $N=2^kn$ and $n'$ is odd, $n'$ divides $n$.
On the other hand the triangle rule $J\leq a+b$ gives
$n'\geq J+1=n$.  Thus $n'=n$, then $d'=d$, and the identities defining
$d'$ and $n'$ give $(a,b)=(\ell,L)$.

Because every $\lambda_a$ is even, distinct positive gaps differ by at
least two.  Under~\eqref{eq:saturated-carrier-isolation-window}, the nearest
possible gaps $N-2$ and $N+2$ already have a carrier separation bounded
below by $c$; gaps farther away have a larger separation.  The
counter-rotating target orientation has size at least $cN$.  The same
argument after changing signs handles negative gaps.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Saturated carrier quadratures generate the full block]
\label{prop:saturated-carrier-full-su}
Retain~\eqref{eq:power-two-saturated-data}, put
\[
 \mathcal G_{\ell,L}=\mathcal H_\ell\oplus\mathcal H_L,\qquad
 C_q=P_{\mathcal H_L}M_qP_{\mathcal H_\ell},
\]
and define the carrier quadratures
\begin{equation}
 H_{q,\theta}=e^{i\theta}C_q+e^{-i\theta}C_q^\ast,
 \qquad q\in\mathcal Q_J,\quad\theta\in\mathbb R.
 \label{eq:saturated-carrier-quadratures}
\end{equation}
Then
\begin{equation}
 \operatorname{Lie}_{\mathbb R}
 \{\,iH_{q,\theta}:q\in\mathcal Q_J,\ \theta\in\mathbb R\,\}
 =
 \mathfrak{su}(\mathcal G_{\ell,L}).
 \label{eq:saturated-carrier-full-su}
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Complexification separates $C_q$ and $C_q^\ast$ by taking
$\theta=0$ and $\theta=\pi/2$.  Under rotations,
$\mathcal H_a$ is the spin-$a$ module $V_a$, while the profiles form $V_J$.
The coupled commutators $[C_q,C_p^\ast]$ are block diagonal.  Their rank-$K$
component on the upper block has reduced coefficient proportional to
\begin{equation}
 \begin{Bmatrix}
  J&J&K\\ L&L&\ell
 \end{Bmatrix}.
 \label{eq:saturated-upper-racah}
\end{equation}
For $0\leq K\leq2L$ the Racah sum for
\eqref{eq:saturated-upper-racah} has the single index $2J+K$.
Every factorial in that term is nonnegative, so the coefficient is nonzero.
The lower block has no tensor component of rank $K>2\ell$.
In particular, the complete tensor spaces of ranks $2L$ and $2L-1$ occur
on the upper block alone.  The matrix-unit argument in the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:adjacent-shell-full-lie-algebra} therefore gives
$\mathfrak{sl}(V_L)$ supported on that block.

For the zonal profile $q_0\in V_J$, the saturated Clebsch--Gordan
coefficients show that $C_{q_0}:V_\ell\to V_L$ maps every magnetic basis
vector to a nonzero multiple of the vector with the same magnetic index.
Thus $C_{q_0}$ is injective.  Left multiplication by
$\operatorname{End}(V_L)$ now spans all of
$\operatorname{Hom}(V_\ell,V_L)$; the identity multiple is supplied by
$C_{q_0}$ itself and the traceless multiples by
$\mathfrak{sl}(V_L)$.  Adjoints give every reverse map, and commutators give
all block-diagonal matrices of total trace zero.  Hence the complexified
algebra is
\[
 \mathfrak{sl}(V_\ell\oplus V_L).
\]
The original generators are skew-Hermitian and traceless, so the real
compact form is exactly~\eqref{eq:saturated-carrier-full-su}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Finite algebra audit]
The two-quadrature Lie closures give
\[
 \begin{array}{c|c|c}
  (\ell,L)&\text{computed dimension}&\text{target algebra}\\ \hline
  (0,2)&35&\mathfrak{su}(6)\\
  (1,3)&99&\mathfrak{su}(10)\\
  (2,4)&195&\mathfrak{su}(14)
 \end{array}
\]
This is a finite audit only; Proposition
~\ref{prop:saturated-carrier-full-su} is the proof for all $\ell$ and $k$.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Shrinking-window carrier lift in full operator norm]
\label{thm:shrinking-window-carrier-lift}
Retain~\eqref{eq:power-two-saturated-data}, let
$q_1,\ldots,q_m$ be a real basis of $\mathcal Q_J$, and fix
$c>0$, $R<\infty$, and
\[
 P\in\mathrm{SU}(\mathcal G_{\ell,L}).
\]
For every $\varepsilon>0$, $B>0$, and $\zeta>0$, there are
$T<\infty$, $\lambda>0$, and smooth real controls
$u_{1,\lambda},\ldots,u_{m,\lambda}$ supported in
$[0,T/\lambda]$ such that the full propagator
\begin{equation}
 i\dot U_s(t)=
 s\left(A_3+\sum_{r=1}^m
 u_{r,\lambda}(t)M_{q_r}\right)U_s(t),
 \qquad U_s(0)=\Id,
 \label{eq:shrinking-carrier-physical-system}
\end{equation}
satisfies, on
\begin{equation}
 S_\lambda=
 \left[c-\frac{\lambda R}{N},
       c+\frac{\lambda R}{N}\right],
 \label{eq:shrinking-carrier-window}
\end{equation}
the estimate
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{s\in S_\lambda}
 \left\|
 e^{is(T/\lambda)A_3}U_s(T/\lambda)
 -\bigl(P\oplus\Id_{\mathcal G_{\ell,L}^{\perp}}\bigr)
 \right\|<\varepsilon .
 \label{eq:shrinking-carrier-full-operator-gate}
\end{equation}
If
\[
 Q_\lambda(t,\omega)=
 \sum_{r=1}^mu_{r,\lambda}(t)q_r(\omega),
\]
the controls may simultaneously be chosen so that
\begin{align}
 \sup_t\|Q_\lambda(t,\cdot)\|_\infty&\leq B,
 \label{eq:shrinking-carrier-height}\\
 \int_0^{T/\lambda}\|Q_\lambda(t,\cdot)\|_\infty^2\,\dd t&\leq\zeta,
 \label{eq:shrinking-carrier-l2-cost}\\
 \int_0^{T/\lambda}\|Q_\lambda(t,\cdot)\|_\infty\,\dd t&\leq C_P<\infty,
 \label{eq:shrinking-carrier-l1-budget}
\end{align}
where the finite $L^1$ budget is fixed before any outgoing Galerkin cutoff.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For smooth real slow-time envelopes $x_r,y_r$ supported in $[0,T]$, set
\begin{equation}
 u_{r,\lambda}(t)=
 \frac{2\lambda}{c}
 \left\{
 x_r(\lambda t)\cos(cNt)
 -y_r(\lambda t)\sin(cNt)
 \right\}.
 \label{eq:shrinking-carrier-control}
\end{equation}
Write $s=c+\lambda\nu/N$, so $|\nu|\leq R$, and pass to slow time
$\tau=\lambda t$ and the interaction picture
\[
 V_{\lambda,\nu}(\tau)=
 e^{is(\tau/\lambda)A_3}U_s(\tau/\lambda).
\]
The resonant part of its Hamiltonian is, up to the factor
$s/c=1+O(\lambda)$,
\begin{equation}
 K_\nu(\tau)=
 \sum_{r=1}^m
 \left\{
 (x_r(\tau)-iy_r(\tau))e^{i\nu\tau}C_{q_r}
 +(x_r(\tau)+iy_r(\tau))e^{-i\nu\tau}C_{q_r}^\ast
 \right\}.
 \label{eq:shrinking-carrier-resonant-system}
\end{equation}

We first choose the envelopes.  By Proposition
~\ref{prop:saturated-carrier-full-su}, a finite product of exponentials of
the constant Hamiltonians~\eqref{eq:saturated-carrier-quadratures} equals
$P$.  Multiply each Hamiltonian in this word by $M$ and divide the length
of its time interval by $M$.  The control-only endpoint is unchanged,
whereas the total slow time tends to zero.  Uniformly for $|\nu|\leq R$,
the factors $e^{\pm i\nu\tau}$ in
\eqref{eq:shrinking-carrier-resonant-system} then tend to one throughout the
word.  Thus, for $M$ sufficiently large, the endpoint of
\eqref{eq:shrinking-carrier-resonant-system} is within
$\varepsilon/3$ of $P$, uniformly in $\nu$.  Smoothing the finitely many
junctions changes the endpoint by an arbitrarily small amount.  Notice that
this strong-pulse compression increases the slow-time peak but leaves
\[
 \int_0^T\sum_r(|x_r|+|y_r|)\,\dd\tau
 \]
bounded by the finite word length.

It remains to justify the full-sphere lift.  Decompose each pure-degree
multiplier into its finitely many shell shifts as in the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:isolated-shell-bandpass-filter}.  On the block
$\mathcal H_a\to\mathcal H_b$, every nonresonant term in slow time has a
phase
\[
 \exp\!\left(
  i\frac{s(\lambda_b-\lambda_a)\pm cN}{\lambda}\tau
 \right).
\]
After decreasing $\lambda$, the window
\eqref{eq:shrinking-carrier-window} satisfies
\eqref{eq:saturated-carrier-isolation-window}.  Proposition
~\ref{prop:power-two-saturated-gap} therefore gives a common nonzero lower
bound for every off-target carrier mismatch.  Integration by parts for the
smooth envelopes, together with the orthogonality of the domain and range
blocks for each fixed shell shift, gives
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{\substack{|\nu|\leq R\\0\leq\tau\leq T}}
 \left\|
  \int_0^\tau\mathcal R_{\lambda,\nu}(r)\,\dd r
 \right\|
 \leq C\lambda ,
 \label{eq:shrinking-carrier-remainder-primitive}
\end{equation}
where $\mathcal R_{\lambda,\nu}$ is the sum of all nonresonant terms.
The constant is finite because the number of spatial profiles and shell
shifts is finite; it may depend on their smooth variation, but not on
$\lambda$ or $\nu$.

For completeness, if
$\mathcal Q(\tau)=\int_0^\tau\mathcal R_{\lambda,\nu}(r)\,\dd r$, the
Duhamel integral between the resonant and full propagators may be integrated
by parts.  Unitarity and the differential equations bound the endpoint
error by
\[
 \sup_\tau\|\mathcal Q(\tau)\|
 \left[
  2+\int_0^T
  \bigl(2\|K_\nu(\tau)\|+\|\mathcal R_{\lambda,\nu}(\tau)\|\bigr)
  \,\dd\tau
 \right].
\]
The bracket is finite and independent of $\lambda$.  Equation
\eqref{eq:shrinking-carrier-remainder-primitive}, and the
$O(\lambda)$ error from $s/c-1$, therefore make the full interaction
endpoint uniformly $O(\lambda)$-close to the ideal endpoint.  Choosing
$\lambda$ after the strong-pulse word proves
\eqref{eq:shrinking-carrier-full-operator-gate}.

Finally put
\[
 F(\tau)=\sum_r
 (|x_r(\tau)|+|y_r(\tau)|)\|q_r\|_\infty .
\]
Equation~\eqref{eq:shrinking-carrier-control} gives
\begin{align*}
 \sup_t\|Q_\lambda(t)\|_\infty
 &\leq\frac{2\lambda}{c}\|F\|_\infty,\\
 \int_0^{T/\lambda}\|Q_\lambda(t)\|_\infty\,\dd t
 &\leq\frac2c\int_0^TF(\tau)\,\dd\tau,\\
 \int_0^{T/\lambda}\|Q_\lambda(t)\|_\infty^2\,\dd t
 &\leq\frac{4\lambda}{c^2}\int_0^TF(\tau)^2\,\dd\tau.
 \end{align*}
After the envelopes are fixed, a further decrease of $\lambda$ proves
\eqref{eq:shrinking-carrier-height}--%
\eqref{eq:shrinking-carrier-l1-budget}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Local high-gap population swaps are physical]
\label{cor:local-high-gap-physical-swaps}
Let $\mathcal E$ be a finite $A_3$-invariant space with an orthonormal
eigenbasis $e_1,\ldots,e_d$, and fix $c>0$.  For every
$\delta,B,\zeta>0$ there are mutually orthogonal auxiliary eigenvectors
$f_1,\ldots,f_d\perp\mathcal E$, a nonempty interval
$S\ni c$, and physical scalar-control words $\mathcal S_{j,s}$ such that:
\begin{enumerate}
\item their gaps satisfy
\[
 \Delta_j=\mu_j-\lambda_j>d\lambda_\ast;
\]
\item for an $\mathrm{SU}(2)$ population inversion $R_j$ on
$\operatorname{span}\{e_j,f_j\}$ which fixes its orthogonal complement,
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{s\in S}
 \|(\mathcal S_{j,s}-e^{-isT_jA_3}R_j)|_{\mathcal F}\|<\delta,
 \qquad
 \mathcal F=\operatorname{span}\{e_1,\ldots,e_d,f_1,\ldots,f_d\};
 \label{eq:local-high-gap-drift-swap}
 \end{equation}
\item the pointwise control height is at most $B$, the total quadratic
control cost is at most $\zeta$, and every word has a finite $L^1$ budget
fixed before the safety and outgoing cutoffs.
\end{enumerate}
Consequently the hypothesis of Proposition
~\ref{prop:population-swap-phase-cancellation} holds on some nonempty
window about every prescribed centre $c$.  On that window, positive flights
and scalar masks synthesize the inverse Talbot drift on $\mathcal E$, up to
arbitrary error and scalar phase.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Write $e_j\in\mathcal H_{\ell_j}$.  Choose distinct powers $2^{k_j}$ so
large that, with $L_j=\ell_j+2^{k_j}$,
\[
 N_j=\lambda_{L_j}-\lambda_{\ell_j}>d\lambda_\ast,
\]
and choose $f_j\in\mathcal H_{L_j}$.  Distinct upper shells make the
$f_j$ orthogonal.  On
$\mathcal H_{\ell_j}\oplus\mathcal H_{L_j}$ choose a determinant-one
population swap sending $e_j$ to $f_j$ and $f_j$ to $-e_j$, while fixing
the remaining vectors.  Apply Theorem
~\ref{thm:shrinking-window-carrier-lift} to this target.  It gives
\eqref{eq:local-high-gap-drift-swap} on a nonempty window centred at $c$,
with full-operator rather than projected leakage control.  Restrict all
finitely many constructions to the intersection of their windows and
allocate the height and cost budgets among them.  Each smooth controlled
interval may be approximated, uniformly for $s\in S$ on the finite smooth
space $\mathcal F$, by Lie--Trotter products of positive free flights and
signed scalar masks.  Thus the controls have the word form required by
Proposition~\ref{prop:broadband-transposition-criterion}.  Proposition
~\ref{prop:population-swap-phase-cancellation}, followed by Proposition
~\ref{prop:broadband-transposition-criterion}, gives the inverse.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[What the carrier theorem does and does not close]
\label{rem:carrier-local-not-global}
Theorem~\ref{thm:shrinking-window-carrier-lift} is a one-shot nonlinear
gate, not a delayed concatenation of first-order filters.  It therefore does
not contradict Proposition~\ref{prop:causal-filter-cost}: the whole Lie word
is compressed into one fixed slow-time interval before $\lambda$ is sent to
zero.  Its long physical free-flight time costs no potential norm, while
\eqref{eq:shrinking-carrier-l2-cost} makes the weak-mask cost arbitrarily
small.  Lemmas~\ref{lem:scale-compatibility} and~\ref{lem:window-cost} may
therefore be applied after the finite word has been fixed.

The theorem closes the previously missing \emph{local} selective-pulse lift.
It does not give a uniform lower bound on the window
$|S_\lambda|=2\lambda R/N$.  Hence it does not by itself prove the adaptive
hazard divergence~\eqref{eq:hazard-divergence} or the fixed-prescribed-band
Problem~\ref{prob:lens-assisted-inverse}.  The remaining obstruction is
global chromatic coverage, not physical realization of one local
drift-decorated swap.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Dipole carriers separate every finite adjacent-line family]
\label{prop:dipole-direct-sum-carrier-algebra}
For $a\geq0$, put
\[
 \mathcal G_a=\mathcal H_a\oplus\mathcal H_{a+1},
 \qquad
 C_{a,q}=P_{\mathcal H_{a+1}}M_qP_{\mathcal H_a},
 \qquad q\in\mathcal Q_1,
\]
and
\[
 H_{a,q,\theta}
 =e^{i\theta}C_{a,q}+e^{-i\theta}C_{a,q}^\ast.
\]
Then
\begin{equation}
 \operatorname{Lie}_{\mathbb R}
 \{\,iH_{a,q,\theta}:q\in\mathcal Q_1,\ \theta\in\mathbb R\,\}
 =
 \mathfrak{su}(\mathcal G_a)
 \cong\mathfrak{su}(4a+4).
 \label{eq:dipole-pair-full-su}
\end{equation}

More generally, let $\mathcal A\subset\mathbb N_0$ be finite and let
$\alpha_a\neq0$.  One common dipole control has the direct-sum algebra
\begin{equation}
 \operatorname{Lie}_{\mathbb R}
 \left\{
  i\bigoplus_{a\in\mathcal A}
