  \alpha_aH_{a,q,\theta}:
  q\in\mathcal Q_1,\ \theta\in\mathbb R
 \right\}
 =
 \bigoplus_{a\in\mathcal A}\mathfrak{su}(\mathcal G_a).
 \label{eq:dipole-finite-line-separation}
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Complexify and use the standard spherical components
$q=-1,0,1$ of the degree-one tensor.  In magnetic bases,
\begin{equation}
 C_{a,q}|a,m\rangle=c_{m,q}|a+1,m+q\rangle,
 \label{eq:dipole-clebsch-graph}
\end{equation}
where, up to one positive factor depending only on $a$,
\begin{align}
 |c_{m,1}|^2&=(a+m+1)(a+m+2),\notag\\
 |c_{m,0}|^2&=2\bigl((a+1)^2-m^2\bigr),                 \label{eq:dipole-clebsch-weights}\\
 |c_{m,-1}|^2&=(a-m+1)(a-m+2).\notag
\end{align}
Every displayed coefficient is nonzero on its allowed edge.  The three
diagonal commutators
\[
 D_q=[C_{a,q},C_{a,q}^\ast]
\]
belong to the complexified algebra.  For an edge
$e=(m,q)$ in~\eqref{eq:dipole-clebsch-graph}, form its three-component
signature
\[
 d_e^{(r)}
 =D_r(a+1,m+q)-D_r(a,m),
 \qquad r=-1,0,1,
 \qquad
 \mathbf d_e=(d_e^{(-1)},d_e^{(0)},d_e^{(1)}).
\]
Substitution of~\eqref{eq:dipole-clebsch-weights} shows that the signatures
are pairwise distinct.  Choose a real linear combination
$D=\gamma_{-1}D_{-1}+\gamma_0D_0+\gamma_1D_1$ outside the finitely many
hyperplanes
$\gamma\cdot(\mathbf d_e-\mathbf d_{e'})=0$.  The eigenvalue differences of
$\operatorname{ad}_D$ on all edges are then distinct.
For each fixed $q$, the vectors
\[
 C_{a,q},\ \operatorname{ad}_D C_{a,q},\
 \ldots,\
 \operatorname{ad}_D^{\,2a}C_{a,q}
\]
give a Vandermonde system and isolate every individual matrix unit on the
$q$-edge set.  Their adjoints are also present.  The bipartite graph in
\eqref{eq:dipole-clebsch-graph}, using $q=-1,0,1$, is connected.  Brackets
along paths therefore give every off-diagonal matrix unit, and brackets with
adjoints give every traceless diagonal matrix.  The complexified algebra is
$\mathfrak{sl}(4a+4,\mathbb C)$, proving
\eqref{eq:dipole-pair-full-su}.

For~\eqref{eq:dipole-finite-line-separation}, the projection of the generated
algebra onto each factor is surjective by the first part.  The factors
$\mathfrak{su}(4a+4)$ are simple and pairwise nonisomorphic.  The Lie-algebra
Goursat lemma therefore rules out a diagonal identification between two
factors; induction over the finite set $\mathcal A$ gives the full direct
sum.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Ideal dipole profiles on finitely many lines are reachable]
\label{cor:ideal-dipole-multiline-profiles}
Let $\mathcal A\subset\mathbb N_0$ be finite, let $R<\infty$, and let
$\alpha_a\neq0$.  On
\[
 \prod_{a\in\mathcal A}\mathrm{SU}(\mathcal G_a)
\]
consider the common-control ensemble whose $a$th component has bounded
detuning drift $\nu Z_a$, $|\nu|\leq R$, and dipole carrier quadratures
$\alpha_aH_{a,q,\theta}$.  Every continuous target family
\[
 \nu\longmapsto
 \bigl(G_a(\nu)\bigr)_{a\in\mathcal A}
 \in
 \prod_{a\in\mathcal A}\mathrm{SU}(\mathcal G_a)
\]
belongs to the uniform reachable closure under one finite piecewise-constant
common control.  The control has finite amplitude and finite $L^1$ budget,
although no bound uniform in $(R,G,\varepsilon)$ is asserted.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Proposition~\ref{prop:dipole-direct-sum-carrier-algebra} supplies the
control-only direct sum of the simple factors.  Haar convexification in each
factor puts the negative traceless detuning drift in the uniform reachable
closure using positive time.  Bracketing independently supported factor
generators with $\nu Z_a$ gives every monomial
$\nu^kX_a$, $X_a\in\mathfrak{su}(\mathcal G_a)$.  Polynomial approximation
on $[-R,R]$, followed by the standard Lie-product construction, gives every
continuous Lie-algebra profile.  Finite products of local exponential charts
cover the compact target image and give the asserted group-valued profile.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Dipole parity postpones off-line leakage to third order]
\label{prop:dipole-second-order-parity}
Put $\lambda_j=j(j+1)$ and $\Delta_a=2(a+1)$.  Fix a dipole carrier
$\omega>0$ and one of its resonance centres
\[
 s_a=\frac{\omega}{\Delta_a}.
\]
In the interaction picture, every elementary degree-one carrier term maps
$\mathcal H_j$ to $\mathcal H_{j+\delta}$, $\delta\in\{-1,1\}$, and has a
phase with frequency
\begin{equation}
 \Gamma_{j,\delta,\sigma}(s)
 =s(\lambda_{j+\delta}-\lambda_j)+\sigma\omega,
 \qquad \sigma\in\{-1,1\}.
 \label{eq:dipole-elementary-carrier-frequency}
\end{equation}
At $s=s_a$, every resonant product of two such terms preserves the spherical
shell.  Equivalently, if $\mathcal N_{2,a}$ denotes the resonant quadratic
mean produced by the first near-identity averaging step, then the order-one
effective Hamiltonian in the slow-scale normal form commutes with $A_3$:
\begin{equation}
 [A_3,\mathcal N_{2,a}(\tau)]=0.
 \label{eq:dipole-quadratic-normal-form-diagonal}
\end{equation}
The assertion is uniform on a sufficiently small scaled-detuning tube about
each member of any fixed finite family of resonance lines.

This statement is sharp in order.  If $a\geq1$, the three-step path
\[
 \mathcal H_{a-1}\longrightarrow\mathcal H_a
 \longrightarrow\mathcal H_{a+1}\longrightarrow\mathcal H_{a+2}
\]
has the exact three-photon resonance
\begin{equation}
 \lambda_{a+2}-\lambda_{a-1}
 =6(a+1)=3\Delta_a,
 \label{eq:dipole-three-photon-resonance}
\end{equation}
so a nonadjacent resonant term may first occur at order two in the slow-scale
effective Hamiltonian.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For a two-step path
$j_0\to j_1\to j_2$, multiplication of the two interaction-picture
phases telescopes the spectral part.  Its total frequency is
\[
 s(\lambda_{j_2}-\lambda_{j_0})
 +(\sigma_1+\sigma_2)\omega .
\]
At $s=s_a$, vanishing of this frequency is equivalent to
\begin{equation}
 \lambda_{j_2}-\lambda_{j_0}
 +(\sigma_1+\sigma_2)\Delta_a=0.
 \label{eq:dipole-two-step-resonance-equation}
\end{equation}
Here $j_2-j_0\in\{-2,0,2\}$ and
$\sigma_1+\sigma_2\in\{-2,0,2\}$.  If $j_2=j_0$, the equation may hold,
and the resulting operator preserves that shell.  If $j_2=j_0+2$, then
\[
 \lambda_{j_0+2}-\lambda_{j_0}=4j_0+6\equiv2\pmod4,
\]
whereas every nonzero value of
$(\sigma_1+\sigma_2)\Delta_a$ is divisible by four.  Thus
\eqref{eq:dipole-two-step-resonance-equation} is impossible.  The case
$j_2=j_0-2$ is identical after reversing the path.  This proves
\eqref{eq:dipole-quadratic-normal-form-diagonal}.

The nonzero frequency gaps just used are separated from zero.  Continuity in
$s$, followed by a finite minimum over any prescribed finite resonance family,
gives the asserted uniform tube.  For smooth envelopes supported away from
the slow-time endpoints, the standard near-identity integration by parts has
no endpoint kick.  Its quadratic mean is precisely the sum of the resonant
two-step products classified above, so it enters with one power of the slow
scale and is shell preserving.

Finally,
\[
 \lambda_{a+2}-\lambda_{a-1}
 =(a+2)(a+3)-(a-1)a=6(a+1).
\]
Taking all three carrier signs opposite to the positive shell shifts makes
the total frequency zero at $s=s_a$.  Three interactions contribute at order
two after slow-time rescaling, proving
\eqref{eq:dipole-three-photon-resonance}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Uniform first dipole normal form on a finite safety space]
\label{prop:dipole-first-normal-form}
Fix one resonance line $a$, a compact scaled-detuning interval
$|\nu|\leq R$, and smooth dipole-carrier envelopes supported in the interior
of a slow-time interval $[0,T]$.  Let $U_\nu$ be the corresponding ideal
resonant propagator on $\mathcal G_a$, extended by the identity, and let
$V_{\lambda,\nu}$ be the exact full-sphere interaction propagator with
\[
 s=s_a+\frac{\lambda\nu}{\Delta_a}.
\]
If $\mathcal F$ is any fixed finite sum of spherical shells containing
$\mathcal G_a$, then there are continuous bounded self-adjoint families
$D_{\mathrm{res},\nu}(\tau)$ and
$D_{\mathrm{St},\nu}(\tau)$ such that
\begin{align}
 D_{\mathrm{res},\nu}(\tau)
 &=P_{\mathcal G_a}D_{\mathrm{res},\nu}(\tau)P_{\mathcal G_a},
 \label{eq:dipole-resonant-first-correction}\\
 [A_3,D_{\mathrm{St},\nu}(\tau)]&=0,
 \label{eq:dipole-stark-first-correction}
\end{align}
and, with
\begin{equation}
 E_\nu=
 \int_0^T U_\nu(\tau)^*
 \{D_{\mathrm{res},\nu}(\tau)+D_{\mathrm{St},\nu}(\tau)\}
 U_\nu(\tau)\,\dd\tau ,
 \label{eq:dipole-endpoint-first-correction}
\end{equation}
one has
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{|\nu|\leq R}
 \left\|
 \left.
 \left\{
 V_{\lambda,\nu}(T)
 -U_\nu(T)(\Id-i\lambda E_\nu)
 \right\}
 \right|_{\mathcal F}
 \right\|
 \leq C_{\mathcal F,R}\lambda^2
 \label{eq:dipole-first-normal-form-endpoint}
\end{equation}
for all sufficiently small $\lambda>0$.
In particular $E_\nu\mathcal F\subseteq\mathcal F$: the entire
$O(\lambda)$ endpoint defect is a finite-dimensional phase/frame error on
the prescribed safety space, not leakage out of it.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Write every degree-one multiplier as the sum of its raising and lowering
shell shifts.  In slow time, the exact interaction Hamiltonian is the
resonant Hamiltonian $K_\nu$ plus a sum of off-resonant terms
\begin{equation}
 R_{\lambda,\nu}(\tau)
 =\sum_\gamma e^{i\gamma(s)\tau/\lambda}B_{\gamma,\nu}(\tau),
 \label{eq:dipole-fast-remainder-decomposition}
\end{equation}
where the frequencies and blocks are those in
\eqref{eq:dipole-elementary-carrier-frequency}.  On the fixed detuning
compactum, every frequency retained in
\eqref{eq:dipole-fast-remainder-decomposition} is separated from zero.
For each shell shift the domain blocks and range blocks are mutually
orthogonal.  Hence division by $\gamma(s)$ and differentiation of the smooth
envelopes define bounded banded operators uniformly in $\lambda$ and $\nu$;
the infinite shell sum costs only the finite number of shifts.

Set
\[
 S_{\lambda,\nu}(\tau)
 =\lambda\sum_\gamma
 \frac{e^{i\gamma(s)\tau/\lambda}}{i\gamma(s)}
 B_{\gamma,\nu}(\tau)
\]
and combine adjoint frequencies so that $S_{\lambda,\nu}$ is self-adjoint.
The envelopes are supported in $(0,T)$, so
$S_{\lambda,\nu}(0)=S_{\lambda,\nu}(T)=0$.  Conjugating by
$e^{-iS_{\lambda,\nu}}$ removes
$R_{\lambda,\nu}$ to first order.  The resulting Hamiltonian is
\[
 K_\nu
 +\lambda D_{\mathrm{res},\nu}
 +\lambda D_{\mathrm{St},\nu}
 +\lambda R^{(1)}_{\lambda,\nu}
 +O(\lambda^2).
\]
Here $D_{\mathrm{res},\nu}$ contains the explicit
$s/s_a-1$ correction to the resonant control and is supported on
$\mathcal G_a$.  The mean quadratic term is
$D_{\mathrm{St},\nu}$.  Proposition
~\ref{prop:dipole-second-order-parity} shows that every one of its resonant
two-step products preserves a shell, proving
\eqref{eq:dipole-stark-first-correction}.  The remaining
$R^{(1)}_{\lambda,\nu}$ has zero fast mean.  A second integration by parts,
again with a near-identity transformation equal to the identity at both
endpoints, removes it with an $O(\lambda^2)$ remainder.

All operators in these two transformations have only finitely many shell
shifts and are uniformly bounded on the stated detuning compactum.  Duhamel's
formula therefore gives the expansion
\eqref{eq:dipole-first-normal-form-endpoint}, with the first variation around
$U_\nu$ equal to \eqref{eq:dipole-endpoint-first-correction}.
Equations~\eqref{eq:dipole-resonant-first-correction} and
\eqref{eq:dipole-stark-first-correction} show that conjugation by $U_\nu$
cannot take a vector in $\mathcal F$ out of $\mathcal F$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[The first dipole defect is locally correctable]
\label{prop:dipole-first-defect-correctable}
Retain the hypotheses of Proposition
~\ref{prop:dipole-first-normal-form}, and enlarge the finite safety space if
necessary so that
\[
 \mathcal F_L=\bigoplus_{j=0}^{L}\mathcal H_j
\]
for some $L\geq a+1$.  On every fixed compact detuning interval
$|\nu|\leq R$, there is a sequence $\lambda_n\downarrow0$ and finite
additional dipole-carrier words, using the adjacent carrier frequencies
$s_a\Delta_j$, $0\leq j<L$, such that the corrected exact propagators satisfy
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{|\nu|\leq R}
 \left\|
 \left.
 \left\{
 V^{\mathrm{corr}}_{\lambda_n,\nu}
 -\chi_n(\nu)U_\nu(T)
 \right\}
 \right|_{\mathcal F_L}
 \right\|
 =o(\lambda_n)
 \label{eq:dipole-locally-corrected-endpoint}
\end{equation}
for continuous scalar phases $\chi_n(\nu)\in\mathbb T$.
The correction word has slow-time $L^1$ size $o(1)$; consequently its
physical height is bounded and its additional physical $L^1$ budget tends
to zero after the carrier pullback.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The overlapping adjacent-pair algebras generate the full algebra on the
finite shell chain:
\begin{equation}
 \operatorname{Lie}\{
 \mathfrak{su}(\mathcal G_0),\ldots,
 \mathfrak{su}(\mathcal G_{L-1})\}
 =\mathfrak{su}(\mathcal F_L).
 \label{eq:overlapping-dipole-chain-full-su}
\end{equation}
Indeed, every factor supplies all matrix units within
$\mathcal H_j\oplus\mathcal H_{j+1}$, and brackets along the connected shell
chain give every off-diagonal matrix unit on $\mathcal F_L$; brackets with
adjoints give the traceless diagonal algebra.

For the carrier centred at $s_a\Delta_j$, the scaled detuning on the same
energy tube is a fixed nonzero multiple of $\nu$.  Applying the polynomial
Lie-extension argument of Corollary
~\ref{cor:ideal-dipole-multiline-profiles} to
\eqref{eq:overlapping-dipole-chain-full-su} therefore gives every continuous
profile in $C([-R,R];\mathfrak{su}(\mathcal F_L))$ in the uniform Lie
closure.  In particular it gives the traceless part
\[
 E_\nu^\circ
 =E_\nu-\frac{\operatorname{tr}_{\mathcal F_L}E_\nu}
 {\dim\mathcal F_L}\Id_{\mathcal F_L}
\]
of the continuous endpoint defect in
\eqref{eq:dipole-endpoint-first-correction}.

To retain the small scale, first approximate $E_\nu^\circ$ uniformly by one
finite Lie polynomial in the available ensemble generators.  Let $r$ be its
largest bracket depth.  The standard commutator product formulas synthesize
\[
 \exp(i\lambda E_\nu^\circ)
\]
with elementary slow-control size $O(\lambda^{1/r})$ and total slow
$L^1$ size $O(\lambda^{1/r})$; lower-depth monomials use the corresponding
powers $\lambda^{1/d}$.  Subdivision makes the finite Lie-product error
$o(\lambda)$.  The carrier integration-by-parts estimate used in Theorem
~\ref{thm:shrinking-window-carrier-lift} now has a remainder bounded by
$\lambda$ times this vanishing slow-control size.  Thus the exact physical
lift of the correction differs from its ideal endpoint by
$o(\lambda)$ on $\mathcal F_L$.

Choose successively finer Lie-polynomial approximations to
$E_\nu^\circ$, and after each finite word has been fixed choose
$\lambda_n$ small enough for the Lie-product and physical-lift errors to be
$o(\lambda_n)$.  Concatenating the correction with
\eqref{eq:dipole-first-normal-form-endpoint} cancels
$-i\lambda_nE_\nu^\circ$.  The trace part is the continuous scalar phase
$\chi_n$, proving \eqref{eq:dipole-locally-corrected-endpoint}.  The stated
budget assertions follow from the $O(\lambda_n^{1/r})$ slow-control size and
the carrier pullback.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[What the parity calculation settles]
\label{rem:dipole-stark-only-first-defect}
The $O(\lambda)$ bound in Theorem
~\ref{thm:shrinking-window-carrier-lift} is not, by itself, a leakage lower
bound for a dipole carrier.  Proposition
~\ref{prop:dipole-second-order-parity} shows that its resonant first correction
is an AC--Stark-type operator which preserves every spherical shell.  On a
fixed finite safety space it is therefore a finite-dimensional phase/frame
defect; leakage to a nonadjacent shell begins at $O(\lambda^2)$.

Proposition~\ref{prop:dipole-first-defect-correctable} cancels this defect on
every fixed safety space and fixed detuning compactum.  What it does not
control is the compactified tail as the scaled detuning grows like
$\lambda^{-1}$ across a prescribed energy band.  The next proposition
decides whether that tail can be controlled by absolute-error patching.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Every nontrivial dipole pulse has a nonsummable Stark tail]
\label{prop:dipole-unavoidable-one-over-nu-tail}
Let $\mathcal H_-$ and $\mathcal H_+$ be finite-dimensional, let
$C\in C_c^\infty((0,T);
\operatorname{Hom}(\mathcal H_-,\mathcal H_+))$, and put
\[
 B(\tau)=
 \begin{pmatrix}0&0\\ C(\tau)&0\end{pmatrix}
 \quad\hbox{on }\mathcal H_-\oplus\mathcal H_+.
\]
Let $W_\nu$ solve
\begin{equation}
 i\dot W_\nu(\tau)
 =
 \{e^{i\nu\tau}B(\tau)+e^{-i\nu\tau}B(\tau)^*\}W_\nu(\tau),
 \qquad W_\nu(0)=\Id.
 \label{eq:far-detuned-dipole-system}
\end{equation}
Then, as $|\nu|\to\infty$,
\begin{equation}
 W_\nu(T)
 =
 \Id-\frac{i}{\nu}\mathcal S_C+O(\nu^{-2}),
 \qquad
 \mathcal S_C=
 \int_0^T
 \begin{pmatrix}
 C(\tau)^*C(\tau)&0\\
 0&-C(\tau)C(\tau)^*
 \end{pmatrix}\dd\tau .
 \label{eq:unavoidable-stark-tail}
\end{equation}
The remainder is uniform on bounded subsets of
$C_c^\infty((0,T))$.  If $C$ is not identically zero, then
$\mathcal S_C\neq0$ and
\begin{equation}
 \|W_\nu(T)-\Id\|
 =\frac{\|\mathcal S_C\|}{|\nu|}+O(\nu^{-2}).
 \label{eq:sharp-one-over-detuning-tail}
\end{equation}
Consequently, for every $h>0$ and every nonzero such pulse,
\begin{equation}
 \sum_{k\in\mathbb Z\setminus\{0\}}
 \|W_{kh}(T)-\Id\|=\infty .
 \label{eq:dipole-tail-not-ell-one}
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Set
\[
 S_\nu(\tau)=\frac1{i\nu}
 \{e^{i\nu\tau}B(\tau)-e^{-i\nu\tau}B(\tau)^*\}.
\]
It is self-adjoint, $S_\nu(0)=S_\nu(T)=0$, and
\[
 \dot S_\nu
 =e^{i\nu\tau}B+e^{-i\nu\tau}B^*
 +O(\nu^{-1})
\]
with the last term oscillatory and containing $B'$.  Conjugating
\eqref{eq:far-detuned-dipole-system} by $e^{-iS_\nu}$ cancels its order-one
oscillation.  The quadratic mean in the transformed Hamiltonian is
\[
 \frac1{\nu}[B(\tau)^*,B(\tau)]
 =
 \frac1{\nu}
 \begin{pmatrix}
 C(\tau)^*C(\tau)&0\\
 0&-C(\tau)C(\tau)^*
 \end{pmatrix}.
\]
All remaining order-$\nu^{-1}$ terms have zero fast mean.  A second
integration by parts removes them with an $O(\nu^{-2})$ endpoint error;
there are no endpoint kicks because every derivative of $B$ vanishes near
$0$ and $T$.  Duhamel's formula gives
\eqref{eq:unavoidable-stark-tail}.

If $C\not\equiv0$, then
\[
 \int_0^T C(\tau)^*C(\tau)\,\dd\tau
\]
is a nonzero positive operator, so $\mathcal S_C\neq0$.
Equation~\eqref{eq:sharp-one-over-detuning-tail} follows from the norm
expansion, and comparison with the harmonic series proves
\eqref{eq:dipole-tail-not-ell-one}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[The absolute-error multiband patch is impossible]
\label{cor:absolute-multiband-tail-no-go}
No nontrivial family of isolated dipole population-transfer pulses can close
the fixed-band problem by assigning each pulse an absolutely summable
operator-norm tail and then telescoping the errors over an
$O(\lambda^{-1})$ window cover.  Any successful fixed-band construction must
instead cancel the signed $1/\nu$ Stark terms collectively between different
carriers, or use a genuinely one-shot broadband mechanism.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proposition}[Unconstrained matrix inverse scattering is not the
physical dipole inverse]
\label{prop:matrix-zs-dipole-mismatch}
For one adjacent line put
\[
 \mathcal D_a
 =\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb C}
   \{C_{a,-1},C_{a,0},C_{a,1}\}
 \subset
 \operatorname{Hom}(\mathcal H_a,\mathcal H_{a+1}).
\]
Then
\begin{equation}
 \dim_{\mathbb C}\mathcal D_a=3,
 \qquad
 \dim_{\mathbb C}\operatorname{Hom}
   (\mathcal H_a,\mathcal H_{a+1})
 =(2a+1)(2a+3).
 \label{eq:dipole-zs-dimension-mismatch}
\end{equation}
In particular the inclusion is proper for every $a\geq1$.

More precisely, let $u_q\in C_c^\infty((0,T);\mathbb C)$ and let
$W_{\nu,\epsilon}$ solve
\begin{equation}
 i\dot W_{\nu,\epsilon}
 =\epsilon\left\{
 e^{i\nu t}\sum_{q=-1}^{1}u_q(t)C_{a,q}
 +e^{-i\nu t}\sum_{q=-1}^{1}\overline{u_q(t)}C_{a,q}^*
 \right\}W_{\nu,\epsilon},
 \qquad W_{\nu,\epsilon}(0)=\Id .
 \label{eq:physical-dipole-zs-line}
\end{equation}
Uniformly on every compact $\nu$-set,
\begin{equation}
 P_{\mathcal H_{a+1}}
 \frac{W_{\nu,\epsilon}(T)-\Id}{\epsilon}
 P_{\mathcal H_a}
 =
 -i\sum_{q=-1}^{1}\widehat u_q(\nu)C_{a,q}
 +O(\epsilon).
 \label{eq:dipole-scattering-first-born-range}
\end{equation}
Consequently the differential at the zero pulse of the physical scattering
map has its off-diagonal range pointwise inside $\mathcal D_a$.  If
$a\geq1$, $D\notin\mathcal D_a$, and
$0\neq\phi\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R)$, the admissible infinitesimal matrix
reflection profile
\begin{equation}
 R_\epsilon(\nu)=\epsilon\phi(\nu)D+O(\epsilon^2)
 \label{eq:unconstrained-matrix-reflection-direction}
\end{equation}
cannot be the image, to first order, of any physical dipole potential.
Thus no differentiable local right inverse for arbitrary matrix scattering
data can take values in the physical dipole-control class.

For a finite family $\mathcal A$, the obstruction is stronger.  A common
physical control has
\begin{equation}
 Q_a(t)=\alpha_a\sum_{q=-1}^{1}u_q(t)C_{a,q},
 \qquad a\in\mathcal A,
 \label{eq:simultaneous-clebsch-potential-constraint}
\end{equation}
so the same three Fourier coefficients occur in every factor.  Independent
matrix reflection data on the different lines are therefore not independent
physical inputs.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The map
\[
 \mathcal Q_1\longrightarrow
 \operatorname{Hom}(\mathcal H_a,\mathcal H_{a+1}),
 \qquad q\longmapsto C_{a,q},
\]
is a nonzero equivariant map from the irreducible three-dimensional
$\mathrm{SO}(3)$ module $\mathcal Q_1$.  Its kernel is invariant and hence
zero.  This proves the first equality in
\eqref{eq:dipole-zs-dimension-mismatch}; the second follows from
$\dim\mathcal H_j=2j+1$.

Duhamel's formula applied to
\eqref{eq:physical-dipole-zs-line} gives
\[
 W_{\nu,\epsilon}(T)
 =\Id-i\epsilon\int_0^T
 \left\{
 e^{i\nu t}\sum_q u_q(t)C_{a,q}
 +e^{-i\nu t}\sum_q\overline{u_q(t)}C_{a,q}^*
 \right\}\dd t
 +O(\epsilon^2).
\]
The Dyson remainder is uniform when $\nu$ stays in a compact set.  Taking
the upper--lower block proves
\eqref{eq:dipole-scattering-first-born-range}.  Its right-hand side lies in
$\mathcal D_a$ for every $\nu$, whereas
\eqref{eq:unconstrained-matrix-reflection-direction} does not at any point
where $\phi\neq0$.  The last assertion follows by taking the direct sum of
the first-Born formula and observing the common coefficients in
\eqref{eq:simultaneous-clebsch-potential-constraint}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Exact consequence for the one-shot route]
\label{rem:matrix-zs-not-physical-lift}
The classical selective-excitation inverse theorem is a $2\times2$
Zakharov--Shabat theorem with one unrestricted complex potential
\cite{Magland2009}.  Matrix AKNS inverse scattering similarly starts from an
arbitrary matrix potential in its prescribed off-diagonal block class; see,
for example, \cite{VanDerMee2025}.  Proposition
~\ref{prop:matrix-zs-dipole-mismatch} shows that neither theorem may be
applied and then merely ``reinterpreted'' as a physical scalar dipole pulse.
The exception $a=0$ is only one vector line:
$\mathcal D_0=\operatorname{Hom}(\mathcal H_0,\mathcal H_1)$.  It does not
solve the higher-line or simultaneous common-control problem.

Nor can the missing matrix directions be manufactured by a drift-free
control factorization on the full sphere: scalar multiplication operators
commute,
\[
 [M_q,M_r]=0.
\]
The full special-unitary algebras in Proposition
~\ref{prop:dipole-direct-sum-carrier-algebra} arise only after resonant
compression, and their physical lift uses drift-mediated carrier averaging.
Therefore a surviving one-shot theorem must solve the genuinely constrained
simultaneous inverse problem
\eqref{eq:simultaneous-clebsch-potential-constraint}, including the
full-sphere leakage and cost ledgers.  The proposition rules out an
off-the-shelf matrix inverse-scattering closure; it does not rule out such a
new constrained theorem.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Chebyshev carriers cancel a common Stark tail]
\label{prop:chebyshev-stark-cancellation}
Let $S=S^*$ act on a finite-dimensional Hilbert space and suppose
$W:\mathbb R\to\mathrm U(d)$ satisfies, for $|\nu|\geq\nu_0$,
\begin{equation}
 \left\|W(\nu)-\exp\!\left(-\frac{iS}{\nu}\right)\right\|
 \leq\frac{C}{\nu^2}.
 \label{eq:common-stark-log-tail}
\end{equation}
For $N\geq1$ put
\[
 h_N=\frac{\pi}{N},\qquad
 \theta_{j,N}=\left(j-\frac12\right)h_N,\qquad
 x_{j,N}=\cos\theta_{j,N},\qquad 1\leq j\leq N.
\]
Fix $0<\delta<\pi/2$.  For
$\theta\in[\delta,\pi-\delta]$, $x=\cos\theta$, and an integer $M$ satisfying
\begin{equation}
 2\leq M,\qquad Mh_N\leq\delta/2,
 \label{eq:chebyshev-cluster-scale}
\end{equation}
call a node local when
$|\theta_{j,N}-\theta|<(M+1)h_N$ and remote otherwise.  Then
\begin{align}
 \left|
  \frac1N\sum_{\rm remote}\frac1{x-x_{j,N}}
 \right|
 &\leq C_\delta\left(\frac1M+\frac{M}{N}\right),
 \label{eq:truncated-chebyshev-cauchy}\\
 \frac1{N^2}\sum_{\rm remote}
 \frac1{|x-x_{j,N}|^2}
 &\leq\frac{C_\delta}{M}.
 \label{eq:truncated-chebyshev-square}
\end{align}
Consequently, in any fixed order,
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{\theta\in[\delta,\pi-\delta]}
 \left\|
  \prod_{\rm remote}
  W\!\left(N(x-x_{j,N})\right)-\Id
 \right\|
 \leq
 C_{\delta,S,C}
 \left(\frac1M+\frac{M}{N}\right)
 \label{eq:chebyshev-remote-product}
\end{equation}
whenever $M$ is large enough in terms of $(\delta,\nu_0)$.
In particular the remote product converges uniformly to the identity for
every diagonal choice
\[
 M=M_N\longrightarrow\infty,
 \qquad
 M_N/N\longrightarrow0.
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The arcsine measure becomes normalized Lebesgue measure under
$t=\cos\varphi$, and its interior Cauchy transform vanishes:
\begin{equation}
 \operatorname{p.v.}\frac1\pi\int_0^\pi
 \frac{\dd\varphi}{\cos\theta-\cos\varphi}=0,
 \qquad 0<\theta<\pi.
 \label{eq:arcsine-zero-cauchy-transform}
\end{equation}
For completeness, this also follows from
\[
 \frac1{\cos\theta-\cos\varphi}
 =
 -\frac1{2\sin\theta}
 \left\{
  \cot\frac{\theta-\varphi}{2}
  +\cot\frac{\theta+\varphi}{2}
 \right\}.
\]
The first cotangent has zero principal value and the second supplies the
opposite regular contribution.

Remove the symmetric interval
$|\varphi-\theta|<(M+1)h_N$.  On its complement the total variation of
$(\cos\theta-\cos\varphi)^{-1}$ is at most
$C_\delta/(Mh_N)$.  The midpoint quadrature error is therefore at most
$C_\delta/M$.  The principal-value integral over the removed symmetric
interval is $O_\delta(Mh_N)$: its odd
$(\sin\theta(\varphi-\theta))^{-1}$ singularity cancels, and the remaining
part is bounded.  Adjusting the two boundary midpoint cells costs another
$O_\delta(M^{-1})$.  This proves
\eqref{eq:truncated-chebyshev-cauchy}.

For a remote node,
\[
 |\cos\theta-\cos\theta_{j,N}|
 \geq c_\delta\min\{|\theta-\theta_{j,N}|,1\}.
\]
Summing by angular distance from $\theta$ gives
\[
 \frac1{N^2}\sum_{\rm remote}
 \frac1{|x-x_{j,N}|^2}
 \leq
 C_\delta\sum_{r\geq M}\frac1{r^2}
 +\frac{C_\delta}{N}
 \leq\frac{C_\delta}{M},
\]
which is~\eqref{eq:truncated-chebyshev-square}.

Put
\[
 E_{j,N}
 =\exp\!\left(-\frac{iS}{N(x-x_{j,N})}\right).
\]
All $E_{j,N}$ commute and are unitary.  Equations
\eqref{eq:common-stark-log-tail} and
\eqref{eq:truncated-chebyshev-square}, followed by a unitary telescoping
estimate, give
\[
 \left\|\prod_{\rm remote}W\!\left(N(x-x_{j,N})\right)
       -\prod_{\rm remote}E_{j,N}\right\|
 \leq\frac{C_\delta}{M}.
\]
On the other hand,
\[
 \prod_{\rm remote}E_{j,N}
 =
 \exp\!\left\{
  -iS\,\frac1N\sum_{\rm remote}\frac1{x-x_{j,N}}
 \right\},
\]
whose distance from the identity is bounded by
$\|S\|$ times~\eqref{eq:truncated-chebyshev-cauchy}.  This proves
\eqref{eq:chebyshev-remote-product}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[A quantitative triangular-array stopping criterion]
\label{cor:chebyshev-triangular-array}
For every $N$, let $S_N=S_N^*$ and let
$W_{j,N}:\mathbb R\to\mathrm U(d)$, $1\leq j\leq N$, satisfy
\begin{equation}
 \left\|W_{j,N}(\nu)-\exp\!\left(-\frac{iS_N}{\nu}\right)\right\|
 \leq\frac{C_N}{\nu^2},
 \qquad |\nu|\geq\nu_{0,N},
 \label{eq:triangular-common-stark-tail}
\end{equation}
with constants independent of $j$.  Use the Chebyshev centres and the same
local/remote convention as in Proposition
~\ref{prop:chebyshev-stark-cancellation}.  Then, whenever
$M\geq c_\delta\nu_{0,N}$ and $Mh_N\leq\delta/2$,
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{\theta\in[\delta,\pi-\delta]}
 \left\|
  \prod_{\rm remote}W_{j,N}\!\left(N(x-x_{j,N})\right)-\Id
 \right\|
 \leq
 C_\delta\left[
  \|S_N\|\left(\frac1M+\frac{M}{N}\right)+\frac{C_N}{M}
 \right].
 \label{eq:triangular-chebyshev-remote-bound}
\end{equation}
In particular, if
\begin{equation}
 K_N:=1+\|S_N\|+C_N+\nu_{0,N}=o(\sqrt N),
 \label{eq:sub-square-root-controller-growth}
\end{equation}
then $M_N=\lfloor\sqrt N\rfloor$ makes the remote product converge uniformly
to the identity.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:chebyshev-stark-cancellation} is unchanged.
The telescoping remainder is $C_\delta C_N/M$, while the commuting
exponentials contribute
$C_\delta\|S_N\|(M^{-1}+M/N)$.  A remote node has
$|N(x-x_{j,N})|\geq c_\delta M$, which explains the threshold on $M$.
Substitution of $M_N=\lfloor\sqrt N\rfloor$ and
\eqref{eq:sub-square-root-controller-growth} proves the last assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Symmetric sidebands equalize every finite Stark family]
\label{prop:finite-stark-gramian-equalization}
Let $C_1,\ldots,C_m\in
\operatorname{Hom}(\mathcal H_-,\mathcal H_+)$ and, for a smooth compactly
supported vector control $u=(u_1,\ldots,u_m)$, put
\[
 C_u(t)=\sum_{q=1}^m u_q(t)C_q,
 \qquad
 \Gamma(u)_{qr}=\int_{\mathbb R}\overline{u_q(t)}u_r(t)\,\dd t .
\]
The Stark residue in
Proposition~\ref{prop:dipole-unavoidable-one-over-nu-tail} is the linear
function of this positive Gramian
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal S(\Gamma)=
 \begin{pmatrix}
  \displaystyle\sum_{q,r}\Gamma_{qr}C_q^*C_r&0\\[2mm]
  0&\displaystyle-\sum_{q,r}\Gamma_{rq}C_qC_r^*
 \end{pmatrix}.
 \label{eq:stark-residue-gramian-map}
\end{equation}
Let $u^{(1)},\ldots,u^{(J)}$ be any finite family of such controls.  For every
$R<\infty$ and $\eta>0$ there are one positive matrix
$\Gamma_*$ and padded controls
$\widetilde u^{(1)},\ldots,\widetilde u^{(J)}$ with the following properties:
\begin{align}
 \Gamma(\widetilde u^{(j)})&=\Gamma_*,
 \qquad 1\leq j\leq J,
 \label{eq:exact-common-control-gramian}\\
 \sup_{|\nu|\leq R}
 \left\|W_\nu[\widetilde u^{(j)}]-W_\nu[u^{(j)}]\right\|
 &<\eta .
 \label{eq:sideband-padding-locally-invisible}
\end{align}
Here the original pulse is kept first and the padding is concatenated after
it, so the two endpoint propagators are compared in the same absolute
interaction frame.  For each fixed padding construction,
\begin{equation}
 W_\nu[\widetilde u^{(j)}]
 =
 \Id-\frac{i}{\nu}\mathcal S(\Gamma_*)+O_j(\nu^{-2}),
 \qquad |\nu|\longrightarrow\infty .
 \label{eq:equalized-common-stark-tail}
\end{equation}
Thus common-residue equalization has no algebraic obstruction on a fixed
finite carrier family.  The statement does not assert a remainder or cost
bound uniform as $J$ grows without additional hypotheses.  Quantitatively, if
a triangular input family has uniformly bounded Gramians and its unpadded
quadratic-tail constants and thresholds are $o(\sqrt N)$ uniformly in the
carrier index, then the padding can be chosen so that the equalized family
still satisfies~\eqref{eq:sub-square-root-controller-growth}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Choose
\[
 \gamma>\max_{1\leq j\leq J}\|\Gamma(u^{(j)})\|,
 \qquad
 \Gamma_*=\gamma\Id_m,
 \qquad
 H_j=\Gamma_*-\Gamma(u^{(j)}).
\]
Every $H_j$ is positive definite.  By a Cholesky decomposition and disjoint
smooth bump intervals, construct finitely many vector controls
$p_{j,\ell}$ such that
\begin{equation}
 2\sum_\ell\Gamma(p_{j,\ell})=H_j.
 \label{eq:padding-half-gramian}
\end{equation}
For a parameter $\Omega>0$, append for every $\ell$ two disjoint copies of
$p_{j,\ell}$, modulated respectively by
$e^{i\Omega t}$ and $e^{-i\Omega t}$.  Modulation by a scalar phase does not
change a control Gramian, and disjoint concatenation adds Gramians.  Hence
\eqref{eq:padding-half-gramian} proves
\eqref{eq:exact-common-control-gramian} exactly.

It remains to check that the padding is locally invisible.  In
\eqref{eq:far-detuned-dipole-system}, modulation of a fixed bump by
$e^{\pm i\Omega t}$ replaces its detuning by $\nu\pm\Omega$.  Translating the
bump to its appended interval only conjugates the off-diagonal block by a
scalar block phase; this leaves its Stark residue invariant.  Proposition
~\ref{prop:dipole-unavoidable-one-over-nu-tail}, applied to the two unmodulated
copies, therefore gives uniformly for $|\nu|\leq R$,
\[
 W_{\nu,+\Omega}
 =
 \Id-\frac{i\mathcal S_\ell}{\nu+\Omega}+O_R(\Omega^{-2}),
 \qquad
 W_{\nu,-\Omega}
 =
 \Id-\frac{i\mathcal S_\ell}{\nu-\Omega}+O_R(\Omega^{-2}).
\]
The leading terms cancel because
\[
 \frac1{\nu+\Omega}+\frac1{\nu-\Omega}
 =\frac{2\nu}{\nu^2-\Omega^2}
 =O_R(\Omega^{-2}).
\]
Thus every appended pair has endpoint $\Id+O_R(\Omega^{-2})$.  There are only
finitely many pairs, so their product has the same bound with a finite
constant.  Taking $\Omega$ large proves
\eqref{eq:sideband-padding-locally-invisible}.

Finally, Proposition
~\ref{prop:dipole-unavoidable-one-over-nu-tail} and
\eqref{eq:stark-residue-gramian-map} give
\eqref{eq:equalized-common-stark-tail}.  In the physical dipole controller,
the complex components $u_q=x_q-iy_q$ are precisely the two available real
carrier quadratures.  The two scalar sideband modulations therefore remain
admissible real dipole controls.

For the quantitative last assertion, choose $\gamma$ once above the uniform
Gramian bound and use one fixed bump template in the Cholesky construction.
The coefficients of all padding bumps are then uniformly bounded.  For
$|\nu|\geq2\Omega$, the shifted expansions above and
\[
 \left|
  \frac1{\nu+\Omega}+\frac1{\nu-\Omega}-\frac2\nu
 \right|
 =
 \frac{2\Omega^2}{|\nu|\,|\nu^2-\Omega^2|}
 \leq\frac{C\Omega}{\nu^2}
\]
show that the padding adds a tail threshold $O(\Omega)$ and a quadratic-tail
constant $O(1+\Omega)$, uniformly in the carrier index.  Choose
$\Omega=\Omega_N\to\infty$ so slowly that the original growth bound plus
$\Omega_N$ remains $o(\sqrt N)$.  The local padding error is then
$O_R(\Omega_N^{-2})\to0$, while the equalized family satisfies
\eqref{eq:sub-square-root-controller-growth}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Under a common residue, the harmonic tail is not the global obstruction]
\label{cor:chebyshev-tail-criterion}
Consider a family of rescaled dipole words whose far-detuned propagators have
the common-residue expansion
\[
 W_{j,N}(x)
 =
 \exp\!\left\{
  -\frac{iS}{N(x-x_{j,N})}
 \right\}
 +O\!\left(\frac1{N^2|x-x_{j,N}|^2}\right)
\]
on one fixed finite safety space, with constants independent of $j$ and $N$.
Place their centres at the affine images of the Chebyshev nodes in an interval
strictly larger than the target energy band.  If, for every fixed $M$, the
$O(M)$ local words can be jointly synthesized with an error tending to zero
after $N\to\infty$, then a diagonal choice
$M_N\to\infty$, $M_N/N\to0$ gives a uniformly compactified multiband word.

Thus Proposition~\ref{prop:dipole-unavoidable-one-over-nu-tail} rules out
absolute telescoping but does not rule out the multiband theorem.  Under the
displayed common-residue hypothesis, its leading tail is cancelled
by~\eqref{eq:chebyshev-remote-product}.  The remaining physical obligation is
now quantitative and explicit.  Proposition
~\ref{prop:finite-stark-gramian-equalization} supplies a gate-preserving common
residue for every fixed finite tiling.  What is not supplied is a bound uniform
as the tiling and its local cluster grow: the padded residue, the
$O(\nu^{-2})$ constant, and the joint local synthesis error must still admit
one compatible diagonal limit.
\end{corollary}

\begin{problem}[The single remaining multiband tail estimate]
\label{prob:dipole-multiband-tail}
Fix $K=[s_0,s_1]\Subset(0,\infty)$ and a finite harmonic safety space.
For a dipole carrier $\omega$, the only positive coupled gaps are
\[
 \Delta_a=2(a+1),\qquad a\geq0,
\]
and the resonance centres in $K$ form the finite set
\[
 \mathcal A(\omega,K)
 =\{\,a\geq0:\omega/\Delta_a\in K\,\}.
\]
Prove a quantitative physical lift of Corollary
~\ref{cor:ideal-dipole-multiline-profiles} with the following order of
quantifiers:
\begin{enumerate}
\item first prescribe continuous target profiles on the finitely many
resonance tubes, taking the identity on every unwanted line;
\item then choose one slow carrier scale $\lambda$ so that the exact
full-sphere interaction propagator realizes those profiles uniformly on
\emph{all} of $K$, including the unbounded scaled-detuning tails between the
tubes;
\item retain one finite $L^1$ budget before the safety and outgoing cutoffs,
bounded physical height, and arbitrarily small $X_3$ cost.
\end{enumerate}
Such a theorem gives compactly energy-supported adjacent-shell gates.
Overlapping adjacent blocks generate the full special-unitary group on every
finite harmonic safety space.  A finite partition of unity on $K$ then
produces the broadband drift-decorated population swaps in
Proposition~\ref{prop:population-swap-phase-cancellation}, and hence solves
Problem~\ref{prob:lens-assisted-inverse}.  The obstruction left in this
problem is quantitative rather than algebraic: Corollary
~\ref{cor:ideal-dipole-multiline-profiles} controls every fixed detuning
compactum, while the physical rescaling makes the detuning range across
$K$ grow like $\lambda^{-1}$.  Moreover a cover by windows of width
$O(\lambda)$ uses $O(\lambda^{-1})$ pulses.  Proposition
~\ref{prop:dipole-first-defect-correctable} gives the required
$o(\lambda)$ endpoint error on every fixed detuning compactum, but its
diagonal construction is not uniform as that compactum grows.
Proposition~\ref{prop:dipole-unavoidable-one-over-nu-tail} shows that an
absolute summable tail modulus for each nontrivial pulse is impossible.
Proposition~\ref{prop:chebyshev-stark-cancellation} supplies the required
correlated cancellation whenever the elementary words have one common Stark
residue, and Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-stark-gramian-equalization} imposes
that residue without changing any fixed finite family of ideal gates in the
limit.  Hence the unresolved statement is no longer an unspecified tail bound
or a finite-dimensional equalization problem.  It is the growing-cluster
uniformity in Corollary~\ref{cor:chebyshev-tail-criterion}: after the tiling is
equalized, prove the sub-square-root controller growth
\eqref{eq:sub-square-root-controller-growth} and make the joint
$O(\sqrt N)$ local synthesis error tend to zero.  Corollary
~\ref{cor:chebyshev-triangular-array} then fixes
$M_N=\lfloor\sqrt N\rfloor$ and closes the remote product.  Independent
operator-norm telescoping remains ruled out.

Proposition~\ref{prop:zs-broadband-energy-floor} imposes an additional stopping
test on this last step.  On every fixed finite harmonic safety space, the
effective two-level Rabi envelope is a bounded linear functional of the scalar
profile, so its quadratic energy is bounded above by a constant times the
physical $X_3$ cost.  Hence a partition of $K$ into isolated two-level
inversion passbands has a strictly positive total $X_3$ cost and cannot prove
item~3 above.  A positive solution of the present problem must therefore use
genuinely multilevel excursions in an essential way and prove that their
matrix scattering cost escapes the scalar trace bound.  If the corresponding
matrix Zakharov--Shabat trace identity yields the same lower bound, the
selected multiband route fails structurally.  Proposition
~\ref{prop:matrix-zs-energy-floor} and Corollary
~\ref{cor:dipole-multiband-energy-no-go} prove precisely that lower bound.
Thus the adjacent-block resonant version of this problem is answered
negatively.
\end{problem}

\begin{remark}[Stopping decision for the selected carrier route]
\label{rem:dipole-carrier-route-stops}
The failure is now structural, not a missing estimate.  The common-residue
Chebyshev construction solves the global operator-norm tail, but any
fixed-band population-transfer tiling inside the matrix resonant block spends
a positive amount of quadratic control energy.  It therefore cannot be
inserted into generations whose $X_3$ costs are required to be summable and
arbitrarily prescribed.  In accordance with the stated stopping rule, this
adjacent-block multiband route must be abandoned.  A future positive Lemma~B
would need a genuinely full-sphere mechanism whose essential excursions do
not reduce to a matrix Zakharov--Shabat scattering word.
\end{remark}

\begin{problem}[The lens-assisted inverse stopping test]
\label{prob:lens-assisted-inverse}
Let $A_3=-\Delta_{\mathbb S^2}$, let
$S\Subset(0,\infty)$ be a nondegenerate interval, and let
$\mathcal E\subset C^\infty(\mathbb S^2)$ be finite dimensional and invariant
under conjugation.  Decide whether, for every $\tau>0$ and $\varepsilon>0$,
there are smooth real scalar masks $q_1,\ldots,q_N$ and positive flight times
$t_1,\ldots,t_N$ such that the physically ordered word
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal W_s=
 \overleftarrow{\prod}_{j=1}^{N}
 \left(e^{-is t_jA_3}e^{-isM_{q_j}}\right)
 \label{eq:lens-assisted-word}
\end{equation}
satisfies, for some continuous $\chi:S\to\T$,
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{s\in S}
 \left\|
  \mathcal W_s-\chi(s)e^{is\tau A_3}
 \right\|_{\mathcal E\to L^2(\mathbb S^2)}
 <\varepsilon,
 \label{eq:lens-assisted-inverse-target}
\end{equation}
with the same estimate on the finite compact-output safety enlargement needed
by Lemma~\ref{lem:compact-output-stability}.

A positive answer converts the Talbot drift into an effectively signed
broadband control; the semisimple ensemble-control theorem may then be applied
only after the compressed scalar controls are shown to generate the required
finite-dimensional Lie algebra and the word is realized with the radial
residual and $X_3$ ledgers.  A negative answer is a structural failure of the
only remaining broadband scalar mechanism.  Proposition
~\ref{prop:positive-flight-no-recurrence} proves that masks are essential:
free-flight recurrence alone cannot solve this problem.  Proposition
~\ref{prop:fejer-su2-inverse} proves, conversely, that one exact signed
noncommuting $\mathrm{SU}(2)$ mixer would be sufficient at the ideal
two-level level.  The unresolved physical issue is whether smooth scalar
multiplication supplies such mixers on the required finite safety bundle with
uniform detuning control and without treating a Galerkin compression as an
invariant physical subspace.  Proposition
~\ref{prop:no-exact-scalar-galerkin-mixer} rules out that shortcut: any
successful mixer must make controlled excursions into the complementary
angular modes and refocus them uniformly on $S$.  Proposition
~\ref{prop:scalar-conjugate-average-no-go} further rules out the direct
positive-average lift of the Fej\'er proof.  Any successful word must therefore
use genuinely higher-order noncommutative composite dynamics, not only
first-order convexification of scalar-mask conjugates.  Proposition
~\ref{prop:broadband-transposition-criterion} gives an exact constructive
reduction of that remaining task: it is enough to realize drift-decorated
transpositions
\[
 \mathcal S_{j,s}\simeq
 e^{-isT_jA_3}P_j
\]
between the target harmonics and sufficiently high auxiliary harmonics,
uniformly for $s\in S$.  The terminal free factor is not an error:
Proposition~\ref{prop:broadband-transposition-criterion} absorbs every
$T_j$ exactly into modified positive echo times.  Pointwise controllability
extends by Corollary~\ref{cor:fixed-energy-swap} to some nonempty
neighbourhood, but with no quantitative lower bound.  Proposition
~\ref{prop:adiabatic-phase-orientation-no-go} excludes a simple gapped
adiabatic loop as a phase-reversal shortcut.  It does not exclude a
quasi-adiabatic population permutation through a deliberately collapsing
gap: after such a permutation, the echo rather than the adiabatic phase
reverses the relative phase orientation.  The
finite-dimensional scalar-control theorem of
\cite{LiangBoscainSigalotti2026} proves population inversion of one initial
eigenstate up to an uncontrolled phase, under separated gap intervals.
Proposition~\ref{prop:population-swap-phase-cancellation} shows that this
internal phase is harmless: an isolated $\mathrm{SU}(2)$ inversion suffices.
Proposition~\ref{prop:pp-to-ur-symmetrization} goes further at the ideal
two-level level: time-and-phase reversal converts a point-to-point passband
profile into a full universal rotation, while a zero-angle stopband is
refocused to the identity.  Thus uncontrolled transfer phase and spectator
refocusing are not separate ideal pulse-design obstructions.  Proposition
~\ref{prop:ideal-pp-profile-reachable} proves that this joint
passband--stopband profile is reachable in the ideal Bloch ensemble.  Its
strong-pulse closure has no amplitude bound uniform in the target accuracy,
and the cited finite-dimensional scalar theorem gives no dimension-free
infinite-sphere leakage estimate.  Neither result therefore implies
\eqref{eq:population-only-broadband-swap}.  Proposition
~\ref{prop:single-carrier-high-mode-no-go} shows more in the asymptotic
high-mode regime used to enlarge the echo gaps: every spatial harmonic that
can drive the target line also drives an overlapping transition from the
ground state.  Zeroing those unwanted couplings therefore zeros the target
coupling itself.  A surviving construction must use multiple spatial
profiles and genuinely noncommutative interference, not a single isolated
chirped line.  Propositions~\ref{prop:adjacent-shell-full-lie-algebra} and
\ref{prop:finite-adjacent-shell-ensemble-gate} identify a concrete
algebraically viable replacement.  Pure degree-$2\ell+1$ profiles isolate
the exact adjacent-shell resonance, generate the full special-unitary
algebra on $\mathcal H_\ell\oplus\mathcal H_{\ell+1}$, and synthesize any
continuous compressed ensemble gate with forward time.  Proposition
 ~\ref{prop:adjacent-shell-growing-resonant-cluster} then locates the exact
 fixed-window obstruction: on $S=[s_0,s_1]$, the same profiles obligatorily
 couple every adjacent pair from $\ell$ through
 $\lfloor(s_1/s_0)(\ell+1)\rfloor-1$.  The selected route avoids that cluster
 by Proposition~\ref{prop:adjacent-shell-band-isolation}: on a centred window
 the sharp condition is $h<c/(2\ell+3)$.  Proposition
 ~\ref{prop:isolated-shell-bandpass-filter} then performs the missing
 full-sphere spectral selection in operator norm, without any Galerkin
 projection, and Corollary~\ref{cor:isolated-shell-full-tangent-algebra}
 proves that the resulting physical tangent algebra is
 $\mathfrak{su}(4\ell+4)$.  Proposition
 ~\ref{prop:uniform-scaled-galerkin} separately shows that a fixed $L^1$
 budget would make the leakage cutoff uniform in $s$.

 The local selective-pulse lift is now settled by Propositions
 ~\ref{prop:power-two-saturated-gap} and
 \ref{prop:saturated-carrier-full-su}, Theorem
 ~\ref{thm:shrinking-window-carrier-lift}, and Corollary
 ~\ref{cor:local-high-gap-physical-swaps}.  They produce, about every
 prescribed centre, a bounded-height finite-budget physical scalar word whose
 full-sphere interaction propagator is an $\mathrm{SU}(2)$ population swap on
 the selected pair and the identity on its complement, up to arbitrary
 operator-norm error.  Proposition~\ref{prop:causal-filter-cost} is bypassed
 because the complete nonlinear Lie word is compressed into one slow-time
 pulse before the window scale is sent to zero.

 What these results do not prove is the quantifier in the present problem:
 one word on an \emph{arbitrarily prescribed fixed} nondegenerate interval
 $S$, or a quantitative lower bound on the successive local windows sufficient
 for~\eqref{eq:hazard-divergence}.  Thus the remaining stopping test is global
 chromatic coverage.  It may be closed either by a genuinely fixed-band
 composite word, or by an adaptive factorization whose certified local widths
 have divergent total hazard.
\end{problem}

\subsection{The last lens mechanism: quasi-adiabatic population
permutations}

The preceding carrier analysis does not exhaust smooth scalar potentials.
There is one qualitatively different mechanism.  A slowly moved, high, narrow
potential wall can make two spectral gaps exponentially small and can
alternate adiabatic and effectively diabatic passages.  On an interval this
mechanism realizes finite permutations of Schr\"odinger eigenmodes
\cite{DucaJolyTuraev2020}.  The parameter $s$ in
\eqref{eq:lens-assisted-word} multiplies the \emph{entire} Hamiltonian.
Therefore the worst adiabatic error on a gapped portion occurs at
$s=s_0$, whereas the worst error for an intentionally diabatic passage
through a very small avoided crossing occurs at $s=s_1$.  Uniformity on
$S=[s_0,s_1]\Subset(0,\infty)$ is possible only if the wall construction
opens a two-scale window: the outer gaps must be adiabatic already at $s_0$
while the selected minimum gap remains diabatic even at $s_1$.  The following
propositions separate what is already rigorous from the one sphere-specific
lemma that remains.

\begin{proposition}[Exact positive-speed identity; no projective positivity
obstruction]
\label{prop:lens-positive-speed}
Let
\[
 W_s=e^{-isH_N}\cdots e^{-isH_1},
\]
where the $H_r$ are self-adjoint and nonnegative on a common invariant core.
Then
\begin{equation}
 K_W(s):=iW_s^*\partial_sW_s
 =\sum_{r=1}^N
   (e^{-isH_{r-1}}\cdots e^{-isH_1})^*
   H_r
   (e^{-isH_{r-1}}\cdots e^{-isH_1})
 \ \geq\ 0
 \label{eq:lens-positive-speed}
\end{equation}
in quadratic-form sense, with the $r=1$ conjugating product interpreted as
the identity.  Every physical lens word
\eqref{eq:lens-assisted-word} has this property after multiplication by a
scalar phase.

Nevertheless \eqref{eq:lens-positive-speed} does not obstruct
\eqref{eq:lens-assisted-inverse-target}.  If $\mathcal E$ is a finite
$A_3$-invariant harmonic space and
$c\geq\tau\lambda_{\max}(A_3|_{\mathcal E})$, then
\[
 e^{-ics}e^{is\tau A_3}|_{\mathcal E}
 =e^{-is(cI-\tau A_3|_{\mathcal E})},
 \qquad cI-\tau A_3|_{\mathcal E}\geq0.
\]
Thus the desired inverse drift is itself a positive-speed path after the
allowed projective phase is chosen.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Differentiate the product.  In the term in which the $r$th factor is
differentiated, all factors to its left cancel against their adjoints in
$W_s^*$, leaving the $r$th Hamiltonian conjugated by the factors to its
right.  This gives \eqref{eq:lens-positive-speed}.  For a mask $q$, choose
$a\geq-\min_{\mathbb S^2}q$.  Replacing $q$ by $q+a$ makes
$M_{q+a}\geq0$ and changes $e^{-isM_q}$ only by the scalar $e^{isa}$.
The free Hamiltonians $t_rA_3$ are nonnegative.  The last assertion follows
from the displayed finite-dimensional spectral inequality.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Why the conical-intersection shortcut stops at the
ground mode]
\label{prop:scalar-ground-conical-no-go}
For every smooth real $q$ on the connected sphere, the lowest eigenvalue of
\[
 H(q)=A_3+M_q
\]
is simple and its eigenfunction may be chosen strictly positive.  Hence no
finite-parameter family of smooth real scalar potentials has an intersection
between its first and second ordered eigenvalues.  In particular, the
conically connected-spectrum hypothesis of the infinite-dimensional
adiabatic controllability theorem of
\cite{BoscainGauthierRossiSigalotti2015} can never hold for the full scalar
Schr\"odinger spectrum on $\mathbb S^2$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The heat semigroup of $A_3+M_q$ is positivity improving on the connected
compact manifold.  Equivalently, the strong maximum principle and the
variational characterization of the lowest eigenvalue show that a ground
state has one sign and that two linearly independent ground states are
impossible.  Thus $\lambda_1(q)<\lambda_2(q)$ for every $q$.  Conical
connectedness requires, in particular, an intersection of
$\lambda_1$ and $\lambda_2$, so its hypothesis fails.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[What Proposition~\ref{prop:scalar-ground-conical-no-go} does
and does not say]
The proposition falsifies a tempting direct application of generic conical
intersection theory; it does not prove dynamical noncontrollability.  The
pointwise theorem of \cite{MasonSigalotti2010} supplies generic nonresonant
spectra and connected scalar couplings, but gives a control for one system,
not one control uniform under the common dilation
$H\mapsto sH$.  The driftless semisimple ensemble theorem, Chambrion's
control-amplitude dispersion theorem, and the finite-dimensional isolated-gap
theorem likewise have different dispersion or truncation hypotheses.  None
therefore settles Problem~\ref{prob:lens-assisted-inverse}.  A high-wall
protocol evades Proposition~\ref{prop:scalar-ground-conical-no-go} by using a
gap that becomes arbitrarily small in a singular wall limit, rather than an
exact ground-state intersection at a finite smooth potential.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Uniform common-dilation adiabatic estimate and physical
lens compilation]
\label{prop:uniform-adiabatic-lens-compilation}
Let $S=[s_0,s_1]\Subset(0,\infty)$ and let
\[
 H(r)=A_3+M_{V(r)},\qquad 0\leq r\leq1,
\]
where $V$ is a $C^2$ path of smooth real potentials.  Suppose that a
finite-rank spectral projection $P(r)$ is separated from the rest of the
spectrum by a gap $g>0$ along the path.  If $U_{s,T}$ is the propagator of
\[
 i\partial_t\psi=sH(t/T)\psi,\qquad0\leq t\leq T,
\]
then, after the usual dynamical and geometric unitary inside
$\operatorname{Ran}P(r)$ is removed,
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{s\in S}
 \|(I-P(1))U_{s,T}P(0)\|
 \leq \frac{C(H,P)}{s_0T}.
 \label{eq:uniform-common-dilation-adiabatic}
\end{equation}

For fixed $T$ and $V$, and for every finite compact set of smooth initial
data, $U_{s,T}$ is the uniform limit, for $s\in S$, of physical lens words
\begin{equation}
 \overleftarrow{\prod}_{k=0}^{n-1}
 \left(
  e^{-is\Delta A_3}
  e^{-isM_{\Delta V(k/n)}}
 \right),
 \qquad \Delta=T/n>0.
 \label{eq:adiabatic-lens-trotter}
\end{equation}
Consequently, any uniform population-permutation protocol produced by a
smooth time-dependent scalar potential can be inserted into
Problem~\ref{prob:lens-assisted-inverse}; no extra negative free flight is
needed.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Apply the quantitative adiabatic theorem to the rescaled equation
$i(sT)^{-1}\partial_r\psi=H(r)\psi$.  Its constant is uniform on the compact
path, and $sT\geq s_0T$, which gives
\eqref{eq:uniform-common-dilation-adiabatic}.  For
\eqref{eq:adiabatic-lens-trotter}, use the time-sliced Lie--Trotter formula.
On a finite compact set of smooth data the local splitting error is uniform
for $s\in[s_0,s_1]$; telescoping and compactness then give uniform strong
convergence.  Each time slice has exactly the ordering and signs allowed in
\eqref{eq:lens-assisted-word}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[A finite smooth multiwell has an ideal Dirichlet
permutation limit]
\label{prop:sphere-multiwell-spectral-limit}
Fix $N\geq2$.  There are pairwise disjoint congruent geodesic discs
$D_1,\ldots,D_N\Subset\mathbb S^2$, functions
\[
 b,\eta_1,\ldots,\eta_N\in C^\infty(\mathbb S^2;\mathbb R),
 \qquad b\geq0,
\]
and numbers $\nu_1<\nu_2$ with the following properties:
\[
 \{b=0\}=\bigcup_{j=1}^N\overline D_j,\qquad
 \eta_j=1\ \hbox{near }\overline D_j,\qquad
 \eta_j=0\ \hbox{near }\overline D_k\quad(k\neq j),
\]
and $\nu_1,\nu_2$ are the first two Dirichlet eigenvalues of every $D_j$.
For
\begin{equation}
 H_{B,c}=A_3+BM_b+\sum_{j=1}^Nc_jM_{\eta_j},
 \qquad c=(c_1,\ldots,c_N),
 \label{eq:sphere-multiwell-hamiltonian}
\end{equation}
let $\phi_j$ be the positive Dirichlet ground state of $D_j$, extended by
zero.  If
\begin{equation}
 \operatorname{osc}(c):=\max_jc_j-\min_jc_j<\nu_2-\nu_1,
 \label{eq:multiwell-one-level-window}
\end{equation}
then, as $B\to\infty$, the first $N$ eigenvalues and their spectral
projections converge, uniformly for $c$ in compact subsets of
\eqref{eq:multiwell-one-level-window}, to the ordered collection
\[
 \{\nu_1+c_j:1\leq j\leq N\},
 \qquad
 \{\mathbb C\phi_j:1\leq j\leq N\}.
\]
The $(N+1)$st limiting eigenvalue is at least
$\nu_2+\min_jc_j$.  In particular, when the coordinates of $c$ are
distinct, the first $N$ finite-$B$ eigenvalues are simple for all
sufficiently large $B$, and their normalized eigenfunctions are uniformly
close, up to signs, to the corresponding $\phi_j$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Choose the discs small and disjoint, choose $b$ flat on their closures and
strictly positive off their union, and choose the $\eta_j$ in disjoint
collars.  The closed forms
\[
 \mathfrak h_{B,c}[u]
 =\|\nabla u\|_2^2+B\|\sqrt b\,u\|_2^2
   +\sum_jc_j\|\sqrt{\eta_j}\,u\|_2^2
\]
increase with $B$.  Their monotone limit has form domain
$\bigoplus_jH^1_0(D_j)$ and is the direct sum
\[
 \bigoplus_{j=1}^N
 \left(-\Delta_{D_j}^{\rm D}+c_j\right).
\]
Kato's monotone form theorem gives strong resolvent convergence.  Compact
resolvent, the min--max principle, and compactness of the parameter set
upgrade this to convergence of each fixed finite spectral cluster and its
Riesz projection, uniformly in $c$.  The first two levels in the $j$th
summand are $\nu_1+c_j$ and $\nu_2+c_j$.
Condition~\eqref{eq:multiwell-one-level-window} puts all $N$ ground levels
strictly below every second level.  The final assertions follow from the
spectral projection convergence and the gap at the endpoints.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Barrier-independent fast floor sweep]
\label{prop:sphere-fast-floor-sweep}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:sphere-multiwell-spectral-limit}, let
$S=[s_0,s_1]\Subset(0,\infty)$, and let
$c:[0,\tau]\to\mathbb R^N$ be smooth.  Denote by $U_{s,B,c}(t)$ the
propagator of
\[
 i\partial_tu=sH_{B,c(t)}u.
\]
For every $\varepsilon>0$ there is $\tau_\varepsilon>0$, independent of
$B$ and of the height and speed of the floor sweep $c(t)$, such that
whenever $0<\tau<\tau_\varepsilon$,
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{\substack{s\in S\\1\leq j\leq N}}
 \left\|
 U_{s,B,c}(\tau)\phi_j
 -
 \exp\!\left[-is\int_0^\tau(\nu_1+c_j(t))\,\dd t\right]\phi_j
 \right\|_2
 <\varepsilon .
 \label{eq:uniform-fast-floor-sweep}
\end{equation}
The same assertion holds if $\phi_j$ at the two endpoints is replaced by
the corresponding finite-$B$ eigenfunction, after $B$ is chosen
sufficiently large.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Given $\delta>0$, choose
$\phi_{j,\delta}\in C^\infty_c(D_j)$ with
\[
 \|\phi_{j,\delta}-\phi_j\|_2<\delta,\qquad
 R_\delta:=
 \max_j\|(A_3-\nu_1)\phi_{j,\delta}\|_2<\infty.
\]
On the support of $\phi_{j,\delta}$ one has
$b=0$, $\eta_j=1$, and $\eta_k=0$ for $k\neq j$.  Hence
\[
 \psi_{j,s}(t)=
 \exp\!\left[-is\int_0^t(\nu_1+c_j(r))\,\dd r\right]
 \phi_{j,\delta}
\]
satisfies
\[
 \|(i\partial_t-sH_{B,c(t)})\psi_{j,s}(t)\|_2
 \leq s_1R_\delta .
\]
Duhamel's formula and unitarity give, uniformly in $B$, $c$, and $s$,
\[
 \|U_{s,B,c}(\tau)\phi_j-\psi_{j,s}(\tau)\|_2
 \leq 2\delta+s_1\tau R_\delta .
\]
First choose $\delta$, then $\tau$.  The endpoint replacement follows from
Proposition~\ref{prop:sphere-multiwell-spectral-limit}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Uniform signed permutations of finitely many spherical
eigenmodes]
\label{thm:sphere-multiwell-permutation}
Let $S=[s_0,s_1]\Subset(0,\infty)$ and let
\[
 \mathcal F=\bigoplus_{\ell=0}^{L}\mathcal H_\ell
\]
be a finite harmonic safety space.  There is a real harmonic eigenbasis
$e_1,\ldots,e_N$ of $\mathcal F$ with the following property.  Given a
permutation $\pi$ of $\{1,\ldots,N\}$, $\varepsilon>0$, and any prescribed
finite harmonic enlargement of $\mathcal F$, there are a smooth real
time-dependent potential $V(t,\omega)$, a duration $T>0$, a real number
$C$, and signs $\epsilon_j\in\{-1,1\}$ such that the full-sphere propagator
\[
 i\partial_tU_s(t)=s(A_3+M_{V(t)})U_s(t),\qquad U_s(0)=\Id ,
\]
satisfies
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{\substack{s\in S\\1\leq j\leq N}}
 \left\|
 U_s(T)e_j-
 \epsilon_j e^{-is(C+T\lambda_{\pi(j)})}e_{\pi(j)}
 \right\|_2<\varepsilon ,
 \qquad A_3e_j=\lambda_je_j .
 \label{eq:uniform-signed-sphere-permutation}
\end{equation}
The estimate is an $L^2(\mathbb S^2)$ estimate, not a Galerkin estimate;
in particular it includes leakage into the whole complementary spherical
spectrum.  The construction permits $\pi$ to transpose the constant ground
harmonic with an arbitrarily high auxiliary mode while fixing every listed
spectator.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\emph{Step 1: remove the endpoint degeneracies.}
For each $\ell\leq L$, the real Clebsch--Gordan map
\[
 \bigoplus_{\substack{0\leq r\leq2\ell\\r\ {\rm even}}}\mathcal H_r^\mathbb R
 \longrightarrow
 \operatorname{Sym}(\mathcal H_\ell^\mathbb R),
 \qquad
 q\longmapsto P_\ell M_qP_\ell ,
 \label{eq:cg-symmetric-compression}
\]
is an isomorphism: both sides have dimension
$(\ell+1)(2\ell+1)$, and the multiplicity-one Clebsch--Gordan
decomposition makes the map nonzero on every displayed irreducible
summand.  Consequently a generic real $q_0$ makes every
$P_\ell M_{q_0}P_\ell$, $\ell\leq L$, simple.  Choose $e_1,\ldots,e_N$
to be the resulting real eigenbases inside the harmonic shells.  Analytic
degenerate perturbation theory gives simple eigenvectors
$e_j(a)$ of $A_3+aM_{q_0}$ with
\[
 \max_{j\leq N}\|e_j(a)-e_j\|_2\longrightarrow0
 \quad(a\downarrow0).
 \]
A smooth ramp from zero to $a q_0$ performed in a time tending to zero
changes the finitely many $e_j$ by $o(1)$, uniformly in $s\in S$.
Thus the exactly degenerate endpoint costs only an arbitrarily small
error.

\emph{Step 2: encode the ordered modes in separated wells.}
Apply Proposition~\ref{prop:sphere-multiwell-spectral-limit} with $N$
discs.  Choose two vectors $c^-,c^+$ with distinct coordinates, both
satisfying~\eqref{eq:multiwell-one-level-window}, such that the rank of
the $j$th well ground level for $c^+$ is $\pi$ of its rank for $c^-$.
Choose $B$ so large that the endpoint eigenvectors are as close to the
$\phi_j$ as the error budget requires.

The set of smooth scalar potentials for which the first $N$ eigenvalues
are simple is residual.  More precisely, compression of a multiplication
perturbation to a multiple eigenspace supplies the real symmetric
splitting directions; a two-parameter transverse detour therefore avoids
the codimension-two discriminant.  It follows that the potentials
$a q_0$ and $Bb+\sum c_j^- \eta_j$ can be joined by a smooth path on
which the first $N$ eigenvalues are simple.  The same is true from
$Bb+\sum c_j^+\eta_j$ back to $a q_0$.  Compactness of the two paths
gives a positive minimum gap for the finite cluster.  Traverse these paths
slowly.  Proposition~\ref{prop:uniform-adiabatic-lens-compilation}, with
worst parameter $s_0$, transports all $N$ modes to and from the well
ground states with arbitrarily small full-space leakage.

\emph{Step 3: cross all desired ranks without tunnelling.}
Join $c^-$ to $c^+$ in a time $\tau$.  Proposition
~\ref{prop:sphere-fast-floor-sweep}, with worst parameter $s_1$, shows
that every population stays in its spatial well.  Since the endpoint
ordering has changed, lowering the barrier maps the $j$th incoming mode to
the $\pi(j)$th outgoing mode.  This proves a uniform population
permutation.  Notice the genuine two-scale choice:
\[
 T_{\rm ad}\gg (s_0g^2)^{-1},
 \qquad
 \tau\ll (s_1R_\delta)^{-1};
\]
the slow and fast intervals are independent and therefore compatible.

\emph{Step 4: calibrate the dynamical phases.}
Only a finite phase ledger remains.  At large $B$ the Hellmann--Feynman
matrix for the well floors is
\[
 G_{jk}(B,c)=
 \frac{\partial\lambda_j(H_{B,c})}{\partial c_k}
 =\langle\varphi_j(B,c),M_{\eta_k}\varphi_j(B,c)\rangle
 \longrightarrow\delta_{jk}.
 \label{eq:multiwell-phase-jacobian}
\]
It is therefore invertible.  Insert, while the populations are localized,
two long gapped holds with nearby distinct floor vectors.  By the inverse
function theorem their two eigenvalue vectors can be chosen to have any
prescribed average modulo the common vector $(1,\ldots,1)$.  Taking the
holds long makes the required floor perturbations arbitrarily small, so
\eqref{eq:multiwell-one-level-window} and the cluster gap are preserved.
Their common component is changed freely by adding a scalar constant to
the potential.

The Hamiltonians are real.  A continuous normalized real eigenvector has
zero Berry connection; its only possible closed-path holonomy is a sign.
Choose the holds so that the total dynamical action of the branch starting
at $e_j$ is
\[
 C+T\lambda_{\pi(j)}
\]
modulo an arbitrarily small error.  All nonadiabatic ramps used to insert
the holds may instead be included in the finite action ledger; the
invertibility of~\eqref{eq:multiwell-phase-jacobian} is unchanged.
Thus the remaining geometric factors are precisely signs $\epsilon_j$,
and~\eqref{eq:uniform-signed-sphere-permutation} follows.  Enlarging the
initial finite cluster incorporates any prescribed finite safety list;
all estimates above are full-space estimates.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Signed spectator phases disappear after a doubled echo]
\label{prop:signed-permutation-doubled-echo}
In Proposition~\ref{prop:broadband-transposition-criterion}, replace $P_j$
by a signed transposition
\[
 Q_j=D_jP_j,
\]
where $D_j$ is diagonal with entries in $\{-1,1\}$ in the displayed
eigenbasis.  Then the same inverse conclusion holds, without requiring
$Q_j$ to fix the spectators pointwise.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Every signed transposition satisfies
\[
 Q_jA Q_j^{-1}=P_jAP_j,\qquad Q_j^4=\Id,
\]
and $Q_j^2$ is diagonal, equals the identity on every spectator, and is a
common sign on $\operatorname{span}\{e_j,f_j\}$.  Use two echo blocks for
the $j$th pair:
\[
 (e^{-isT_jA}Q_j)e^{-isd_{j,1}A}(e^{-isT_jA}Q_j)
 (e^{-isT_jA}Q_j)e^{-isd_{j,2}A}(e^{-isT_jA}Q_j).
\]
The signs square away and the resulting generator is
\[
 2T_jA+x_jP_jAP_j,\qquad
 x_j=d_{j,1}+d_{j,2}+2T_j.
\]
The linear calculation in
\eqref{eq:positive-echo-times}--\eqref{eq:echo-eigenvalue-reversal}
is unchanged after replacing $T_\Sigma$ by $2T_\Sigma$ and imposing
$x_j>2T_j$.  The free parameter $D$ can again be taken arbitrarily large,
so this inequality holds and $x_j-2T_j$ splits into two positive numbers.
The same unitary telescoping estimate gives the approximate assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The lens-assisted inverse on a prescribed band]
\label{thm:wall-swap-closes-lens}
Problem~\ref{prob:lens-assisted-inverse} has a positive answer for every
finite harmonic safety space and every prescribed nondegenerate
$S\Subset(0,\infty)$.  Hence the signed-drift input required by the
full-block Talbot--Riesz route is available.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Choose the real harmonic basis supplied by Theorem
~\ref{thm:sphere-multiwell-permutation}.  Enlarge the safety space to contain
mutually orthogonal, sufficiently high auxiliary basis vectors and all
intermediate harmonic shells.  Apply that theorem to each isolated
target--auxiliary transposition, fixing the other displayed modes.
Equation~\eqref{eq:uniform-signed-sphere-permutation} gives, uniformly on
$S$,
\[
 \mathcal S_{j,s}
 \simeq e^{-isC_j}e^{-isT_jA_3}Q_j .
\]
Proposition~\ref{prop:uniform-adiabatic-lens-compilation} turns these
propagators into physical positive-flight scalar-mask words.
Proposition~\ref{prop:signed-permutation-doubled-echo} absorbs all signed
spectator holonomies and all terminal drifts into positive echo times and
produces
$\chi(s)e^{is\tau A_3}$ on the requested space.  Allocate the finitely
many permutation, adiabatic, floor-sweep, and Trotter errors below the
telescoping budget.  This is
\eqref{eq:lens-assisted-inverse-target}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[The double-well scale window gives a physical broadband
$\mathrm{SU}(2)$ module]
\label{prop:double-well-broadband-su2}
Let $S=[s_0,s_1]\Subset(0,\infty)$.  For every $\varepsilon>0$ there are a
smooth real double-well Hamiltonian $H_0=A_3+M_{V_0}$, a rank-two spectral
projection $P$ separated from the remaining spectrum by a gap
$G_{\rm ext}>0$, and a smooth real floor-imbalance profile $q$ such that the
following two operations hold on $P L^2(\mathbb S^2)$, uniformly for
$s\in S$ and with full-space leakage below $\varepsilon$:
\begin{enumerate}
\item equal-floor holds realize every forward tunnel flight
$e^{-istX}$, $t\geq0$, up to a scalar phase;
\item signed imbalance holds realize $e^{-isuZ}$ for every
$u\in\mathbb R$, up to a scalar phase.
\end{enumerate}
Here $X,Z$ are two anticommuting Pauli generators in a localized well basis.
Consequently finite positive-time scalar-control words realize every
continuous target $G:S\to\mathrm{SU}(2)$ uniformly on the doublet.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Take two congruent discs separated by a high smooth barrier and, if
necessary, add a common floor so that their ground doublet is the chosen
spectral cluster.  Let its eigenvalues be $c-\gamma$ and $c+\gamma$,
$\gamma>0$.  In the localized basis obtained by adding and subtracting the
two real eigenfunctions,
\[
 PH_0P=c\Id+\gamma X.
\]
The external gap can be increased by shrinking the discs, while $\gamma$
can be decreased independently by raising or widening only the separating
barrier.

Choose $q$ equal to $1$ on one well and $-1$ on the other, with its
transition inside the barrier.  Norm-resolvent convergence gives
\[
 PM_qP=Z+o(1)
\]
in the localized basis.  Use a small imbalance $\delta M_q$ and choose the
three scales in the compatible order
\begin{equation}
 \gamma\ll\delta\ll G_{\rm ext}.
 \label{eq:double-well-compatible-scales}
\end{equation}
For an equal-floor hold, $P$ is exactly invariant and a duration
$t/\gamma$ gives $e^{-istX}$ after the scalar $e^{-isc t/\gamma}$ is
removed.

For an imbalance hold, let $P_\delta$ be the rank-two spectral projection of
$H_0+\delta M_q$.  The Riesz projection formula and the Schur complement
give
\begin{align}
 \|P_\delta-P\|&\leq C\delta/G_{\rm ext},\\
 \left\|
 P_\delta(H_0+\delta M_q)P_\delta
 -\bigl(c\Id+\gamma X+\delta Z\bigr)
 \right\|&\leq C\delta^2/G_{\rm ext}+o(\delta).
 \label{eq:double-well-static-reduction}
\end{align}
The subspace $P_\delta L^2$ is invariant for the entire static hold.
Therefore these estimates do not grow linearly through leakage when the
hold is long.  A duration $|u|/\delta$, using
$\operatorname{sgn}(u)q$, gives
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{s\in S}
 \left\|
 U_s(|u|/\delta)|_{PL^2}
 -\xi_u(s)e^{-isuZ}
 \right\|
 \leq C_{S,u}\left(
 \frac{\gamma}{\delta}+\frac{\delta}{G_{\rm ext}}\right)+o(1).
 \label{eq:double-well-signed-floor-pulse}
\end{equation}
This proves the two claimed operations by
\eqref{eq:double-well-compatible-scales}.

Apply Proposition~\ref{prop:fejer-su2-inverse} with $Z$ relabelled as the
forward tunnel generator and $X$ as the signed floor generator.  It supplies
the missing negative tunnel flights uniformly on $S$.  We now have signed
flows of two noncommuting generators.  Their iterated commutators give odd
powers of $s$ in the real-symmetric part of $\mathfrak{su}(2)$ and even
powers in the real-skew part.  On $S\Subset(0,\infty)$, polynomials
$s p(s^2)$ and $s^2q(s^2)$ are respectively dense in both required
coefficient spaces.  Standard commutator products and a finite exponential
factorization of $G$ finish the uniform $\mathrm{SU}(2)$ synthesis.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Full-sphere finite-frame ensemble compiler]
\label{thm:sphere-finite-frame-compiler}
Let $S\Subset(0,\infty)$, let
$\mathcal E\subset C^\infty(\mathbb S^2)$ be finite dimensional and invariant
under conjugation, and let $G:S\to\mathcal U(\mathcal E)$ be continuous.
For every $\varepsilon>0$ and every prescribed finite compact-output safety
list, a smooth real time-dependent scalar potential has a common-dilation
propagator satisfying
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{s\in S}
 \|(U_s-\chi(s)G(s))|_{\mathcal E}\|_{\mathcal E\to L^2(\mathbb S^2)}
 <\varepsilon
 \label{eq:finite-frame-ensemble-compiler}
\end{equation}
for a continuous scalar phase $\chi$.  The estimate includes leakage into
the entire complementary spherical spectrum.  The propagator is uniformly
approximable on the safety list by a finite word of positive $A_3$-flights
and signed smooth scalar masks.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Approximate the given smooth frame, its conjugates, the compact family of
target images, and the safety list in one finite harmonic space and
polar-correct the finite Gram matrices.  Use the generic small endpoint
splitting from Theorem~\ref{thm:sphere-multiwell-permutation} to choose a
real basis.

Construct $N=\dim\mathcal E$ separated wells.  At any one gate, lower only
the barrier between a selected pair, leaving every other inter-well
tunnelling splitting smaller than the selected $\gamma$ by an arbitrarily
prescribed factor.  The switching time can be chosen in the nonempty window
\[
 (s_0G_{\rm ext})^{-1}\ll T_{\rm switch}
 \ll(s_1\gamma)^{-1}.
\]
Thus cluster adiabaticity controls the full complement while the finite
populations do not move appreciably during switching.  Independent well
floors detune and phase-calibrate the spectators.  Proposition
~\ref{prop:double-well-broadband-su2} then realizes a broadband
$\mathrm{SU}(2)$ gate on the selected pair.

The pairwise Pauli generators and diagonal floor generators generate
$\mathfrak{su}(N)$.  The same parity-aware polynomial argument as in the
last paragraph of Proposition~\ref{prop:double-well-broadband-su2} gives
the uniform closure $C(S,\mathrm{SU}(N))$.  Equivalently, decompose a finite
piecewise-exponential approximation of $G(s)$ into pairwise Givens gates and
diagonal phases.  There are only finitely many modules, so choose the
inter-well splittings, external gaps, switching errors, and spectator phase
errors successively below one telescoping budget.

Slow gapped entrance and exit paths map the free harmonic basis to the well
frame and back.  Their continuous dynamical phases and real holonomy signs
are included in the prescribed finite-dimensional target; independent floor
holds provide the required diagonal calibration.  This proves
\eqref{eq:finite-frame-ensemble-compiler}.  The time-sliced Lie--Trotter
argument of Proposition~\ref{prop:uniform-adiabatic-lens-compilation}
produces the claimed physical word.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[The outward angular-time budget]
\label{prop:outward-angular-time-budget}
An outward radial controller supported beyond radius $R$ has total positive
angular kinetic time at most
\begin{equation}
 \int_R^\infty\frac{\dd r}{r^2}=\frac1R
 \label{eq:outward-angular-time-budget}
\end{equation}
before the harmless common energy factor is inserted.  In particular, an
abstract positive-flight/mask word with total $A_3$-flight bounded below
independently of $R$ cannot be placed on successive exterior shells by the
weak-slab scale ledger alone.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
In the outgoing radial reduction the angular generator is
$A_3/r^2$ (and the common factor $(2k)^{-1}$ is the dilation parameter
$s$).  Positive radial ordering adds its coefficient, so any subdivision
into free collars and weak slabs has total coefficient bounded by the
displayed integral.  Long weak slabs do reduce the charged cost of a mask,
but they also consume their share of the same positive angular time.  Thus
the bound cannot be evaded by subdividing the shell.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Direct scaled slabs fit the outward time and cost ledgers]
\label{prop:direct-scaled-slab}
Let $A_3=-\Delta_{\mathbb S^2}$, let
$S=[s_0,s_1]\Subset(0,\infty)$, and fix $T<\infty$.  For each $N\to\infty$
choose
\begin{equation}
 \sqrt N\ll R_N\ll N,
 \qquad
 L_N=\frac{R_N^2}{N}.
 \label{eq:direct-slab-scale-window}
\end{equation}
Let $Q_N\in C^\infty([0,T]\times\mathbb S^2;\mathbb R)$, and compare the
scaled angular evolution
\begin{equation}
 i\partial_x\mathcal U_{s,N}(x)
 =
 s\left(\frac{A_3}{N}+M_{Q_N(x)}\right)
 \mathcal U_{s,N}(x)
 \label{eq:semiclassical-angular-gate}
\end{equation}
with the outward forward evolution on
$r=R_N+L_Nx$,
\begin{equation}
 i\partial_r U_{s,N}(r)
 =
 s\left(\frac{A_3}{r^2}+M_{V_N(r)}\right)U_{s,N}(r),
 \qquad
 V_N(R_N+L_Nx,\omega)=L_N^{-1}Q_N(x,\omega).
 \label{eq:direct-radial-slab}
\end{equation}
Suppose a prescribed compact safety family $\mathfrak C_N$ and both
propagators obey the trajectory bound
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{\substack{s\in S,\ 0\leq x\leq T\\f\in\mathfrak C_N}}
 \left(
  \|A_3\mathcal U_{s,N}(x)f\|_2+
  \|A_3U_{s,N}(R_N+L_Nx)f\|_2
 \right)
 \leq C_{\mathfrak C}N.
 \label{eq:direct-slab-trajectory-bound}
\end{equation}
Then
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{\substack{s\in S,\ 0\leq x\leq T\\f\in\mathfrak C_N}}
 \left\|
 U_{s,N}(R_N+L_Nx)f-\mathcal U_{s,N}(x)f
 \right\|_2
 \leq C_{S,T,\mathfrak C}\frac{R_N}{N}.
 \label{eq:direct-slab-forward-error}
\end{equation}
Moreover,
\begin{align}
 \int_{R_N}^{R_N+L_NT}\|V_N(r)\|_2^2\,\dd r
 &=
 \frac{N}{R_N^2}
 \int_0^T\|Q_N(x)\|_2^2\,\dd x,
 \label{eq:direct-slab-cost}\\
 \|V_N\|_\infty
 &=
 \frac{N}{R_N^2}\|Q_N\|_\infty,
 \label{eq:direct-slab-height}\\
 \int_{R_N}^{R_N+L_NT}\frac{\dd r}{r^2}
 &=
 \frac{T}{N}+O_T\!\left(\frac{R_N}{N^2}\right)
 =
 o(R_N^{-1}).
 \label{eq:direct-slab-angular-time}
\end{align}
Thus any family~\eqref{eq:semiclassical-angular-gate} with uniformly bounded
quadratic control action and the trajectory bound
\eqref{eq:direct-slab-trajectory-bound} has a forward radial realization
with vanishing charged cost, vanishing height, and sufficient outward
angular time.  Angular motion inside the slab is used deliberately; it is
not treated as the weak-mask error in
Proposition~\ref{prop:gradient-flow-radial-cost-no-go}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
After the change of variables $r=R_N+L_Nx$,
\eqref{eq:direct-radial-slab} becomes
\[
 i\partial_xU_{s,N}
 =
 s\left(
  \frac{L_N}{(R_N+L_Nx)^2}A_3+M_{Q_N(x)}
 \right)U_{s,N}.
\]
Uniformly for $0\leq x\leq T$,
\[
 \left|
 \frac{L_N}{(R_N+L_Nx)^2}-\frac1N
 \right|
 \leq
 \frac{C_T R_N}{N^2},
\]
because $L_N/R_N=R_N/N\to0$.  Duhamel's formula, unitarity, and
\eqref{eq:direct-slab-trajectory-bound} give
\eqref{eq:direct-slab-forward-error}.  The change of variables gives
\eqref{eq:direct-slab-cost} and
\eqref{eq:direct-slab-height} exactly.  Expanding
$(R_N+L_Nx)^{-2}$ and using~\eqref{eq:direct-slab-scale-window} gives
\eqref{eq:direct-slab-angular-time}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Direct scaled slabs have vanishing counter-propagation]
\label{prop:direct-scaled-slab-backscatter}
Retain Proposition~\ref{prop:direct-scaled-slab}, let
$K=[k_0,k_1]\Subset(0,\infty)$, and let $P_N$ be an $A_3$-invariant
finite-rank projection such that
\begin{equation}
 \|A_3P_N\|\leq C_AN.
 \label{eq:direct-slab-active-energy}
\end{equation}
Assume, uniformly in $N$,
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{0\leq x\leq T}
 \left(
  \|P_NM_{Q_N(x)}P_N\|
  +
  \|P_NM_{\partial_xQ_N(x)}P_N\|
 \right)
 \leq C_Q.
 \label{eq:direct-slab-radial-regularity}
\end{equation}
Let $\mathcal U^{(P)}_{s,N}$ denote the propagator of the compression of
\eqref{eq:semiclassical-angular-gate} to $P_NL^2(\mathbb S^2)$.
Consider on $P_NL^2(\mathbb S^2)$ the exact truncated radial equation
\begin{equation}
 -u''(r)+
 \left(
  \frac{P_NA_3P_N}{r^2}+P_NM_{V_N(r)}P_N
 \right)u(r)
 =
 k^2u(r).
 \label{eq:direct-slab-second-order-radial}
\end{equation}
After the harmless plane-wave phases at the two endpoints are removed, let
$\mathsf T_N(k)$ and $\mathsf R_N(k)$ denote its forward transmission and
backward reflection blocks.  Then, uniformly for $k\in K$,
\begin{align}
 \|\mathsf R_N(k)\|
 &\leq
 C_{K,T,A,Q}
 \left(\frac1{L_N}+\frac{R_N}{N}\right),
 \label{eq:direct-slab-backscatter-bound}\\
 \left\|
 \mathsf T_N(k)
 -
 \mathcal U^{(P)}_{(2k)^{-1},N}(T)
 \right\|
 &\leq
 C_{K,T,A,Q}
 \left(\frac1{L_N}+\frac{R_N}{N}\right).
 \label{eq:direct-slab-transmission-bound}
\end{align}
