Both errors tend to zero in the scale window
\eqref{eq:direct-slab-scale-window}.  Consequently, once the scaled angular
gate has a full-space safety enlargement and the outgoing Schur cutoff is
taken after that enlargement, counter-propagation does not create an
additional radial obstruction.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Write
\[
 W_N(r)=\frac{P_NA_3P_N}{r^2}+P_NM_{V_N(r)}P_N.
\]
Equations~\eqref{eq:direct-slab-active-energy},
\eqref{eq:direct-slab-radial-regularity}, and
$L_N=R_N^2/N$ give, on the slab,
\begin{equation}
 \|W_N(r)\|\leq\frac{C}{L_N},
 \qquad
 \|W_N'(r)\|\leq\frac{C}{L_N^2}.
 \label{eq:direct-slab-W-bounds}
\end{equation}
For the centrifugal derivative, use
$N/R_N^3=(L_NR_N)^{-1}\leq L_N^{-2}$, which follows from
$L_N/R_N=R_N/N<1$.

Use the exact variation-of-constants representation
\[
 u(r)=e^{ikr}a(r)+e^{-ikr}b(r),
 \qquad
 e^{ikr}a'(r)+e^{-ikr}b'(r)=0.
\]
Equation~\eqref{eq:direct-slab-second-order-radial} is equivalent to
\begin{align*}
 a'&=-\frac{i}{2k}W_N(r)
       \bigl(a+e^{-2ikr}b\bigr),\\
 b'&=\frac{i}{2k}W_N(r)
       \bigl(e^{2ikr}a+b\bigr).
 \end{align*}
Remove the two diagonal propagators generated by
$\mp iW_N/(2k)$.  Their norms and the norms of their derivatives are bounded
uniformly by~\eqref{eq:direct-slab-W-bounds}.  Every remaining off-diagonal
coefficient has the form
\[
 e^{\pm2ikr}B_N(r),
 \qquad
 \|B_N(r)\|\leq C/L_N,
 \qquad
 \|B_N'(r)\|\leq C/L_N^2.
\]
Integration by parts, using $2k\geq2k_0$, gives
\[
 \sup_{R_N\leq y\leq R_N+L_NT}
 \left\|
  \int_{R_N}^{y}e^{\pm2ikr}B_N(r)\,\dd r
 \right\|
 \leq \frac{C}{L_N}.
\]
The standard primitive-form Duhamel estimate, obtained by one further
integration by parts against the diagonal propagators, now gives
\eqref{eq:direct-slab-backscatter-bound} with its $L_N^{-1}$ term and shows
that the forward block differs by the same amount from the diagonal equation
\[
 i a'(r)=\frac1{2k}W_N(r)a(r).
\]
After $r=R_N+L_Nx$, that diagonal equation is the forward evolution in
Proposition~\ref{prop:direct-scaled-slab}.  Apply
\eqref{eq:direct-slab-forward-error} to obtain the additional $R_N/N$ term
in~\eqref{eq:direct-slab-transmission-bound}.  The same comparison absorbs
the harmless $R_N/N$ allowance in
\eqref{eq:direct-slab-backscatter-bound}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Every cost-compatible direct slab freezes spatial
densities]
\label{prop:direct-scaled-slab-density-no-go}
Let $\mathcal U_{s,N}$ solve~\eqref{eq:semiclassical-angular-gate}, and let
$\mathfrak C$ contain normalized trajectories from one fixed finite smooth
input frame.  Put
\begin{equation}
 E_N=
 \sup_{\substack{s\in S,\ 0\leq x\leq T\\f\in\mathfrak C}}
 \|A_3\mathcal U_{s,N}(x)f\|_2.
 \label{eq:direct-slab-trajectory-energy}
\end{equation}
Suppose radii $R_N$ are chosen so that the direct radial realization has
vanishing control cost and vanishing frozen-radius error:
\begin{equation}
 \frac{N}{R_N^2}\longrightarrow0,
 \qquad
 \frac{R_NE_N}{N^2}\longrightarrow0.
 \label{eq:direct-slab-compatibility-no-go}
\end{equation}
Then, for every real $X\in C^\infty(\mathbb S^2)$,
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{\substack{s\in S,\ 0\leq x\leq T\\f\in\mathfrak C}}
 \left|
 \langle\mathcal U_{s,N}(x)f,M_X\mathcal U_{s,N}(x)f\rangle
 -
 \langle f,M_Xf\rangle
 \right|
 \leq
 \frac{C_{S,T,X}(1+\sqrt{E_N})}{N}
 \longrightarrow0.
 \label{eq:direct-slab-density-freezing}
\end{equation}

In particular, let $e_0=(4\pi)^{-1/2}$ and let $e_\lambda$ be a normalized
real spherical harmonic with $A_3e_\lambda=\lambda e_\lambda$,
$\lambda>0$.  On any nondegenerate interval $S$ and for every $\tau>0$,
no such family can converge, up to scalar phase, to
$e^{is\tau A_3}$ on
$\operatorname{span}\{e_0,e_\lambda\}$.  Hence the direct scaled-slab
formulation~\eqref{eq:semiclassical-compiler-target} is false for the lens
inverse required in Problem~\ref{prob:short-time-radial-compiler}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For a normalized solution $\psi(x)=\mathcal U_{s,N}(x)f$, multiplication by
$Q_N(x)$ commutes with $M_X$.  Therefore
\[
 \frac{\dd}{\dd x}\langle\psi,M_X\psi\rangle
 =
 \frac{is}{N}\langle\psi,[A_3,M_X]\psi\rangle .
\]
Since
\[
 [A_3,M_X]\psi
 =
 -(\Delta X)\psi-2\nabla X\cdot\nabla\psi,
\]
Cauchy--Schwarz and
$\|\nabla\psi\|_2^2=\langle\psi,A_3\psi\rangle
\leq\|A_3\psi\|_2$ give
\[
 \left|
 \frac{\dd}{\dd x}\langle\psi,M_X\psi\rangle
 \right|
 \leq
 \frac{C_{S,X}}{N}
 \left(1+\sqrt{E_N}\right).
\]
Integration proves the displayed bound.  The first condition in
\eqref{eq:direct-slab-compatibility-no-go} implies $R_N\to\infty$, and the
second gives
\[
 \frac{E_N}{N^2}
 =
 \frac1{R_N}\frac{R_NE_N}{N^2}\longrightarrow0.
\]
Thus the right side of~\eqref{eq:direct-slab-density-freezing} tends to zero.

For the final assertion take
\[
 f=\frac{e_0+e_\lambda}{\sqrt2},
 \qquad X=e_\lambda.
\]
The scalar phase is irrelevant to expectations, and
\[
 \left\langle
 e^{is\tau A_3}f,M_{e_\lambda}e^{is\tau A_3}f
 \right\rangle
 -
 \langle f,M_{e_\lambda}f\rangle
 =
 \frac1{\sqrt{4\pi}}
 \{\cos(s\tau\lambda)-1\}.
\]
This continuous function is not identically zero on a nondegenerate
interval $S$.  Uniform operator convergence to the inverse drift would
therefore contradict~\eqref{eq:direct-slab-density-freezing}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Bounded-depth multiscale slabs also freeze spatial
densities]
\label{prop:bounded-depth-density-no-go}
For each $q$, concatenate $m_q$ scaled angular slabs.  On slab $j$, of scaled
duration $T_{q,j}$, let the actual normalized trajectory solve
\begin{equation}
 i\partial_x\psi_{q,j}
 =
 s\left(\frac{A_3}{N_{q,j}}+M_{Q_{q,j}(x)}\right)\psi_{q,j},
 \qquad 0\leq x\leq T_{q,j},
 \label{eq:multiscale-angular-slab}
\end{equation}
with the final state of one slab used as the initial state of the next.  Let
$R_{q,j}$ be its physical radius and put
\[
 E_{q,j}
 =
 \sup_{\substack{s\in S,\ 0\leq x\leq T_{q,j}\\
                  f\in\mathfrak C}}
 \|A_3\psi_{q,j}(x;s,f)\|_2,
\]
where $\mathfrak C$ is one fixed finite smooth input frame.  Define the three
resource sums
\begin{align}
 \alpha_q&=\sum_{j=1}^{m_q}\frac{T_{q,j}}{N_{q,j}},
 &
 \beta_q&=\sum_{j=1}^{m_q}
       \frac{T_{q,j}R_{q,j}E_{q,j}}{N_{q,j}^2},
 &
 \gamma_q&=\sum_{j=1}^{m_q}\frac{T_{q,j}}{R_{q,j}}.
 \label{eq:multiscale-density-resources}
\end{align}
Then every real $X\in C^\infty(\mathbb S^2)$ satisfies
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{\substack{s\in S\\f\in\mathfrak C}}
 \left|
 \langle\psi_{q,m_q}(T_{q,m_q}),M_X
                  \psi_{q,m_q}(T_{q,m_q})\rangle
 -
 \langle f,M_Xf\rangle
 \right|
 \leq
 C_{S,X}\left(\alpha_q+\sqrt{\beta_q\gamma_q}\right).
 \label{eq:multiscale-density-freezing}
\end{equation}
Consequently, if $\mathfrak C$ contains
$f=(e_0+e_\lambda)/\sqrt2$ and
\begin{equation}
 \alpha_q\longrightarrow0,
 \qquad
 \beta_q\gamma_q\longrightarrow0,
 \label{eq:multiscale-density-no-go-condition}
\end{equation}
the cascade cannot converge, up to scalar phase, to
$e^{is\tau A_3}$ on
$\operatorname{span}\{e_0,e_\lambda\}$ for any $\tau>0$ and any
nondegenerate interval $S$.

In particular, every bounded-depth family with
\[
 \sup_qm_q<\infty,\qquad
 \sup_{q,j}T_{q,j}<\infty,\qquad
 \min_jN_{q,j}\longrightarrow\infty,\qquad
 \min_jR_{q,j}\longrightarrow\infty,
\]
and vanishing summed frozen-radius error $\beta_q\to0$ is excluded.  More
generally, any surviving multiscale compiler must violate the explicit
product criterion
\[
 \left(\sum_j\frac{T_{q,j}R_{q,j}E_{q,j}}{N_{q,j}^2}\right)
 \left(\sum_j\frac{T_{q,j}}{R_{q,j}}\right)\longrightarrow0.
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
On slab $j$, the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:direct-scaled-slab-density-no-go} gives
\[
 \left|
 \langle\psi_{q,j}(T_{q,j}),M_X\psi_{q,j}(T_{q,j})\rangle
 -
 \langle\psi_{q,j}(0),M_X\psi_{q,j}(0)\rangle
 \right|
 \leq
 C_{S,X}\frac{T_{q,j}(1+\sqrt{E_{q,j}})}{N_{q,j}}.
\]
Telescoping over the concatenation leaves $\alpha_q$ and the sum
\[
 \sum_j\frac{T_{q,j}\sqrt{E_{q,j}}}{N_{q,j}}
 =
 \sum_j
 \sqrt{\frac{T_{q,j}R_{q,j}E_{q,j}}{N_{q,j}^2}}\,
 \sqrt{\frac{T_{q,j}}{R_{q,j}}}.
\]
Cauchy--Schwarz bounds the latter by $\sqrt{\beta_q\gamma_q}$, proving
\eqref{eq:multiscale-density-freezing}.  The two-harmonic expectation
computed in the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:direct-scaled-slab-density-no-go} is not constant in
$s$, so~\eqref{eq:multiscale-density-no-go-condition} rules out the lens
inverse.  Under the bounded-depth assumptions,
$\alpha_q\leq C/\min_jN_{q,j}\to0$ and
$\gamma_q\leq C/\min_jR_{q,j}\to0$, which proves the stated special case.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[A cheap logarithmic shell must develop
$R^{-1/2}$ angular microstructure]
\label{prop:log-shell-microstructure}
Consider the forward angular model on
$R\leq r\leq Re^T$,
\begin{equation}
 i\partial_rU_{s,R}(r)
 =
 s\left(\frac{A_3}{r^2}+M_{V_R(r)}\right)U_{s,R}(r),
 \qquad U_{s,R}(R)=I,
 \label{eq:log-shell-forward-model}
\end{equation}
where $S\Subset(0,\infty)$ and
\[
 V_R(Re^t,\omega)=\frac{1}{Re^t}W_R(t,\omega).
\]
After the exact change of variables $r=Re^t$,
\begin{equation}
 i\partial_tU_{s,R}
 =
 s\left(\frac{e^{-t}}{R}A_3+M_{W_R(t)}\right)U_{s,R},
 \qquad 0\leq t\leq T,
 \label{eq:log-shell-rescaled-model}
\end{equation}
and the physical quadratic ledger is
\begin{equation}
 \int_R^{Re^T}\|V_R(r)\|_2^2\,\dd r
 =
 \frac1R\int_0^Te^{-t}\|W_R(t)\|_2^2\,\dd t.
 \label{eq:log-shell-cost}
\end{equation}

Let
\[
 \Phi_R(t,\omega)=\int_0^tW_R(x,\omega)\,\dd x,
 \qquad
 Z_{s,R}(t)=e^{-isM_{\Phi_R(t)}}.
\]
If
\begin{equation}
 \sup_R\left\{
 \int_0^T\|W_R(t)\|_2^2\,\dd t+
 \int_0^T\|W_R(t)\|_{W^{2,\infty}(\mathbb S^2)}\,\dd t
 \right\}<\infty,
 \label{eq:log-shell-smoothness}
\end{equation}
then for every fixed finite smooth frame $\mathfrak C$,
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{\substack{s\in S,\ 0\leq t\leq T\\f\in\mathfrak C}}
 \|U_{s,R}(Re^t)f-Z_{s,R}(t)f\|_2
 \leq \frac{C_{S,T,\mathfrak C}}{R}.
 \label{eq:log-shell-multiplier-limit}
\end{equation}
Consequently, uniformly smooth logarithmic shells cannot converge, up to
scalar phase, to $e^{is\tau A_3}$ on
$\operatorname{span}\{e_0,e_\lambda\}$ for any $\tau>0$ and any
nondegenerate interval $S$.

More quantitatively, if such a logarithmic-shell family does approximate that
lens on the two-harmonic frame, then along a subsequence
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{0\leq t\leq T}
 \left(
  \|\Delta\Phi_R(t)\|_\infty+
  \|\nabla\Phi_R(t)\|_\infty^2
 \right)
 \geq cR.
 \label{eq:log-shell-microstructure-necessity}
\end{equation}
Thus a bounded-amplitude phase must contain angular frequencies of order at
least $\sqrt R$; the cheap $1/r$ tail survives the cost ledger only by leaving
every uniformly smooth control class.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The rescaled equation and~\eqref{eq:log-shell-cost} follow from
$\partial_t=r\partial_r$ and $\dd r=Re^t\dd t$.  Since multiplication
operators commute at different times, $Z_{s,R}$ is the propagator obtained
from~\eqref{eq:log-shell-rescaled-model} after deleting its kinetic term.
Duhamel's formula gives
\[
 \|U_{s,R}(Re^t)f-Z_{s,R}(t)f\|_2
 \leq
 \frac{|s|}{R}\int_0^t e^{-x}
 \|A_3Z_{s,R}(x)f\|_2\,\dd x.
\]
For fixed smooth $f$,
\[
 A_3(e^{-is\Phi}f)
 =
 e^{-is\Phi}
 \left[
 A_3f
 {}+is(\Delta\Phi)f
 {}+2is\,\nabla\Phi\cdot\nabla f
 {}+s^2|\nabla\Phi|^2f
 \right],
\]
up to the immaterial sign convention for $\Delta$.  Condition
\eqref{eq:log-shell-smoothness} therefore bounds the Duhamel integrand
uniformly and proves~\eqref{eq:log-shell-multiplier-limit}.

Take $f=(e_0+e_\lambda)/\sqrt2$.  Every $Z_{s,R}(T)$ preserves $|f|$
pointwise, whereas
\[
 e^{is\tau A_3}f
 =
 \frac{e_0+e^{is\tau\lambda}e_\lambda}{\sqrt2}
\]
has a different pointwise modulus whenever
$\cos(s\tau\lambda)\neq1$.  The elementary inequality
\[
 \|g-h\|_2\geq\bigl\||g|-|h|\bigr\|_2
\]
then gives a positive lower bound, for one $s$ in every nondegenerate
interval on which the target is nontrivial, between the target and every
phase multiplier of $f$.  This contradicts
\eqref{eq:log-shell-multiplier-limit}.

Finally, the displayed formula for $A_3(e^{-is\Phi}f)$ shows that if the
left side of~\eqref{eq:log-shell-microstructure-necessity} were $o(R)$, the
Duhamel error would be $o(1)$ on the two-harmonic frame.  The same modulus
argument would again contradict lens convergence.  This proves the
quantitative necessity.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Bounded-action logarithmic shells require ballistic
angular degree]
\label{prop:log-shell-ballistic-degree}
Retain the logarithmic-shell model
\eqref{eq:log-shell-rescaled-model}.  Let $P_{\leq L}$ denote the sum of the
spherical-harmonic shells of degrees at most $L$.  Suppose
\begin{equation}
 W_R(t)\in\bigoplus_{j=0}^{D_R}\mathcal H_j,
 \qquad
 \int_0^T\|W_R(t)\|_\infty\,\dd t\leq M
 \label{eq:log-shell-bandlimited-action}
\end{equation}
for all $R$.  Then, for every fixed $L_0$,
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{\substack{s\in S,\ 0\leq t\leq T\\
                  f\in P_{\leq L_0}L^2,\ \|f\|_2=1}}
 \|\nabla_\omega U_{s,R}(Re^t)f\|_2
 \leq
 C_{S,M,L_0}(1+D_R).
 \label{eq:log-shell-gradient-bandwidth-bound}
\end{equation}
Consequently, every real $X\in C^\infty(\mathbb S^2)$ satisfies
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{\substack{s\in S,\ 0\leq t\leq T\\
                  f\in P_{\leq L_0}L^2,\ \|f\|_2=1}}
 \left|
 \langle U_{s,R}(Re^t)f,M_XU_{s,R}(Re^t)f\rangle
 -
 \langle f,M_Xf\rangle
 \right|
 \leq
 C_{S,T,M,L_0,X}\frac{1+D_R}{R}.
 \label{eq:log-shell-bandwidth-density-bound}
\end{equation}
In particular, if $D_R=o(R)$, the logarithmic shell cannot converge, up to
scalar phase, to $e^{is\tau A_3}$ on
$\operatorname{span}\{e_0,e_\lambda\}$ for any $\tau>0$ and any
nondegenerate interval $S$.

Thus, under the bounded rescaled action
\eqref{eq:log-shell-bandlimited-action}, every successful logarithmic-shell
lens must have
\begin{equation}
 \liminf_{R\to\infty}\frac{D_R}{R}>0.
 \label{eq:log-shell-ballistic-degree-necessity}
\end{equation}
At radius $R$ this is angular wavelength $O(R^{-1})$, hence physical
tangential wavelength $O(1)$.  It is precisely the regime in which
$A_3/r^2$ is order one on the active modes and the paraxial
small-centrifugal approximation is unavailable.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Put
\[
 \vartheta_R(t)=\frac{1-e^{-t}}{R}
\]
and pass to the interaction picture
\[
 Y_{s,R}(t)=e^{is\vartheta_R(t)A_3}U_{s,R}(Re^t).
\]
Its generator is
\[
 B_{s,R}(t)
 =
 s\,e^{is\vartheta_R(t)A_3}
 M_{W_R(t)}
 e^{-is\vartheta_R(t)A_3}.
\]
Conjugation by the free group preserves every harmonic shell and
$\|B_{s,R}(t)\|\leq s_1\|W_R(t)\|_\infty$.  The
Clebsch--Gordan triangle rule gives
\[
 M_{W_R(t)}P_{\leq L}\subseteq P_{\leq L+D_R}L^2.
\]
Hence the $m$th Dyson term applied to $P_{\leq L_0}L^2$ lies in
$P_{\leq L_0+mD_R}L^2$ and has operator norm at most
\[
 \frac{a^m}{m!},
 \qquad a=s_1M.
\]
Since $\|A_3^{1/2}P_{\leq L}\|\leq L+1$, summing the Dyson series yields
\begin{align*}
 \|A_3^{1/2}Y_{s,R}(t)f\|_2
 &\leq
 \sum_{m=0}^\infty
 (L_0+mD_R+1)\frac{a^m}{m!}\\
 &=
 e^a(L_0+1+aD_R).
\end{align*}
The free interaction-picture factor commutes with $A_3$, proving
\eqref{eq:log-shell-gradient-bandwidth-bound}.

For $\psi(t)=U_{s,R}(Re^t)f$, multiplication by $W_R(t)$ commutes with
$M_X$, and therefore
\[
 \frac{\dd}{\dd t}\langle\psi,M_X\psi\rangle
 =
 \frac{is e^{-t}}R\langle\psi,[A_3,M_X]\psi\rangle.
\]
Using
\[
 [A_3,M_X]\psi
 =
 -(\Delta X)\psi-2\nabla X\cdot\nabla\psi
\]
and~\eqref{eq:log-shell-gradient-bandwidth-bound}, then integrating in
$t$, proves~\eqref{eq:log-shell-bandwidth-density-bound}.  The
two-harmonic expectation computed in
Proposition~\ref{prop:direct-scaled-slab-density-no-go} is not constant in
$s$.  Fix one $s_\ast\in S$ for which its absolute change is $d_\ast>0$.
Uniform lens convergence and
\eqref{eq:log-shell-bandwidth-density-bound} imply, for all sufficiently
large $R$,
\[
 \frac{C_{S,T,M,L_0,X}(1+D_R)}R\geq\frac{d_\ast}{2}.
\]
This proves the positive lower limit in
\eqref{eq:log-shell-ballistic-degree-necessity}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[An isolated ballistic carrier has a positive fixed-band
cost floor]
\label{prop:log-shell-ballistic-born-cost}
Let $q_R\in\mathcal H_{D_R}$ be real with $\|q_R\|_2=1$, put
$\Lambda_R=D_R(D_R+1)$, and consider in
\eqref{eq:log-shell-rescaled-model} a single control
\[
 W_R(t,\omega)=w_R(t)q_R(\omega).
\]
Let $e_0=(4\pi)^{-1/2}$.  In the interaction picture of the kinetic term, the
first Born coefficient from $e_0$ to $q_R$ at logarithmic time $T$ is
\begin{equation}
 b_R(s)
 =
 -\frac{is}{\sqrt{4\pi}}
 \int_0^T
 w_R(t)
 \exp\!\left(
  \frac{is\Lambda_R}{R}(1-e^{-t})
 \right)\dd t .
 \label{eq:log-shell-ballistic-born-amplitude}
\end{equation}
If, for some $\eta>0$,
\begin{equation}
 \inf_{s\in S}|b_R(s)|\geq\eta,
 \label{eq:log-shell-ballistic-born-transfer}
\end{equation}
then the physical quadratic ledger obeys
\begin{equation}
 \int_R^{Re^T}\|V_R(r)\|_2^2\,\dd r
 \geq
 c_{S,T}\eta^2\frac{\Lambda_R}{R^2}.
 \label{eq:log-shell-ballistic-born-cost-floor}
\end{equation}
Consequently, if $\liminf D_R/R>0$, every isolated Born-level ballistic
transfer that is uniform on the fixed nondegenerate interval $S$ has a
strictly positive physical cost.  It cannot be one cell of an arbitrarily
small-cost radial lens compiler.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The free logarithmic propagator contributes the relative phase
$s\Lambda_R(1-e^{-t})/R$, while
\[
 \langle q_R,M_{q_R}e_0\rangle=\frac1{\sqrt{4\pi}},
\]
which proves~\eqref{eq:log-shell-ballistic-born-amplitude}.  Set
\[
 x=1-e^{-t},\qquad a=1-e^{-T},\qquad
 g_R(x)=\frac{w_R(-\log(1-x))}{1-x}.
\]
With the Fourier convention
\[
 \widehat g_R(\xi)=\int_0^ag_R(x)e^{i\xi x}\,\dd x,
\]
condition~\eqref{eq:log-shell-ballistic-born-transfer} gives
\[
 |\widehat g_R(\xi)|
 \geq\frac{\sqrt{4\pi}\eta}{s_1}
 \quad\hbox{for}\quad
 \xi\in
 \left[
  \frac{s_0\Lambda_R}{R},
  \frac{s_1\Lambda_R}{R}
 \right].
\]
Plancherel therefore yields
\[
 \int_0^a|g_R(x)|^2\,\dd x
 \geq
 \frac{2\eta^2(s_1-s_0)}{s_1^2}
 \frac{\Lambda_R}{R}.
\]
On the other hand, the exact cost identity
\eqref{eq:log-shell-cost} and $\dd x=e^{-t}\dd t$ give
\[
 \int_R^{Re^T}\|V_R(r)\|_2^2\,\dd r
 =
 \frac1R\int_0^a
 |w_R(-\log(1-x))|^2\,\dd x.
\]
Since $w_R(-\log(1-x))=(1-x)g_R(x)$ and
$1-x\geq e^{-T}$, the last two displays prove
\eqref{eq:log-shell-ballistic-born-cost-floor}.
\end{proof}

\begin{problem}[Short-time radialization of the finite-frame compiler]
\label{prob:short-time-radial-compiler}
Strengthen Theorem~\ref{thm:sphere-finite-frame-compiler} as follows.  Given
$\eta>0$, require the approximating positive-flight/signed-mask word to have
\begin{equation}
 \sum_jt_j<\eta
 \label{eq:short-angular-time-target}
\end{equation}
while retaining uniform common-dilation accuracy, full-sphere leakage
control, a finite mask ledger, and a radial realization with arbitrarily
small charged cost and one pointwise height bound.

The double-well gate core can be accelerated by shrinking the wells: its
absolute tunnel splitting and external gap then grow while the relative
window $\gamma\ll\delta\ll G_{\rm ext}$ remains available.  What is not
proved above is an equally short entrance and exit between the fixed low
harmonic frame and the localized well frame.  The adiabatic construction in
Theorems~\ref{thm:sphere-multiwell-permutation} and
\ref{thm:sphere-finite-frame-compiler} may use arbitrarily large total
$A_3$-flight, so Lemma~\ref{lem:scale-compatibility} cannot be cited to
obtain~\eqref{eq:short-angular-time-target}.

Propositions~\ref{prop:twist-laplacian-amplifier},
\ref{prop:gradient-flow-radial-cost-no-go}, and
\ref{prop:phase-wrapping-cost-no-go} below give a sharp audit of one
natural shortcut.  Rotationally averaged twist conjugation really does
amplify $A_3$ by an arbitrarily large factor, but the known small-time
gradient-flow implementation uses $1/\tau$ phase kicks.  Those kicks cannot
simultaneously satisfy the present weak-slab error and arbitrarily small
$X_3$ ledgers when $\tau\lesssim R^{-1}$.  Taking the phase modulo $2\pi$
does make its representative bounded, but it preserves angular oscillation
of order $\tau^{-1}$ and therefore leaves a positive physical cost floor.
Thus abstract small-time controllability alone is not the missing theorem:
the compiler must also be quantitatively cost-efficient under radialization.

Proposition~\ref{prop:direct-scaled-slab} isolates one direct sufficient
formulation which initially survives that audit.  It asks for controls $Q_N$
in~\eqref{eq:semiclassical-angular-gate}, on one fixed $x$ interval, which
approximate the required common-dilation finite-frame gate while satisfying
\begin{equation}
 \sup_N\int_0^T\|Q_N(x)\|_2^2\,\dd x<\infty,
 \qquad
 \sup_N\|Q_N\|_\infty<\infty,
 \qquad
 \sup_{\rm trajectories}\|A_3u\|_2=O(N).
 \label{eq:semiclassical-compiler-target}
\end{equation}
Proposition~\ref{prop:direct-scaled-slab-backscatter} proves that the
counter-propagating coefficient is $o(1)$ on every active block obeying the
same scale and regularity bounds.  Proposition
~\ref{prop:direct-scaled-slab-density-no-go} then decides the formulation
negatively.  Vanishing radial cost and frozen-radius error force every
position density to remain asymptotically fixed, whereas the required inverse
drift changes the density of a two-harmonic superposition.  Thus the
single-scale direct slab is closed as decisively as the standard
fast-gradient implementation.  Proposition
~\ref{prop:bounded-depth-density-no-go} further excludes every bounded-depth
multiscale cascade.  Any positive solution within this concatenated
frozen-radius model must therefore use an unbounded number of genuinely
interacting scales and must violate the reciprocal-radius product criterion
\eqref{eq:multiscale-density-no-go-condition}; no one bounded collection of
energy parameters may control all intermediate trajectories and the radial
freezing estimate.  Proposition~\ref{prop:log-shell-microstructure} isolates
the other exact escape, logarithmic radial scaling.  Although a $1/r$ control
tail on $[R,Re^T]$ costs only $O(R^{-1})$, every uniformly smooth rescaled
control tends to a pointwise phase multiplier.  Hence a successful
logarithmic-shell compiler must also create angular frequencies at least of
order $\sqrt R$.  Proposition~\ref{prop:log-shell-ballistic-degree} sharpens
this for bounded rescaled action: every degree-$o(R)$ controller still
freezes densities.  The remaining logarithmic target is therefore a
degree-$\asymp R$ scalar grating in the exact second-order radial equation,
with simultaneous control of the growing resonant cluster, radial
reflection, and the full fixed coupling interval.  Proposition
~\ref{prop:log-shell-ballistic-born-cost} excludes the perturbative isolated
carrier version by a positive Plancherel cost floor.  The surviving candidate
is a nonperturbative collective gate controlling the entire growing resonant
cluster.

A positive solution makes the reference-energy full-block concentration
and reverse refocusing words uniform on all of $K$.  It then proves the
broadband cell claimed in the preceding draft, solves Problem
~\ref{prob:physical-grouped-global} by global phase placement, and activates
Proposition~\ref{prop:conditional-counterexample-final}.  A lower bound
excluding~\eqref{eq:short-angular-time-target} would close the present lens
implementation structurally.
\end{problem}

\begin{proposition}[What the available small-time control theorems do and do
not supply]
\label{prop:small-time-literature-audit}
The phase-saturation theorem of Chambrion--Pozzoli
\cite{ChambrionPozzoli2023} supplies strong small-time limits for
multiplication by phases on a closed manifold.  On $\mathbb S^2$ its stated
consequences are arbitrary phase multiplication of one initial state and a
particular transfer between the extremal harmonics in one degenerate
eigenspace.  The Beauchard--Pozzoli framework
\cite{BeauchardPozzoli2025} adds small-time phase and weighted-diffeomorphism
operations in its developed torus and Euclidean examples.  Neither cited
result states the following assertion required in
Problem~\ref{prob:short-time-radial-compiler}: one control, independent of the
input vector, which approximates a prescribed unitary on an entire finite
harmonic frame, uniformly for a continuum of common dilations $s\in S$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The basic limit in \cite[Theorem~2]{ChambrionPozzoli2023} is strong operator
convergence for each fixed phase construction.  Hence, after the construction
has been fixed, it is automatically uniform on the unit sphere of a fixed
finite-dimensional input space.  Its limiting operators are nevertheless
phase multipliers.  The saturation theorem
\cite[Theorem~6]{ChambrionPozzoli2023} is formulated as reachability from one
initial state, and the sphere corollary
\cite[Corollary~4]{ChambrionPozzoli2023} only transfers
$Y^j_j$ and $Y^j_{-j}$ up to a sign.

Definition~2.1 of \cite{BeauchardPozzoli2025} calls a unitary $L$
small-time approximately reachable when, for every initial state $\psi_0$,
there is a control which approximates $L\psi_0$; the control is allowed to
depend on $\psi_0$.  Its controlled phase and diffeomorphism operators have
the form
\[
 e^{i\varphi}f,\qquad
 \mathcal L_Pf=|\det DP|^{1/2}(f\circ P).
\]
Their finite compositions are again weighted-composition operators.  This
does not, merely from the cited theorem, produce a common control for a
prescribed collection of orthogonal inputs or the non-spatial
finite-frame unitary used in
Theorem~\ref{thm:sphere-finite-frame-compiler}.  Finally, neither statement
contains the compact-parameter uniformity in $s$ required by
\eqref{eq:finite-frame-ensemble-compiler}.  Thus the literature supplies
important fast generators but does not close the entrance/exit assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[An exact rotational twist amplifier]
\label{prop:twist-laplacian-amplifier}
Let $A_3=-\Delta_{\mathbb S^2}$ and let $X_z$ be the Killing field generating
rotations about the $z$ axis.  Fix $\epsilon\neq0$, put
\[
 F(z)=1+\epsilon z,\qquad
 f=F(z)x,\qquad g=F(z)y,
\]
and define the area-preserving twist
\[
 P_r=\operatorname{Fl}^{\,r}_{[\nabla f,\nabla g]}.
\]
If $\mathcal L_Pu=u\circ P$ and normalized Haar measure on
$\mathrm{SO}(3)$ is denoted by $\dd R$, then, as a quadratic-form identity
on $H^1(\mathbb S^2)$,
\begin{equation}
 \int_{\mathrm{SO}(3)}
 \mathcal L_{R^{-1}P_rR}^{-1}A_3
 \mathcal L_{R^{-1}P_rR}\,\dd R
 =
 \left(1+\frac{16\epsilon^2}{15}r^2\right)A_3.
 \label{eq:twist-laplacian-amplifier}
\end{equation}
Consequently, if the two twist gates with parameters $\pm r$ were available
with negligible positive $A_3$-time and negligible charged cost, finite
rotation quadrature and Lie--Trotter products would compress any prescribed
positive $A_3$-flight by a factor of order $r^{-2}$ on every fixed smooth
safety compactum.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Using $\operatorname{Hess}x=-xg_{\mathbb S^2}$ and the analogous identities
for $y,z$, or computing in spherical coordinates, gives
\begin{equation}
 [\nabla(Fx),\nabla(Fy)]
 =
 \left(F^2+(1-z^2)(F')^2\right)X_z
 =
 (1+\epsilon^2+2\epsilon z)X_z.
 \label{eq:twist-bracket}
\end{equation}
Thus, in coordinates $(\theta,\phi)$,
\[
 P_r(\theta,\phi)
 =
 \bigl(\theta,\phi+r(1+\epsilon^2+2\epsilon\cos\theta)\bigr).
\]
It preserves spherical area.  In the orthonormal frame
$e_\theta=\partial_\theta$,
$e_\phi=(\sin\theta)^{-1}\partial_\phi$, its differential is a shear, and
\[
 |DP_r|_{\rm HS}^2
 =
 2+4\epsilon^2r^2\sin^4\theta.
\]

For any smooth area-preserving diffeomorphism $P$, the rotational average of
$\mathcal L_P^{-1}A_3\mathcal L_P$ is a rotation-invariant, symmetric
second-order differential operator.  It annihilates constants, so it equals
$a(P)A_3$.  The addition theorem for degree-one harmonics, or equivalently
averaging the principal quadratic form, gives
\[
 a(P)=\frac1{8\pi}\int_{\mathbb S^2}|DP|_{\rm HS}^2\,\dd\omega.
\]
Since
\[
 \int_{\mathbb S^2}\sin^4\theta\,\dd\omega
 =
 2\pi\int_{-1}^{1}(1-z^2)^2\,\dd z
 =
 \frac{32\pi}{15},
\]
we obtain~\eqref{eq:twist-laplacian-amplifier}.  Positive finite quadrature
approximates the Haar form uniformly on any prescribed finite smooth safety
compactum, and the form version of the Lie--Trotter theorem gives the final
conditional assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[The standard fast-gradient implementation violates the
radial cost ledger]
\label{prop:gradient-flow-radial-cost-no-go}
Consider the known conjugation construction of a nontrivial gradient-flow
gate in angular kinetic time $\tau$.  It contains phase masks
$\exp(\pm i\varphi/\tau)$ for some fixed nonconstant smooth real
$\varphi$.  Suppose such a mask is realized near radius $R$ by one or more
weak slabs of total frozen-radius length $T$.

If the radial-freezing error requires $T/R\leq\varepsilon$ and the outward
budget requires $\tau R\leq c<1$, then its weak-mask contribution satisfies
\begin{equation}
 \operatorname{Cost}_{X_3}
 \geq
 \frac{\|\varphi\|_{L^2(\mathbb S^2)}^2}{\tau^2T}
 \geq
 \frac{\|\varphi\|_{L^2(\mathbb S^2)}^2}{\varepsilon c^2}\,R.
 \label{eq:gradient-flow-radial-cost-divergence}
\end{equation}
Even if the freezing condition is discarded and only the angular-motion
condition $D^2T/R^2\leq\varepsilon$ is retained, where $D>0$ is any fixed
nonzero angular frequency in $\varphi$, one still has the positive floor
\begin{equation}
 \operatorname{Cost}_{X_3}
 \geq
 \frac{D^2\|\varphi\|_{L^2(\mathbb S^2)}^2}
 {\varepsilon c^2}.
 \label{eq:gradient-flow-radial-cost-floor}
\end{equation}
The same bounds hold after arbitrary finite subdivision of the phase mask
inside the same frozen-radius cell, provided the aggregate length obeys the
displayed scale constraints.
Hence the Chambrion--Pozzoli fast-gradient construction cannot be used to
radialize Proposition~\ref{prop:twist-laplacian-amplifier} with arbitrarily
small charged cost under the present weak-slab scale ledger.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
A weak slab of length $T_j$ carrying integrated mask $w_j$ has quadratic
cost $T_j^{-1}\|w_j\|_2^2$.  If $\sum_jw_j=\varphi/\tau$, Cauchy--Schwarz
gives
\[
 \sum_j\frac{\|w_j\|_2^2}{T_j}
 \geq
 \frac{\|\varphi/\tau\|_2^2}{\sum_jT_j}.
\]
Use $\sum_jT_j\leq\varepsilon R$ and
$\tau\leq c/R$ to obtain
\eqref{eq:gradient-flow-radial-cost-divergence}.  Alternatively,
$D^2\sum_jT_j/R^2\leq\varepsilon$ gives
\eqref{eq:gradient-flow-radial-cost-floor}.  The lower bounds already apply
to one of the two conjugating masks, so cancellations elsewhere in the
formal transport word cannot remove them.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Phase wrapping preserves the radial cost floor]
\label{prop:phase-wrapping-cost-no-go}
Let $\varphi$ be a fixed nonconstant smooth real function on
$\mathbb S^2$, and put
\[
 q_\tau=\exp(i\varphi/\tau),\qquad \tau\downarrow0.
\]
Suppose that smooth real aggregate phase profiles $w_\tau$ produce
$m_\tau=e^{iw_\tau}$ with
\begin{equation}
 \|m_\tau-q_\tau\|_{L^2(\mathbb S^2)}\longrightarrow0.
 \label{eq:wrapped-phase-approximation}
\end{equation}
There are constants $a_\varphi,b_\varphi>0$ such that
\begin{equation}
 \liminf_{\tau\downarrow0}\|w_\tau\|_2\geq a_\varphi,
 \qquad
 \liminf_{\tau\downarrow0}
 \tau\|\nabla m_\tau\|_2\geq b_\varphi.
 \label{eq:wrapped-phase-invariants}
\end{equation}
Define the intrinsic angular frequency
\[
 D_\tau^2=
 \frac{\|\nabla m_\tau\|_2^2}{\|m_\tau\|_2^2}.
\]
If one or more weak slabs of aggregate frozen-radius length $T_\tau$ realize
this phase at radius $R_\tau$, and the angular-motion ledger requires
\begin{equation}
 \frac{D_\tau^2T_\tau}{R_\tau^2}\leq\varepsilon,
 \qquad
 \tau R_\tau\leq c,
 \label{eq:wrapped-phase-radial-ledger}
\end{equation}
then their quadratic charged cost has a strictly positive asymptotic floor:
\begin{equation}
 \liminf_{\tau\downarrow0}\operatorname{Cost}_{X_3}
 \geq
 \frac{a_\varphi^2b_\varphi^2}
 {4\pi\varepsilon c^2}>0.
 \label{eq:wrapped-phase-cost-floor}
\end{equation}
Thus replacing $\varphi/\tau$ by a bounded principal-argument
representative does not make the known fast-gradient gate arbitrarily cheap.
The proposition does not exclude a different diffractive finite-frame gate
which does not approximate $q_\tau$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Choose a coordinate patch $U$ on which $\dd\varphi\neq0$.  By the coarea
formula, the values of $\varphi/\tau$ become equidistributed modulo $2\pi$
on a smaller regular patch.  In particular,
\[
 \liminf_{\tau\downarrow0}\|q_\tau-1\|_{L^2(U)}>0.
\]
Since $|e^{it}-1|\leq|t|$, the triangle inequality and
\eqref{eq:wrapped-phase-approximation} give
\[
 \|w_\tau\|_2
 \geq\|m_\tau-1\|_2
 \geq\|q_\tau-1\|_{L^2(U)}-o(1),
\]
which proves the first assertion in~\eqref{eq:wrapped-phase-invariants}.

For the derivative bound, choose a smooth vector field $X$ on $U$ with
$X\varphi=1$, and a smaller patch $U_0\Subset U$.  If $\Phi_h$ is its local
flow and $h=\pi\tau$, then
\[
 q_\tau\circ\Phi_h=-q_\tau
\]
on $U_0$ for all sufficiently small $\tau$.  Pullback by $\Phi_h$ is
uniformly bounded on $L^2$, so
\eqref{eq:wrapped-phase-approximation} implies
\[
 \|m_\tau\circ\Phi_h-m_\tau\|_{L^2(U_0)}
 \geq 2|U_0|^{1/2}-o(1).
\]
The fundamental theorem of calculus along the flow gives the reverse bound
\[
 \|m_\tau\circ\Phi_h-m_\tau\|_{L^2(U_0)}
 \leq C_Uh\|\nabla m_\tau\|_{L^2(U)}.
\]
This proves the second assertion in
\eqref{eq:wrapped-phase-invariants}.

Because $|m_\tau|=1$, one has $\|m_\tau\|_2^2=4\pi$, and hence
$D_\tau^2\geq b_\varphi^2/(4\pi\tau^2)+o(\tau^{-2})$.
The first condition in~\eqref{eq:wrapped-phase-radial-ledger} gives
\[
 T_\tau
 \leq
 \frac{4\pi\varepsilon R_\tau^2\tau^2}
 {b_\varphi^2+o(1)}.
\]
If the aggregate phase is subdivided as
$w_\tau=\sum_jw_{\tau,j}$ over lengths $T_{\tau,j}$, Cauchy--Schwarz yields
\[
 \operatorname{Cost}_{X_3}
 =
 \sum_j\frac{\|w_{\tau,j}\|_2^2}{T_{\tau,j}}
 \geq
 \frac{\|w_\tau\|_2^2}{T_\tau}.
\]
Now use~\eqref{eq:wrapped-phase-invariants} and
$\tau R_\tau\leq c$ to obtain
\eqref{eq:wrapped-phase-cost-floor}.
\end{proof}

\iffalse

\begin{proposition}[One-window compact-output reciprocal cell]
\label{prop:one-window-cell}
Let $\mathfrak C$ be a compact subset of
$L^2(\mathbb S^2)$, invariant under conjugation, and let
$k_\star\in K\Subset(0,\infty)$.  Fix $\rho>0$, a Riesz depth $m$, an inner
radius, and $\varepsilon>0$.  Outside that radius there is a smooth bounded
real reciprocal shell cell with transmission and reflection coefficients
$A(k),B(k)$ and a finite smooth safety space $\mathcal E$ such that
$\mathfrak C$ lies within $\varepsilon$ of $\mathcal E$ and
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{y\in\mathfrak C}
 \bigl(\|(A(k)-a\Id)y\|+\|(B(k)-b\Id)y\|\bigr)
 \leq C_{K,\mathfrak C,\rho}m|k/k_\star-1|
       +\varepsilon+Ce^{-cm}
 \label{eq:one-window-residual}
\end{equation}
on a neighbourhood of $k_\star$.  On the safety block its reflection
contraction satisfies
\begin{equation}
 \|B(k)A(k)^{-1}\|
 \leq\tanh(\rho+C_0)+\varepsilon.
 \label{eq:one-window-reflection-gap}
\end{equation}
The cell has $X_3$ cost at most $C_\rho e^{-cm}+\varepsilon$ and its
pointwise amplitude is bounded by the fixed constant in
Lemma~\ref{lem:bounded-reflector}, independently of $m$ and of its radial
location.  A preceding free collar multiplies $B$ by a scalar phase
$z=e^{i\vartheta(k)}$, up to an additional error $\varepsilon$ on
$\mathcal E$, while $\inf_K|\vartheta'(k)|$ can be made arbitrarily large.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Approximate the compact set $\mathfrak C$ by a bounded subset of a finite
dimensional smooth conjugation-invariant space $\mathcal E_0$.  Apply
Proposition~\ref{prop:full-block-talbot} successively to the $m$
superlacunary factors.  The resulting reflectionless forward train sends
every vector in $\mathcal E_0$ into the set on which a smooth cutoff
$\chi_m$ equals one, up to norm $O(\varepsilon+e^{-cm})$, while
$\|\chi_m\|_2^2\leq Ce^{-cm}$.  The reciprocal reverse train refocuses the
same finite family.

At the focus use Lemma~\ref{lem:bounded-reflector}, localized by
$\chi_m$.  Ignoring angular motion during this finite reflector gives
pointwise coefficients $A_0(\omega)=a$, $B_0(\omega)=b$ on the plateau
$\{\chi_m=1\}$ and the identity coefficients on $\{\chi_m=0\}$, with a
smooth transition whose Cartan parameter stays bounded by $\rho+C_0$.
The forward concentration and reverse refocusing therefore give
$A(k_\star)=a\Id+o(1)$ and $B(k_\star)=b\Id+o(1)$ on
$\mathcal E_0$.  The Cartan parameter in every transition fibre is bounded
by $\rho+C_0$, which proves~\eqref{eq:one-window-reflection-gap} before the
small radial-realization error.

Realize each angular mask by a long weak slab.  Variation of constants gives
transmission $\exp(-iw/(2k))$ with error
$O(T^{-1}+D^2T/R^2+T/R)$ and reflection of the same order.  Free collars give
the required spherical Talbot flights with polar error
$O(\sqrt{\log\Lambda}/\Lambda)$.  Angular motion during the reflector costs
$O(D^2\ell_\rho/R^2)$.  Lemma~\ref{lem:scale-compatibility} makes their sum
smaller than $\varepsilon$.  Differentiating the finite product in
$k/k_\star$ gives the chromatic term in
\eqref{eq:one-window-residual}.

Weak slabs have arbitrarily small charged cost, and the reflector is supported
where $\chi_m$ is nonzero, giving $C_\rho e^{-cm}$.  Finally, a free collar
of arbitrarily large radius changes the round-trip reflection phase by
$2kd$ while its angular action on the already fixed safety space is
$O(D^2/R)$.  Reapply the scale order after selecting the collar.  This proves
all assertions.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Reduction to a finite-frame converter]
\label{prop:local-adapted-squeeze}
Let $W$ be a finite smooth real partial potential, let $x(k_\star)$ be a
nonzero inverse Jost column, let $H(k_\star)$ be its reciprocal phase, and
normalize $u=x(k_\star)/\|x(k_\star)\|$.
Let $q_1,q_2$ be a $Q$-real orthonormal basis of
$\operatorname{span}_{\C}\{u,H(k_\star)\bar u\}$, after zero directions are
omitted.  Suppose a reflectionless physical converter $F$ sends $q_1,q_2$ to
ordinary-real orthonormal smooth functions supported on the reflector
plateau, up to error $\varepsilon$.  Then the reciprocal
converter--reflector--inverse-converter cell realizes, up to
$O(\varepsilon)$, the projection version of
Lemma~\ref{lem:two-real-direction-squeeze} at $k_\star$, with
\[
 P=P_{\operatorname{span}_{\C}\{u,H(k_\star)\bar u\}},\qquad
 A_0=\Id+(\cosh\rho-1)P,\qquad
 B_0=(\sinh\rho)H(k_\star)^*P.
\]
The target phase average then obeys
\begin{equation}
 \int_\T\|(A_0+zH(k_\star)B_0)^{-1}u\|^{2\beta}\dd m(z)
 =g_{\beta,\rho}(1)<1,
 \label{eq:local-adapted-gap}
\end{equation}
and all the residual, inverse, bounded-amplitude, and $X_3$ estimates of
Proposition~\ref{prop:one-window-cell} remain valid.  The active reciprocal
rank is at most two; the smooth safety enlargement has rank at most four
before ordinary Galerkin buffering.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Extend $F$ to a unitary and let $P_0$ project onto the ordinary-real output
frame.  In the finite-dimensional Cartan calculation, a scalar boost on
$P_0$ between $F$ and $F^*$ has
\[
 A_0=F^*\bigl(\Id+(\cosh\rho-1)P_0\bigr)F,
 \qquad
 B_0=F^T(\sinh\rho)P_0F.
\]
Since $Fq_i$ is real and $H(k_\star)\bar q_i=q_i$, one has
$F^TFq_i=\bar q_i=H(k_\star)^*q_i$.  Hence these coefficients reduce to the
claimed $A_0,B_0$ on the target block.  Thus
\[
 A_0+zH(k_\star)B_0=\Id+(\cosh\rho+z\sinh\rho-1)P,
\]
which proves~\eqref{eq:local-adapted-gap}.  The proof of Proposition
~\ref{prop:one-window-cell} uses only finiteness of the controlled smooth
block, the bound on the Cartan height, and reciprocal refocusing.  Those
quantities are unchanged, so its error and cost ledger applies verbatim.
What is not supplied by Proposition~\ref{prop:full-block-talbot} alone is the
 prescribed real orientation $Fq_i\in\operatorname{Fix}(\mathcal C)$.  The
 grouped scalar construction below does not require this stronger converter.
\end{proof}

% Legacy alternative route remains hidden inside the existing conditional.
\section{The one remaining physical theorem}

Theorem~\ref{thm:scalar-dovetail} and
Lemma~\ref{lem:two-real-direction-squeeze} show that neither a growing matrix
contraction nor singularity of a partial reciprocal phase is needed.  What
remains is no longer an unspecified \emph{no escape} statement.

For an inner Jost matrix $J$ and the transmission/reflection coefficients
$\mathsf T,\mathsf R$ of one outer shell, exact radial composition gives
\begin{equation}
 J^+=J\mathsf T+\overline J\,\mathsf R
 =J(\mathsf T+H\mathsf R),
 \qquad H=J^{-1}\overline J\in\U(\mathcal H).
 \label{eq:exact-jost-shell-update}
\end{equation}
With $X=J^{-1}$, a scalar probe uses only the column $x=Xp$.  Normalize
$u(k)=x(k)/\|x(k)\|$ and put
\begin{equation}
 Q(k)=H(k)\mathcal C,\qquad
 S(k)=|u(k)\rangle\langle u(k)|
      +|Q(k)u(k)\rangle\langle Q(k)u(k)|.
 \label{eq:moving-two-real-family}
\end{equation}
The ideal coefficients
\begin{equation}
 A_\star(k)=\cosh(\rho S(k)),\qquad
 B_\star(k)=H(k)^*\sinh(\rho S(k))
 \label{eq:broadband-target-pair}
\end{equation}
are norm-continuous on $K$, reciprocal, and differ from $(\Id,0)$ by rank at
most two.  Lemma~\ref{lem:two-real-direction-squeeze} gives one fixed strict
phase-averaged contraction for the target at every $k\in K$.

\begin{problem}[Broadband moving-rank-two reciprocal synthesis]
\label{prob:physical}
Let $W$ be a real smooth compactly supported partial potential, let
$K\Subset(0,\infty)$, and let $x(k)=J_W(k)^{-1}p$ be the nonzero radiation
vector of one compact probe.  Fix $\rho>0$ and define the continuous
rank-two family~\eqref{eq:broadband-target-pair}.  Given $\eta>0$, a finite
list of other probe densities and locked continuous tests, and a prescribed
inner radius, construct a smooth real reciprocal shell generation outside
that radius such that:
\begin{enumerate}
 \item the generation has $X_3$ cost at most $\eta$ and its pointwise
 amplitude is bounded by one fixed finite constant independent of the
 generation;
 \item after a free collar supplies a deterministically placed phase
 $z(k)\in\T$, its exact transmission/reflection pair
 $(\mathsf T(k),\mathsf R(k))$ approximates
 $(A_\star(k),z(k)B_\star(k))$ on the compact ideal-output bundle, uniformly
 for $k\in K$, strongly enough that the exact updated target density $w^+$
 satisfies
 \begin{equation}
  \int_K(w^+)^\beta\dd k
  \leq q\int_Kw^\beta\dd k+\eta,
  \qquad q<1
  \label{eq:physical-target-contraction}
 \end{equation}
 with $q$ depending only on $(\beta,\rho)$;
 \item every protected fractional moment is nonexpansive up to $\eta$, and
 every protected spectral test integral changes by at most $\eta$;
 \item the passage from the Talbot approximation to
 \eqref{eq:physical-target-contraction} is proved by the residual estimate
 \eqref{eq:residual-control} on the compact ideal-output bundle, followed by
 an outgoing Schur elimination of the evanescent sector.  In particular, if
 $D$ is the largest angular frequency used by the cell, its radial scale
 $R$, active cutoff $J$, and Galerkin cutoff $L$ must be chosen in the order
 \begin{equation}
  D\ll R\ll J\ll L,
  \qquad
  2M_{W+v,K}^2\frac{\|W+v\|_\infty^2}
  {(J+1)(J+2)/R^2-\|W+v\|_\infty-k_1^2}<\eta,
  \label{eq:physical-buffer-target}
 \end{equation}
 together with the Neumann smallness condition in
 Proposition~\ref{prop:explicit-buffer-stability}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{problem}

The local transfer-matrix reduction at one energy is Proposition
~\ref{prop:local-adapted-squeeze}.  The weak mask slabs and localized radial
reflector bumps may be chosen smooth; the scale, inverse, and outgoing-buffer
ledgers are already compatible.  What is not yet proved is that the
reflectionless Talbot controls realize the prescribed finite-frame converter,
uniformly in energy.  Lemma~\ref{lem:physical-hardy} closes global
nonexpansion and protected tests, while deterministic phase placement converts
\eqref{eq:two-real-contraction} into the desired integrated strict gap.
Consequently the only unproved quantifier in Problem~\ref{prob:physical} is
\emph{uniform broadband synthesis} of the norm-continuous finite-rank family
\eqref{eq:broadband-target-pair}.  Equivalently, a local construction would
suffice if its adaptive window widths had nonsummable total hazard.  The
root-of-unity obstruction does not apply to this moving rank-two family.

\begin{remark}[Why existing ensemble-control theorems do not yet close the gap]
There is a close abstract analogue.  The semisimple Lie-group ensemble theorem
of Li and Zhang~\cite{LiZhang2020} uniformly approximates continuous
group-valued targets for driftless systems
$\dot U=\sum_j\beta_j u_j(t)B_jU$ once the corresponding pointwise systems are
controllable.  Chambrion's partial ensemble theorem
~\cite{Chambrion2013} treats a bilinear Schr\"odinger family on a selected
two-level block, but its conclusion controls transition moduli rather than a
prescribed phased unitary frame.  Multi-plane light conversion supplies a
powerful practical phase-mask/free-flight architecture
~\cite{ZhangFontaine2023}, not a uniform reciprocal theorem with the present
$X_3$ and outgoing-buffer estimates.

The physical far-field converter here has the unavoidable one-sided kinetic
drift $A_3/r^2$, while its scalar multiplication controls commute when that
drift is deleted.  A finite Galerkin Lie-rank calculation therefore cannot
simply be inserted: one must prove uniform-in-$k$ approximation while retaining
the true kinetic drift, controlling leakage before taking the Galerkin limit,
and then realize the controls on a radial interval with summable reflection
and $X_3$ cost.  None of the cited theorems contains that combined statement.
This identifies a plausible control mechanism, but not a completed physical
lemma.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Conditional completion of the counterexample]
\label{prop:conditional-counterexample}
If Problem~\ref{prob:physical} holds for every finite partial potential and
compact probe, then there is a bounded real $V\in X_3$ and a nonempty compact
$K\Subset(0,\infty)$ such that
\[
 P_{\rm ac}(-\Delta+V)\mathbf1_K(-\Delta+V)=0.
\]
Consequently Simon's multidimensional $L^2$ conjecture is false.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Choose a countable dense set of compact probes and use the triangular schedule
of Theorem~\ref{thm:scalar-dovetail}.  At stage $j$, apply
Problem~\ref{prob:physical} successively a fixed finite number of times until
the product of its uniform factors is at most $1/4$.  Include the first $j$
probes and all previously declared witnesses in every preservation list, and
allocate the combined error and $X_3$ cost at most $2^{-j}$.  The accumulated
target estimate is then at most one half after its error is included.  Put the
generations on disjoint radial shells.  Their sum is bounded, real, and belongs
to $X_3$.

Theorem~\ref{thm:scalar-dovetail} and
Corollary~\ref{cor:dense-probes} give vanishing of the exterior absolutely
continuous projection on $K$.  Strong resolvent convergence identifies the
weakly locked limits with the spectral measures of the summed potential.
Changing the operator inside a fixed ball and recoupling the exterior
Dirichlet boundary are compactly supported scattering perturbations, so the
standard completeness of the local wave operators transfers the conclusion
to the whole-space operator.
\end{proof}

\fi

\section{Conditional completion and the single remaining theorem}

For an inner Jost matrix $J$ and the transmission/reflection coefficients
$\mathsf T,\mathsf R$ of one outer shell, exact radial composition is
\begin{equation}
 J^+=J\mathsf T+\overline J\,\mathsf R
 =J(\mathsf T+H\mathsf R),
 \qquad H=J^{-1}\overline J\in\U(\mathcal H).
 \label{eq:exact-jost-shell-update-final}
\end{equation}
Problem~\ref{prob:physical-grouped-global} is the single missing strict global
contraction required by Theorem~\ref{thm:scalar-dovetail}.  If it is proved,
every finite physical generation is closed by the scale order
\begin{equation}
 D\ll R\ll J\ll L,
 \qquad
 2M_{W+v,K}^2\frac{\|W+v\|_\infty^2}
 {(J+1)(J+2)/R^2-\|W+v\|_\infty-k_1^2}<\eta,
 \label{eq:physical-buffer-target}
\end{equation}
together with the Neumann smallness condition in Proposition
~\ref{prop:explicit-buffer-stability}.

\begin{proposition}[Conditional completion of the counterexample]
\label{prop:conditional-counterexample-final}
If Problem~\ref{prob:physical-grouped-global} holds at every finite stage,
then there is a bounded real $V\in X_3$ and a nonempty compact
$K\Subset(0,\infty)$ such that
\begin{equation}
 P_{\rm ac}(-\Delta+V)\mathbf1_K(-\Delta+V)=0.
 \label{eq:final-counterexample}
\end{equation}
Under the same hypothesis, Simon's multidimensional $L^2$ conjecture is false.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Choose a countable dense set of smooth compact probes.  At stage $j$, apply
the asserted global physical grouped Lemma B to the first $j$ probes and
include every previously declared witness and the first $j$ continuous tests
in its preservation list.  Allocate the combined phase-placement, residual,
outgoing-buffer, and $X_3$ error at most $2^{-2j}$.  The stage contracts each
of the first $j$ fractional moments by $3/4+o(1)$ and preserves all declared
spectral tests.  Repeating the finite grouped generation three
times gives $(3/4)^3<1/2$ and hence the factor in
\eqref{eq:dovetail-contract}, after the allocated error is included.  Put all
cells on successive disjoint radial shells.  Their amplitudes have one
uniform bound and
\begin{equation}
 \|V\|_{X_3}^2\leq\sum_{j\geq1}2^{-2j}<\infty.
 \label{eq:counterexample-X3-cost}
\end{equation}

Theorem~\ref{thm:scalar-dovetail} and Corollary~\ref{cor:dense-probes} give
vanishing of the exterior absolutely continuous projection on $K$.  Strong
resolvent convergence identifies the weakly locked limits with the spectral
measures of the summed potential.  Changing the operator inside a fixed ball
and recoupling the exterior Dirichlet boundary are compactly supported
scattering perturbations, so completeness of the local wave operators
transfers the conclusion to the whole-space operator.
\end{proof}

\section{Conclusions and the remaining radialization problem}

The supervisor's proposed one-cell full-block stopping test has a definite
answer: Proposition~\ref{prop:no-dim-free} disproves it.  Theorem
~\ref{thm:singular-bundle-grouped-contraction} supplies the correct repair:
a finite product of fixed-height scalar cells is one random effective Poisson
barrier, and it contracts every compact bundle of singular reciprocal spectral
measures by a prescribed factor:
\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{gathered}
 \text{one-cell dimension-free Lemma B is false;}\\
  \text{fixed-height grouped singular-bundle Lemma B is true;}\\
  \text{short-time radialization of its lens compiler remains open.}
 \end{gathered}}
\]
The order of quantifiers is decisive.  First select the finite group length
from the infinite-dimensional compact singular bundle.  Only afterwards take
one finite smooth safety enlargement for the already fixed recursion.  The
root-Zeno and M\"obius-orbit examples show why the former local-window
argument could not close the global step.  Theorem
~\ref{thm:sphere-finite-frame-compiler} supplies the required abstract
fixed-band angular gate, but Proposition
~\ref{prop:outward-angular-time-budget} prevents its present adiabatic
realization from being inserted automatically on remote radial shells.
Problem~\ref{prob:short-time-radial-compiler} is therefore the only remaining
physical input to Problem~\ref{prob:physical-grouped-global} and Proposition
~\ref{prop:conditional-counterexample-final}.

 Proposition~\ref{prop:positive-flight-no-recurrence} rules out the shortcut
 of obtaining a broadband inverse from free-flight recurrence.
 Propositions~\ref{prop:fejer-su2-inverse},
 \ref{prop:broadband-transposition-criterion}, and
 \ref{prop:population-swap-phase-cancellation} instead reduce the local
 problem to high-gap drift-decorated population swaps.  Positive echo times
 absorb the terminal drift, and the internal two-level phase cancels.
 The adiabatic and direct single-profile constructions fail for the reasons
 proved in Propositions~\ref{prop:adiabatic-phase-orientation-no-go} and
 \ref{prop:single-carrier-high-mode-no-go}; adjacent-shell carriers fail on a
 fixed band because of the growing resonant cluster in Proposition
 ~\ref{prop:adjacent-shell-growing-resonant-cluster}.

 The new step is to combine a saturated nonadjacent transition with a
 shrinking window.  Proposition~\ref{prop:power-two-saturated-gap} proves
 arithmetically that
 \[
  L=\ell+2^k,\qquad J=\ell+L
 \]
 gives exactly one coupled positive gap
 $N=\lambda_L-\lambda_\ell$.  Proposition
 ~\ref{prop:saturated-carrier-full-su} proves that both carrier quadratures of
 the degree-$J$ profiles generate
 $\mathfrak{su}(\mathcal H_\ell\oplus\mathcal H_L)$.  Theorem
 ~\ref{thm:shrinking-window-carrier-lift} then realizes any gate in that group
 on
 \[
  S_\lambda=[c-\lambda R/N,c+\lambda R/N]
 \]
 with full-sphere operator-norm leakage $o(1)$, peak control $O(\lambda)$,
 fixed $L^1$ budget, and quadratic control cost $O(\lambda)$.  Corollary
 ~\ref{cor:local-high-gap-physical-swaps} therefore proves the exact
 drift-decorated swaps needed by the echo on a nonempty window about every
 centre.  This is the previously missing nonlinear local lift.  Proposition
 ~\ref{prop:causal-filter-cost} remains correct but no longer blocks the local
 route: it excludes delayed first-order filters, whereas the carrier theorem
 compresses the complete Lie word before taking the window scale to zero.

 Proposition~\ref{prop:dipole-direct-sum-carrier-algebra} and Corollary
 ~\ref{cor:ideal-dipole-multiline-profiles} remove the last fixed-band
 algebraic ambiguity.  Degree-one carrier quadratures generate
 $\mathfrak{su}(4a+4)$ on every adjacent pair and separate any finite family
 of resonant lines, so arbitrary line-by-line profiles are reachable on each
 fixed detuning compactum.  Proposition
 ~\ref{prop:dipole-second-order-parity} removes one possible quantitative
 obstruction: the resonant $O(\lambda)$ normal-form term preserves every
 spherical shell, while the first nonadjacent resonance is the
 three-photon $O(\lambda^2)$ path.  Proposition
 ~\ref{prop:dipole-first-normal-form} identifies the complete first defect,
 and Proposition~\ref{prop:dipole-first-defect-correctable} cancels it to
 $o(\lambda)$ on every fixed safety space and detuning compactum.
 Proposition~\ref{prop:dipole-unavoidable-one-over-nu-tail} then decides the
 naive global patch negatively: every nonzero dipole pulse has a sharp
 $1/\nu$ Stark tail, so the individual operator-norm tails cannot be summed
 over the carrier lattice.  Proposition
 ~\ref{prop:matrix-zs-dipole-mismatch} rules out importing that word from
 unconstrained matrix Zakharov--Shabat inverse scattering: its first-Born
 map has matrix directions absent from every physical higher dipole line,
 and independent line potentials violate the common-control constraint.
 Proposition~\ref{prop:chebyshev-stark-cancellation} nevertheless removes the
 harmonic-tail obstruction for any elementary family with a common Stark
 residue: after only a growing local Chebyshev cluster is retained, the whole
 remote product is uniformly
 $O(M^{-1}+M/N)$.  Corollary
 ~\ref{cor:chebyshev-tail-criterion} isolates the corresponding conditional
 analytic criterion:
 Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-stark-gramian-equalization} equalizes every fixed
 finite collection by locally invisible symmetric sidebands, while Corollary
 ~\ref{cor:chebyshev-triangular-array} identifies the sufficient growth target
 $K_N=o(\sqrt N)$ together with vanishing joint synthesis error on the
 $O(\sqrt N)$ local cluster.  Proposition
 ~\ref{prop:matrix-zs-energy-floor} and Corollary
 ~\ref{cor:dipole-multiband-energy-no-go} now show that the selected
 adjacent-block implementation cannot meet that target with arbitrarily
 prescribed $X_3$ cost: the determinant trace identity imposes a positive
 bandwidth--energy floor independent of the passband partition.  Thus the
 Chebyshev construction repairs the remote operator-norm tail but does not
 rescue the resonant multiband carrier route; that route is closed
 structurally, not merely missing an estimate.

 The lens audit identifies the full-sphere mechanism outside the selected
 carrier model.  Proposition
 ~\ref{prop:lens-positive-speed} proves that projective positivity is
 compatible with inverse drift, while Proposition
 ~\ref{prop:scalar-ground-conical-no-go} rules out the direct
 conically-connected shortcut at the ground harmonic.  Propositions
 ~\ref{prop:sphere-multiwell-spectral-limit} and
 \ref{prop:sphere-fast-floor-sweep}, Theorem
 ~\ref{thm:sphere-multiwell-permutation}, and Proposition
 ~\ref{prop:signed-permutation-doubled-echo} prove the required uniform,
 spectator-refocused, full-sphere population permutations and remove their
 dynamical and real-holonomy phases.  Theorem
 ~\ref{thm:wall-swap-closes-lens} consequently proves the prescribed-band
 inverse drift.  Proposition~\ref{prop:double-well-broadband-su2} adds a
 natural tunnel generator and a signed floor generator in the compatible
 scale window $\gamma\ll\delta\ll G_{\rm ext}$; this avoids the invalid
 shortcut of normalizing a vanishing off-diagonal multiplication overlap.
 Theorem~\ref{thm:sphere-finite-frame-compiler} gives the resulting
 full-sphere ensemble word.  Proposition
 ~\ref{prop:outward-angular-time-budget} then supplies the final stopping
 test: the word must be compressed to total positive $A_3$-time $o(R^{-1})$
 before it can enter the remote-shell Riesz--Talbot ledger.  This precise
 missing assertion is Problem~\ref{prob:short-time-radial-compiler}; until it
 is proved, the global contraction and counterexample are not claimed.
 Proposition~\ref{prop:direct-scaled-slab} shows that the outward budget is
 not by itself a no-go.  In the compatible window
 $\sqrt N\ll R_N\ll N$, a continuous slab realizes
 $A_3/N+M_{Q_N}$ with angular time $O(N^{-1})$, forward error
 $O(R_N/N)$, and charged cost $O(N/R_N^2)$; Proposition
 ~\ref{prop:direct-scaled-slab-backscatter} also makes its
 counter-propagating block $o(1)$.  Proposition
 ~\ref{prop:direct-scaled-slab-density-no-go} supplies the decisive stopping
 test: the same compatibility conditions freeze all multiplication
 observables, while the inverse drift changes one explicitly displayed
 two-harmonic density.  Hence the single-scale direct slab is false as a
 lens compiler.  Proposition~\ref{prop:bounded-depth-density-no-go}
 telescopes that commutator estimate and excludes every bounded-depth
 multiscale cascade as well.  A survivor within the frozen-radius slab model
 must have unbounded depth and keep the product of its total frozen-radius
 error with its total reciprocal-radius budget away from zero.  Proposition
 ~\ref{prop:log-shell-microstructure} treats the exact logarithmic scaling
 $V(r)=r^{-1}W(\log(r/R))$: its cost is $O(R^{-1})$, but a uniformly smooth
 $W$ converges to a phase multiplier.  Thus this escape requires angular
 frequency at least $\sqrt R$.  Proposition
 ~\ref{prop:log-shell-ballistic-degree} supplies the density-level stopping
 test: under bounded rescaled action, every degree-$o(R)$ grating still
 freezes multiplication observables.  A logarithmic-shell survivor must
 therefore reach degree comparable to $R$, where exact radial dispersion and
 the whole growing resonant cluster must be retained.  Proposition
 ~\ref{prop:log-shell-ballistic-born-cost} then excludes an isolated
 Born-level carrier on the fixed coupling interval by a positive physical
 cost floor.
 Proposition~\ref{prop:twist-laplacian-amplifier} shows that rotation-averaged
 area-preserving twists would give an exact abstract kinetic amplifier.
 Proposition~\ref{prop:gradient-flow-radial-cost-no-go} supplies its physical
 stopping test: the standard fast-gradient realization requires
 $1/\tau$ phase kicks, whose weak-slab quadratic cost has a positive floor
 (and under the frozen-radius ledger actually diverges) once
 $\tau\lesssim R^{-1}$.  Proposition
 ~\ref{prop:phase-wrapping-cost-no-go} closes the immediate modulo-$2\pi$
 escape: a bounded wrapped representative still has nonvanishing $L^2$
 size, while the multiplier itself has intrinsic angular frequency
 $D_\tau\gtrsim\tau^{-1}$.  The angular-motion ledger therefore restores a
 positive charged-cost floor.  Thus the available small-time
 phase/diffeomorphism theorem does not repair the radial gap.  Any successful
 compiler must use either an unbounded-depth frozen-radius cascade that
 violates the product criterion or a non-paraxial logarithmic-scale
 full-sphere excursion of angular degree comparable to $R$; neither the
 standard fast-gradient construction, a single scaled slab, any
 bounded-depth cascade, nor any bounded-action degree-$o(R)$ logarithmic
 shell can serve as that solution.  In the logarithmic case the remaining
 candidate is a nonperturbative collective gate controlling the entire
 growing resonant cluster; an isolated Born-level ballistic carrier is also
 excluded.
